Madhyama Carita · Chapter 4 of 13 · 42 mantras (4.1–4.42)

शक्रादिस्तुतिः

śakrādistutiḥ

The Praise by Indra and the Gods · Complete with Noted Variants

Introduction

Summary
Medhas resumes (4.1). With Mahiṣa and his army slain, Indra and the gods, bowed and thrilling with joy, praise the Devī (4.2). Their hymn (4.3–23) bows to Ambikā, whose form is the gathered powers of all the gods but who pervades the world by her own power (4.3), whose majesty not even Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva can tell (4.4); she is fortune in the homes of the good and misfortune in the homes of the wicked, understanding, faith and modesty (4.5). The gods cannot describe her form, valour or deeds (4.6), yet name her: the cause of the worlds and the primal Prakṛti (4.7), Svāhā and Svadhā (4.8), the knowledge that liberates (4.9), the Veda whose self is sound, and Vārttā, livelihood (4.10), Medhā, Durgā, Śrī and Gaurī (4.11). They recall the battle: that Mahiṣa could look on her gentle, moon-like face and still strike, and that he did not die at the sight of it in anger (4.12–13), and they pray for her grace, having seen what her anger does (4.14). Her favour brings honour, wealth and a household at peace (4.15), heaven to the doer of good (4.16), and freedom from fear to whoever remembers her (4.17). Why she kills with weapons and not with a glance: so that even her enemies, purified, reach heaven (4.18–19); their eyes were spared because they gazed at her face (4.20); compassion and relentlessness are found together in her alone (4.21–22); the worlds are saved, the enemies sent to heaven, the gods’ fear gone (4.23). In four anuṣṭubh verses they ask her protection with trident, sword, bell and bow, in every quarter, through her gentle and terrible forms and all her weapons (4.24–27). Medhas tells how the gods worshipped her with flowers of Nandana, perfumes and incense (4.28–30). The Devī offers a boon (4.31–32); the gods answer that nothing remains to be done now that Mahiṣa is dead, but ask that, whenever remembered, she destroy their calamities, and that she increase the wealth and household of any mortal who praises her with these hymns (4.33–37). Saying ‘So be it’, Bhadrakālī vanishes (4.38–39). Medhas closes the story of her rising from the gods’ bodies and announces the next: how she arose again from Gaurī’s body to slay Śumbha and Niśumbha (4.40–42).
Forms of the Devī
Ambikā, ‘the Mother’, the name the gods use most (4.3, 4.24, 4.27, 4.37); Caṇḍikā (4.4, 4.25); Bhagavatī (4.9, 4.10, 4.34) and the sovereign titles Īśvarī (4.25) and Maheśvarī (4.35). In the hymn she is named as qualities and powers: Śrī and Alakṣmī, Buddhi, Śraddhā and Lajjā (4.5); the primal, unmanifest Prakṛti with the three guṇas (4.7); Svāhā and Svadhā (4.8); the supreme Vidyā, cause of liberation (4.9); Trayī, the Veda, whose self is sound (शब्दात्मिका), and Vārttā (4.10); Medhā, Durgā, Śrī as Viṣṇu’s consort and Gaurī as Śiva’s (4.11). The gods address her as Durgā (4.17) and as giver of boons (वरदे, 4.22), liken her angry face to Death (Antaka) enraged (4.13), and ask the protection of her gentle and her terrible forms alike (4.26). In the narrative she is ‘the sustainer of the worlds’ (जगतां धात्री, 4.29), gracious of face (प्रसादसुमुखी, 4.30), and, as she vanishes, Bhadrakālī (4.39).
Significance of the events
The chapter turns the victory of chapter 3 into praise, and the praise into a promise. Its hymn is the first in the text addressed to the Goddess as warrior — Brahmā’s hymn in chapter 1 was to Yoganidrā within the sleeping Viṣṇu — and it says what the battle meant: the gods did not lend her their power (4.3), and her killing is itself compassion (4.18–23). The boon the gods ask (4.36–37) is the first statement in the text of the fruit of reciting it, which the Goddess sets out at length in chapter 12. The closing verses (4.40–42) end the middle carita and announce the third, so that the chapter is both an ending and a hinge.
Sanskrit patterns to watch
Most of the hymn (4.2–23) is in vasantatilakā, fourteen syllables to a printed line, so words and junctions often run across a line-end (दोषै- / र्न, 4.7; एत- / न्नीतं, 4.14; भवतीप्रसादा- / ल्लोकत्रये, 4.16); the petitions (4.24–27) return to anuṣṭubh. The hymn style is relative and correlative (या … तां त्वाम्, 4.5; यस्याः … सा, 4.4; यानि … तैः, 4.26–27), with predicates in the nominative and असि understood or stated (मेधासि, दुर्गासि, 4.11). The Goddess is the subject of second-person passives (ज्ञायसे, 4.7; उच्चार्यसे, 4.8; अभ्यस्यसे, 4.9); impersonal passives carry the wonders of the battle (प्रहृतम्, 4.12; कैर्जीव्यते, 4.13). Prayer uses the imperative (प्रसीद, पाहि, रक्ष), deliberation the first-person imperative (किं वर्णयाम, 4.6), courtesy the passive imperative (व्रियताम्, 4.32) and the respectful third person (भवती, भगवत्या). The gods’ boons are asked in epic ātmanepada optatives (हिंसेथाः, भवेथाः, 4.36–37), with the repeated participle संस्मृता संस्मृता ‘each time remembered’ (4.36).
The teaching (as distinct from the narrative)
Literally, the gods praise the Goddess who has saved them, worship her, ask two boons, and she grants them and disappears; Medhas then announces the next story. As teaching, the Śākta tradition reads the hymn as a statement of who the Goddess is: one power behind fortune and misfortune, intellect and faith, sacrifice and liberation (4.5–4.11), whose anger and grace are one act — she destroys the asuras and purifies them in the same stroke (4.18–4.23). It also draws from the boon that remembrance and the recitation of these verses are enough to draw her help (4.17, 4.36–4.37). A modern reflection, which is ours and not the text’s, might add that the gods, once rescued, ask first for nothing and then for others: every mortal who will say their words. The text itself states only the hymn, the worship, the boons and her promise.

Themes

  • The Goddess is not the sum of the gods’ powers
  • One Devī in fortune, intellect, faith and the Veda
  • Gentle and terrible: one face
  • Compassion even to enemies
  • Remembrance, recitation and the boon
Recited before the chapter (liturgical, from the Gita Press pāṭha)

These lines belong to the recitation, not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras. They are given here in Sanskrit and IAST only.

Dhyāna

ॐ कालाभ्राभां कटाक्षैररिकुलभयदां मौलिबद्धेन्दुरेखां
शङ्खं चक्रं कृपाणं त्रिशिखमपि करैरुद्वहन्तीं त्रिनेत्राम्।
सिंहस्कन्धाधिरूढां त्रिभुवनमखिलं तेजसा पूरयन्तीं
ध्यायेद् दुर्गां जयाख्यां त्रिदशपरिवृतां सेवितां सिद्धिकामैः॥

ॐ kālābhrābhāṃ kaṭākṣairarikulabhayadāṃ maulibaddhendurekhāṃ
śaṅkhaṃ cakraṃ kṛpāṇaṃ triśikhamapi karairudvahantīṃ trinetrām|
siṃhaskandhādhirūḍhāṃ tribhuvanamakhilaṃ tejasā pūrayantīṃ
dhyāyed durgāṃ jayākhyāṃ tridaśaparivṛtāṃ sevitāṃ siddhikāmaiḥ||

4.1Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ॐ ऋषिरुवाच ॥ १ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā, the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel: ऋषिः → ऋषिर् before उ, written together.

C. Morphology

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicating syllable (व → उ): ‘(he) said’, the narrative past.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe seer said:

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe sage Medhas resumes his story for King Suratha (and the merchant Samādhi). Chapter 3 ended with Mahiṣāsura beheaded (3.42), his army destroyed (3.43) and the gods beginning to praise the Devī (3.44); this chapter gives that praise in full. In recitation the mantra carries the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the pāṭha, not to the Purāṇa.

4.2Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

शक्रादयः सुरगणा निहतेऽतिवीर्ये
तस्मिन्दुरात्मनि सुरारिबले च देव्या।
तां तुष्टुवुः प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसा
वाग्भिः प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः ॥ २ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शक्रादयः | सुरगणाः | निहते | अतिवीर्येतस्मिन् | दुरात्मनि | सुरारिबले | च | देव्याताम् | तुष्टुवुः | प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसाःवाग्भिः | प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः

3IAST

As recitedśakrādayaḥ suragaṇā nihate'tivīrye
tasmindurātmani surāribale ca devyā |
tāṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ praṇatinamraśirodharāṃsā
vāgbhiḥ praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ ||

Separatedśakrādayaḥ | suragaṇāḥ | nihate | ativīrye
tasmin | durātmani | surāribale | ca | devyā
tām | tuṣṭuvuḥ | praṇatinamraśirodharāṃsāḥ
vāgbhiḥ | praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ

चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); joins तस्मिन् … and सुरारिबले

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सुरगणा निहते suragaṇā nihate → सुरगणाः + निहते
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga (as रु) becomes y, and that y is dropped before a consonant (a further rule, not cited here by number), leaving -ā. The same change gives प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसा before वाग्भिः.
  • निहतेऽतिवीर्ये nihate'tivīrye → निहते + अतिवीर्ये
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Final -e before initial a: the a is absorbed (avagraha).
  • सुरारिबले surāribale → सुर + अरि + बले
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • तां तुष्टुवुः tāṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ → ताम् + तुष्टुवुः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.
  • प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ → प्रहर्ष + पुलक + उद्गम + चारु + देहाः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o (पुलक + उद्गम → पुलकोद्गम). Inside उद्गम the t of the prefix उत् is voiced before g (झलां जशोऽन्ते, 8.2.39).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शक्रादयः śakrādayaḥ — bahuvrīhi (tadguṇasaṃvijñāna: Śakra himself is among them)
    Members: शक्र + आदि · Vigraha: शक्रः आदिः येषां ते śakraḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those of whom Śakra is the first”; in the verse: Indra and the other gods
  • सुरगणाः suragaṇāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: सुर + गण · Vigraha: सुराणां गणाः surāṇāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally “hosts of the gods”; in the verse: the assembled gods
  • अतिवीर्ये ativīrye — bahuvrīhi (with the prefix अति as first member)
    Members: अति + वीर्य · Vigraha: अतिशयितं वीर्यं यस्य सः, तस्मिन् atiśayitaṃ vīryaṃ yasya saḥ, tasmin
    Literally “one whose valour is excessive”; in the verse: the mighty Mahiṣāsura
  • दुरात्मनि durātmani — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दुर् + आत्मन् · Vigraha: दुष्टः आत्मा यस्य सः, तस्मिन् duṣṭaḥ ātmā yasya saḥ, tasmin
    Literally “one whose self is bad”; in the verse: the wicked one
  • सुरारिबले surāribale — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सुरारि)
    Members: सुरारि + बल · Vigraha: सुराणाम् अरयः सुरारयः; सुरारीणां बलं सुरारिबलम्, तस्मिन् surāṇām arayaḥ surārayaḥ; surārīṇāṃ balaṃ surāribalam, tasmin
    Literally “in the army of the enemies of the gods”; in the verse: Mahiṣa’s asura host
  • प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसाः praṇatinamraśirodharāṃsāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (शिरोधरांसाः) and a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (प्रणतिनम्र)
    Members: प्रणति + नम्र + शिरोधरा + अंस · Vigraha: शिरोधराः च अंसाः च शिरोधरांसाः; प्रणत्या नम्राः शिरोधरांसाः येषां ते śirodharāḥ ca aṃsāḥ ca śirodharāṃsāḥ; praṇatyā namrāḥ śirodharāṃsāḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “whose necks and shoulders are bent by obeisance”; in the verse: bowing low as they praised
    Alternative: शिरोधरा ‘neck’ is itself a compound, शिरः धरति इति ‘that which holds the head’ (upapada); the whole is still one bahuvrīhi.
  • प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing tatpuruṣas (ṣaṣṭhī पुलकोद्गम; tṛtīyā प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गम)
    Members: प्रहर्ष + पुलकोद्गम + चारु + देह · Vigraha: पुलकानाम् उद्गमः पुलकोद्गमः; प्रहर्षेण (जातः) पुलकोद्गमः प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमः; तेन चारवः देहाः येषां ते pulakānām udgamaḥ pulakodgamaḥ; praharṣeṇa (jātaḥ) pulakodgamaḥ praharṣapulakodgamaḥ; tena cāravaḥ dehāḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “whose bodies are beautiful with the rising of hair-thrills from great joy”; in the verse: thrilling with joy

C. Morphology

  • तुष्टुवुः tuṣṭuvuḥ — Perfect of √स्तु: reduplication तु-स्तु-, the root’s s becomes ṣ after the u of the reduplicated syllable and the t then becomes ṭ beside it (ष्टुना ष्टुः, 8.4.41); before the vowel ending -उस् the root’s u becomes uv: तुष्टुवुः ‘they praised’.
  • निहते nihate — Locative absolute: participle and noun in the locative (तस्मिन् … निहते) state the circumstance ‘when he had been slain’; the agent देव्या stands in the instrumental.
  • वाग्भिः vāgbhiḥ — Instrumental plural of वाच्: before the ending -भिः the final c becomes k and is then voiced to g (झलां जशोऽन्ते, 8.2.39).

D. Anvaya — prose order

तस्मिन् अतिवीर्ये दुरात्मनि सुरारिबले च देव्या निहते, प्रणतिनम्रशिरोधरांसाः प्रहर्षपुलकोद्गमचारुदेहाः शक्रादयः सुरगणाः तां वाग्भिः तुष्टुवुः।

tasmin ativīrye durātmani surāribale ca devyā nihate, praṇatinamraśirodharāṃsāḥ praharṣapulakodgamacārudehāḥ śakrādayaḥ suragaṇāḥ tāṃ vāgbhiḥ tuṣṭuvuḥ |

A locative absolute (तस्मिन् … सुरारिबले च देव्या निहते) sets the time; the main clause is शक्रादयः सुरगणाः तां तुष्टुवुः, with two bahuvrīhi epithets of the gods and the instrument वाग्भिः.

6Translation

LiteralWhen that one of surpassing valour, the evil-souled, and the army of the gods’ enemies had been slain by the Devī, the hosts of the gods, Śakra and the rest, their necks and shoulders bent low in reverence, their bodies beautiful with hair standing on end for joy, praised her with words.

ReadableWhen the Devī had slain that mighty, evil-hearted one and the army of the gods’ enemies, Indra and the hosts of the gods, necks and shoulders bowed in reverence, their bodies thrilling with joy, praised her in words.

‘That one’ (तस्मिन्) is Mahiṣāsura, who is named only in 4.12.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe narrative frame of the hymn: Medhas tells how the gods praised the Devī after Mahiṣa’s death (3.42–44). With this stanza the text changes metre: 4.2–4.23 are in vasantatilakā, a fourteen-syllable quarter printed one quarter to a line, so a word can run over a line-end (as in 4.7–4.10); 4.24–4.27 return to anuṣṭubh.

Traditional interpretationThe chapter’s colophon names it शक्रादिस्तुतिः, ‘the praise by Śakra and the others’, after the first word of this stanza. The bodily signs — bowing and the thrill of joy (pulaka) — are the classical marks of devotion: the praise is offered by the whole person, not only in words.

4.3Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

देव्या यया ततमिदं जगदात्मशक्त्या
निश्शेषदेवगणशक्तिसमूहमूर्त्या।
तामम्बिकामखिलदेवमहर्षिपूज्यां
भक्त्या नताः स्म विदधातु शुभानि सा नः ॥ ३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देव्या | यया | ततम् | इदम् | जगत् | आत्मशक्त्यानिश्शेषदेवगणशक्तिसमूहमूर्त्याताम् | अम्बिकाम् | अखिलदेवमहर्षिपूज्याम्भक्त्या | नताः | स्म | विदधातु | शुभानि | सा | नः

3IAST

As reciteddevyā yayā tatamidaṃ jagadātmaśaktyā
niśśeṣadevagaṇaśaktisamūhamūrtyā |
tāmambikāmakhiladevamaharṣipūjyāṃ
bhaktyā natāḥ sma vidadhātu śubhāni sā naḥ ||

Separateddevyā | yayā | tatam | idam | jagat | ātmaśaktyā
niśśeṣadevagaṇaśaktisamūhamūrtyā
tām | ambikām | akhiladevamaharṣipūjyām
bhaktyā | natāḥ | sma | vidadhātu | śubhāni | sā | naḥ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ततमिदं जगदात्मशक्त्या tatamidaṃ jagadātmaśaktyā → ततम् + इदम् + जगत् + आत्मशक्त्या
    final m before vowel and consonant; consonant sandhi (voicing) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    ततम् keeps its m before the vowel of इदम्; इदम् shows anusvāra before j; the t of जगत् becomes d before the vowel ā.
  • निश्शेष niśśeṣa → निः + शेष
    visarga sandhi inside a compound · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    The visarga of निः becomes s before the sibilant and the s becomes ś beside ś: निश्शेष. Visarga before a sibilant may also simply stay (an optional rule): निःशेष, the reading of GRETIL and Freund (corrections.json, variant).
  • तामम्बिकामखिल tāmambikāmakhila → ताम् + अम्बिकाम् + अखिल…
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    Both final m’s stand before vowels and are written together with them.
  • अखिलदेवमहर्षिपूज्यां akhiladevamaharṣipūjyāṃ → अखिल + देव + महा + ऋषि + पूज्याम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + ṛ → ar (महा + ऋषि → महर्षि). The final m becomes anusvāra before the bh of भक्त्या (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • नताः स्म natāḥ sma → नताः + स्म
    visarga before a sibilant · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before the s the visarga is kept (the optional alternative to assimilation).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • आत्मशक्त्या ātmaśaktyā — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: आत्मन् + शक्ति · Vigraha: आत्मनः शक्तिः, तया ātmanaḥ śaktiḥ, tayā
    Literally “by the power of (her) self”; in the verse: by her own power, needing nothing outside her
  • निश्शेषदेवगणशक्तिसमूहमूर्त्या niśśeṣadevagaṇaśaktisamūhamūrtyā — bahuvrīhi containing ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas (…शक्ति, …समूह) and a karmadhāraya (निश्शेषदेवगण)
    Members: निश्शेष + देवगण + शक्ति + समूह + मूर्ति · Vigraha: निश्शेषाः देवगणाः; निश्शेषदेवगणानां शक्तयः; तासां समूहः; सः मूर्तिः यस्याः सा, तया niśśeṣāḥ devagaṇāḥ; niśśeṣadevagaṇānāṃ śaktayaḥ; tāsāṃ samūhaḥ; saḥ mūrtiḥ yasyāḥ sā, tayā
    Literally “whose form is the mass of the powers of the entire hosts of gods”; in the verse: the Devī whose body was formed from the gathered energies of all the gods (2.10–19)
    Alternative: It can also be read as a karmadhāraya qualifying आत्मशक्त्या: ‘by her own power, which is the embodied mass of the gods’ powers’. The feminine i-stem ending fits either; the bahuvrīhi, describing the Devī herself, matches chapter 2.
  • निश्शेष niśśeṣa — bahuvrīhi (prādi)
    Members: निस् + शेष · Vigraha: निर्गतः शेषः यस्मात् तत् nirgataḥ śeṣaḥ yasmāt tat
    Literally “from which the remainder has gone”; in the verse: all, without exception
  • अखिलदेवमहर्षिपूज्याम् akhiladevamaharṣipūjyām — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (with the gerundive पूज्य) containing an itaretara dvandva (देवमहर्षि); महर्षि is a karmadhāraya (महान् ऋषिः)
    Members: अखिल + देव + महर्षि + पूज्य · Vigraha: देवाः च महर्षयः च देवमहर्षयः; अखिलैः देवमहर्षिभिः पूज्या, ताम् devāḥ ca maharṣayaḥ ca devamaharṣayaḥ; akhilaiḥ devamaharṣibhiḥ pūjyā, tām
    Literally “to be worshipped by all the gods and great sages”; in the verse: honoured by every god and seer

C. Morphology

  • ततम् tatam — Past passive participle of √तन्: the root’s nasal is lost before the participle ending (तन् + त → तत). With no copula expressed, ततम् is a finite-like predicate: ‘(this world) is pervaded’.
  • विदधातु vidadhātu — Imperative third singular of वि + √धा, a reduplicating (juhotyādi) root: दधा- + तु. ‘May she grant’ — the benediction that closes the first stanza.
  • स्म sma — The metre (vasantatilakā, ⏑ in the fifth syllable) needs a light syllable here, so the printed स्म cannot be a sandhi form of स्मः (which would give स्मो before v). The same phrase returns in 4.5 (नताः स्म परिपालय).

D. Anvaya — prose order

यया निश्शेषदेवगणशक्तिसमूहमूर्त्या देव्या आत्मशक्त्या इदं जगत् ततम्, तां अखिलदेवमहर्षिपूज्याम् अम्बिकां (वयं) भक्त्या नताः स्म। सा नः शुभानि विदधातु।

yayā niśśeṣadevagaṇaśaktisamūhamūrtyā devyā ātmaśaktyā idaṃ jagat tatam, tāṃ akhiladevamaharṣipūjyām ambikāṃ (vayaṃ) bhaktyā natāḥ sma | sā naḥ śubhāni vidadhātu |

A relative clause (यया … ततम्) describes the Devī; the main clause (ताम् … नताः स्म) is the act of bowing; a separate benediction with the imperative विदधातु closes the stanza.

6Translation

LiteralTo that Ambikā, worthy of worship by all gods and great sages — the Devī, whose form is the mass of the powers of all the hosts of gods, by whom through her own power this world is pervaded — we are bowed with devotion. May she bestow auspicious things on us.

ReadableTo Ambikā, worshipped by all the gods and great sages — the Devī who by her own power pervades this world, her form the gathered powers of all the hosts of gods — we bow in devotion. May she grant us what is good.

The passive ‘by whom … is pervaded’ is turned active; ‘we bow’ renders नताः स्म.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first stanza of praise recalls how the Devī came to be: her body was formed from the tejas of all the gods (2.10–19). Yet the world is pervaded by her own power (आत्मशक्त्या): what the gods gave was not borrowed strength. The gods bow and ask for blessings (शुभानि).

Traditional interpretationThe prayer विदधातु शुभानि सा नः has an echo in the gods’ hymn of chapter 5 (5.81: करोतु सा नः शुभहेतुरीश्वरी शुभानि). The name Ambikā, ‘Mother’, is the one the text uses most often for the warrior Goddess.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has the name Ambikā (name 295, अम्बिका). The two works are separate; the name is shared Śākta usage.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Variant: निश्शेष (Gita Press, kept) against निःशेष (GRETIL, Freund) — orthographic only, same word. Interpretation: नताः स्म is taken in the usual sense ‘we are bowed’ (स्म for स्मः, the visarga absent, as the metre requires); it may also be the particle स्म with the bare participle (‘bowed indeed’). The sense is the same.

4.4Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

यस्याः प्रभावमतुलं भगवाननन्तो
ब्रह्मा हरश्च न हि वक्तुमलं बलं च।
सा चण्डिकाखिलजगत्परिपालनाय
नाशाय चाशुभभयस्य मतिं करोतु ॥ ४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यस्याः | प्रभावम् | अतुलम् | भगवान् | अनन्तःब्रह्मा | हरः | च | न | हि | वक्तुम् | अलम् | बलम् | चसा | चण्डिका | अखिलजगत्परिपालनायनाशाय | च | अशुभभयस्य | मतिम् | करोतु

3IAST

As recitedyasyāḥ prabhāvamatulaṃ bhagavānananto
brahmā haraśca na hi vaktumalaṃ balaṃ ca |
sā caṇḍikākhilajagatparipālanāya
nāśāya cāśubhabhayasya matiṃ karotu ||

Separatedyasyāḥ | prabhāvam | atulam | bhagavān | anantaḥ
brahmā | haraḥ | ca | na | hi | vaktum | alam | balam | ca
sā | caṇḍikā | akhilajagatparipālanāya
nāśāya | ca | aśubhabhayasya | matim | karotu

चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

मतिम्matimmind, resolvef. acc. sg. of मति; object of करोतु
करोतुkarotumay she make, may she set√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भगवाननन्तो bhagavānananto → भगवान् + अनन्तः
    final n before a vowel; visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    After the long ā the final n is not doubled (ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम्, 8.3.32, needs a short vowel); it is simply written with the vowel. अनन्तः before the voiced b of ब्रह्मा becomes अनन्तो.
  • हरश्च haraśca → हरः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c → s → ś.
  • वक्तुमलं बलं च vaktumalaṃ balaṃ ca → वक्तुम् + अलम् + बलम् + च
    final m before vowel and consonants · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    वक्तुम् keeps its m before the vowel of अलम्; अलम् and बलम् show anusvāra before b and c.
  • चण्डिकाखिल caṇḍikākhila → चण्डिका + अखिल…
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. Likewise चाशुभ (च + अशुभ).
  • मतिं करोतु matiṃ karotu → मतिम् + करोतु
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतुलम् atulam — nañ-bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ + तुला · Vigraha: न विद्यते तुला यस्य तत् na vidyate tulā yasya tat
    Literally “that which has no balance (to weigh it against)”; in the verse: incomparable
  • अनन्तः anantaḥ — nañ-bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ + अन्त · Vigraha: न विद्यते अन्तः यस्य सः na vidyate antaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally “he who has no end”; in the verse: Ananta — here Viṣṇu, beside Brahmā and Hara
    Alternative: Ananta is also the name of Śeṣa, the serpent with a thousand mouths; the triad with Brahmā and Hara favours Viṣṇu.
  • अखिलजगत्परिपालनाय akhilajagatparipālanāya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (अखिलजगत्)
    Members: अखिल + जगत् + परिपालन · Vigraha: अखिलं जगत् अखिलजगत्; अखिलजगतः परिपालनम्, तस्मै akhilaṃ jagat akhilajagat; akhilajagataḥ paripālanam, tasmai
    Literally “for the protection of the whole world”; in the verse: to protect all that lives
  • अशुभभयस्य aśubhabhayasya — samāhāra dvandva (neuter singular)
    Members: अशुभ + भय · Vigraha: अशुभं च भयं च अशुभभयम्, तस्य aśubhaṃ ca bhayaṃ ca aśubhabhayam, tasya
    Literally “of evil and fear”; in the verse: of misfortune and dread
    Alternative: Also read as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa, अशुभात् भयम्, ‘fear arising from evil’. The sense is close; the dvandva is the more common reading.
  • अशुभ aśubha — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + शुभ · Vigraha: न शुभम् na śubham
    Literally “not auspicious”; in the verse: evil, misfortune

C. Morphology

  • वक्तुम् vaktum — Infinitive of √वच् (वच् + तुम्, c → k before t). With अलम् it expresses capacity: न … वक्तुम् अलम् ‘not able to tell’; the verb ‘are’ is understood.
  • करोतु karotu — Imperative third singular of √कृ: stem करो- (tanādi u-class) + तु. मतिं करोतु ‘may she set her mind on’.
  • भगवान् bhagavān — Nominative singular of भगवत् (भग + matup → -वत्): the vowel before the -nt is lengthened and the final t lost: भगवान्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यस्याः अतुलं प्रभावं बलं च भगवान् अनन्तः ब्रह्मा हरः च वक्तुं न हि अलम्, सा चण्डिका अखिलजगत्परिपालनाय अशुभभयस्य नाशाय च मतिं करोतु।

yasyāḥ atulaṃ prabhāvaṃ balaṃ ca bhagavān anantaḥ brahmā haraḥ ca vaktuṃ na hi alam, sā caṇḍikā akhilajagatparipālanāya aśubhabhayasya nāśāya ca matiṃ karotu |

A relative clause (यस्याः … अलम्, the verb ‘are’ understood) and its correlative main clause (सा चण्डिका … करोतु); two datives of purpose depend on मतिं करोतु.

6Translation

LiteralWhose incomparable majesty and strength the Blessed Ananta, Brahmā and Hara are indeed not able to tell — may that Caṇḍikā set her mind on the protection of the whole world and on the destruction of evil and fear.

ReadableHer incomparable majesty and strength not even blessed Viṣṇu, Brahmā or Śiva can describe. May that Caṇḍikā turn her mind to protecting the whole world and to ending evil and fear.

Ananta and Hara are named as Viṣṇu and Śiva; ‘not even’ renders the emphatic न हि.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe three great gods of the triad cannot describe her power; the lesser gods therefore do not try to measure her but ask her to act. The prayer has two aims: protection (परिपालन) and the removal of evil and fear.

Traditional interpretationThe thought that even Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva cannot describe the Goddess is a commonplace of Śākta hymns; the next stanzas (4.6–4.7) develop it. Caṇḍikā, ‘the Fierce One’, is the name from which the whole text takes its popular title, the Caṇḍī.

4.5Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

या श्रीः स्वयं सुकृतिनां भवनेष्वलक्ष्मीः
पापात्मनां कृतधियां हृदयेषु बुद्धिः।
श्रद्धा सतां कुलजनप्रभवस्य लज्जा
तां त्वां नताः स्म परिपालय देवि विश्वम् ॥ ५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | श्रीः | स्वयम् | सुकृतिनाम् | भवनेषु | अलक्ष्मीःपापात्मनाम् | कृतधियाम् | हृदयेषु | बुद्धिःश्रद्धा | सताम् | कुलजनप्रभवस्य | लज्जाताम् | त्वाम् | नताः | स्म | परिपालय | देवि | विश्वम्

3IAST

As recitedyā śrīḥ svayaṃ sukṛtināṃ bhavaneṣvalakṣmīḥ
pāpātmanāṃ kṛtadhiyāṃ hṛdayeṣu buddhiḥ |
śraddhā satāṃ kulajanaprabhavasya lajjā
tāṃ tvāṃ natāḥ sma paripālaya devi viśvam ||

Separatedyā | śrīḥ | svayam | sukṛtinām | bhavaneṣu | alakṣmīḥ
pāpātmanām | kṛtadhiyām | hṛdayeṣu | buddhiḥ
śraddhā | satām | kulajanaprabhavasya | lajjā
tām | tvām | natāḥ | sma | paripālaya | devi | viśvam

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भवनेष्वलक्ष्मीः bhavaneṣvalakṣmīḥ → भवनेषु + अलक्ष्मीः
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v.
  • पापात्मनां कृतधियां हृदयेषु pāpātmanāṃ kṛtadhiyāṃ hṛdayeṣu → पापात्मनाम् + कृतधियाम् + हृदयेषु
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The final m of both genitive plurals is written as anusvāra before a consonant. Likewise सतां कुल…, तां त्वां.
  • पापात्मनां pāpātmanāṃ → पाप + आत्मनाम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • श्रीः स्वयं śrīḥ svayaṃ → श्रीः + स्वयम्
    visarga before a sibilant · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    The visarga is kept before s (assimilation to स्स् is the optional alternative).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अलक्ष्मीः alakṣmīḥ — nañ tatpuruṣa (in the sense of the opposite)
    Members: अ + लक्ष्मी · Vigraha: न लक्ष्मीः na lakṣmīḥ
    Literally “not-Lakṣmī”; in the verse: misfortune, Lakṣmī’s opposite
  • पापात्मनाम् pāpātmanām — bahuvrīhi
    Members: पाप + आत्मन् · Vigraha: पापः आत्मा येषां ते, तेषाम् pāpaḥ ātmā yeṣāṃ te, teṣām
    Literally “of those whose self is sinful”; in the verse: of the wicked
  • कृतधियाम् kṛtadhiyām — bahuvrīhi
    Members: कृत + धी · Vigraha: कृता (संस्कृता) धीः येषां ते, तेषाम् kṛtā (saṃskṛtā) dhīḥ yeṣāṃ te, teṣām
    Literally “of those whose understanding is made (formed)”; in the verse: of the cultivated, the learned
  • कुलजनप्रभवस्य kulajanaprabhavasya — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (कुलजन)
    Members: कुलजन + प्रभव · Vigraha: कुलस्य (सत्कुलस्य) जनाः कुलजनाः; कुलजनाः प्रभवः यस्य सः, तस्य kulasya (satkulasya) janāḥ kulajanāḥ; kulajanāḥ prabhavaḥ yasya saḥ, tasya
    Literally “of one whose origin is the people of a family”; in the verse: of the well-born
    Alternative: कुल here has the sense ‘good family’, as in कुलीन. The compound may also be taken as a pañcamī-type bahuvrīhi, कुलजनेभ्यः प्रभवः यस्य; the sense is unchanged.

C. Morphology

  • सुकृतिनाम् sukṛtinām — सुकृतिन् is a possessive in -in from सुकृत ‘good deed’: ‘one who has good deeds’. Genitive plural -इनाम्.
  • कृतधियाम् kṛtadhiyām — धी ‘thought’ at the end of a compound declines as a root-noun: before a vowel ending ī becomes iy (कृतधियाम्).
  • सताम् satām — Present participle of √अस् (सत्, the a of the root lost), genitive plural: ‘of those who are (truly)’, i.e. the good.
  • परिपालय paripālaya — Imperative second singular of the curādi stem पालय-: the bare stem serves as the form. The hymn turns from description to request.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या (त्वं) सुकृतिनां भवनेषु स्वयं श्रीः, पापात्मनां (भवनेषु) अलक्ष्मीः, कृतधियां हृदयेषु बुद्धिः, सतां श्रद्धा, कुलजनप्रभवस्य लज्जा (असि), तां त्वां (वयं) नताः स्म। देवि, विश्वं परिपालय।

yā (tvaṃ) sukṛtināṃ bhavaneṣu svayaṃ śrīḥ, pāpātmanāṃ (bhavaneṣu) alakṣmīḥ, kṛtadhiyāṃ hṛdayeṣu buddhiḥ, satāṃ śraddhā, kulajanaprabhavasya lajjā (asi), tāṃ tvāṃ (vayaṃ) natāḥ sma | devi, viśvaṃ paripālaya |

Five predicates in a relative clause (या … लज्जा, the verb ‘are’ understood), each with a genitive of the persons in whom she is that quality; the correlative ताम् त्वाम् is the object of bowing; a closing imperative.

6Translation

LiteralYou who are yourself Śrī in the homes of the virtuous, Alakṣmī (in those) of the evil-souled, understanding in the hearts of those of cultivated mind, faith of the good, modesty of one born of a noble family — to that you we are bowed. O Devī, protect the universe.

ReadableYou are Fortune herself in the homes of the virtuous and Misfortune in the homes of the wicked; understanding in the hearts of the wise, faith in the good, modesty in the well-born. To you we bow: O Devī, protect the world.

‘Homes’ is supplied for the wicked from the first clause; ‘in the good’, ‘in the well-born’ render genitives.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningOne Devī is named in five qualities, each placed in a kind of person: prosperity and its opposite, understanding, faith and modesty. The same power that blesses the virtuous is misfortune in the house of the wicked; the stanza does not make her two goddesses.

Traditional interpretationSeveral of these names return as forms of the Devī ‘established in all beings’ in the hymn of chapter 5: बुद्धिरूपेण (5.20-22), लज्जारूपेण (5.44-46), श्रद्धारूपेण (5.50-52), लक्ष्मीरूपेण (5.56-58). Brahmā’s hymn had already named her श्री and बुद्धि (1.79).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) includes Lajjā among the Goddess’s names (name 740, लज्जा).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the stanza places the divine in a household’s fortune, a mind’s clarity, a person’s faith and restraint — the Goddess is met in character as much as in temples.

4.6Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

किं वर्णयाम तव रूपमचिन्त्यमेतत्
किं चातिवीर्यमसुरक्षयकारि भूरि।
किं चाहवेषु चरितानि तवाद्भुतानि
सर्वेषु देव्यसुरदेवगणादिकेषु ॥ ६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

किम् | वर्णयाम | तव | रूपम् | अचिन्त्यम् | एतत्किम् | च | अतिवीर्यम् | असुरक्षयकारि | भूरिकिम् | च | आहवेषु | चरितानि | तव | अद्भुतानिसर्वेषु | देवि | असुरदेवगणादिकेषु

3IAST

As recitedkiṃ varṇayāma tava rūpamacintyametat
kiṃ cātivīryamasurakṣayakāri bhūri |
kiṃ cāhaveṣu caritāni tavādbhutāni
sarveṣu devyasuradevagaṇādikeṣu ||

Separatedkim | varṇayāma | tava | rūpam | acintyam | etat
kim | ca | ativīryam | asurakṣayakāri | bhūri
kim | ca | āhaveṣu | caritāni | tava | adbhutāni
sarveṣu | devi | asuradevagaṇādikeṣu

वर्णयामvarṇayāmashould we describe√वर्ण् (curādi, 10P ‘to describe, depict’), loṭ (imperative), uttama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada; deliberative

अद्भुतानिadbhutāniwondrousn. acc. pl. of अद्भुत; qualifies चरितानि
सर्वेषुsarveṣuamong allpronominal adjective सर्व, m. loc. pl.; qualifies असुरदेवगणादिकेषु
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
असुरदेवगणादिकेषुasuradevagaṇādikeṣuthe hosts of asuras, gods and the restm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • रूपमचिन्त्यमेतत् rūpamacintyametat → रूपम् + अचिन्त्यम् + एतत्
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    Both m’s stand before vowels and are written together with them.
  • चातिवीर्यमसुरक्षयकारि cātivīryamasurakṣayakāri → च + अतिवीर्यम् + असुरक्षयकारि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); final m before a vowel · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā; the m of अतिवीर्यम् is written with the following a. Likewise चाहवेषु (च + आहवेषु) and तवाद्भुतानि (तव + अद्भुतानि).
  • देव्यसुर devyasura → देवि + असुर…
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    The final i of the vocative becomes y before a.
  • किं वर्णयाम kiṃ varṇayāma → किम् + वर्णयाम
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise किं च in lines 2 and 3.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अचिन्त्यम् acintyam — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + चिन्त्य · Vigraha: न चिन्त्यम् na cintyam
    Literally “not to be thought”; in the verse: beyond the reach of thought
  • अतिवीर्यम् ativīryam — prādi tatpuruṣa
    Members: अति + वीर्य · Vigraha: अतिशयितं वीर्यम् atiśayitaṃ vīryam
    Literally “excessive valour”; in the verse: valour beyond all measure
  • असुरक्षयकारि asurakṣayakāri — upapada tatpuruṣa (agent noun कारिन्, kṛt -in) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (असुरक्षय)
    Members: असुर + क्षय + कारिन् · Vigraha: असुराणां क्षयः असुरक्षयः; तं करोति इति असुरक्षयकारि asurāṇāṃ kṣayaḥ asurakṣayaḥ; taṃ karoti iti asurakṣayakāri
    Literally “making the destruction of the asuras”; in the verse: that wiped out the demons
  • असुरदेवगणादिकेषु asuradevagaṇādikeṣu — bahuvrīhi (with आदि + svārthe -क) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa and an itaretara dvandva (असुरदेव)
    Members: असुर + देव + गण + आदिक · Vigraha: असुराः च देवाः च असुरदेवाः; तेषां गणाः असुरदेवगणाः; ते आदयः येषां ते असुरदेवगणादिकाः, तेषु asurāḥ ca devāḥ ca asuradevāḥ; teṣāṃ gaṇāḥ asuradevagaṇāḥ; te ādayaḥ yeṣāṃ te asuradevagaṇādikāḥ, teṣu
    Literally “among those beginning with the hosts of asuras and gods”; in the verse: among gods, demons and all other beings

C. Morphology

  • वर्णयाम varṇayāma — Imperative first plural of the curādi stem वर्णय-: the first-person imperative (‘let us …’) with किम् asks a deliberative question — ‘how are we to describe?’
  • असुरक्षयकारि asurakṣayakāri — Neuter accusative singular of an -in stem: कारिन् → कारि (the n is dropped in the nominative/accusative neuter). It agrees with अतिवीर्यम्.
  • अचिन्त्यम् acintyam — Gerundive (yat) of √चिन्त् ‘to think’ with the negative: ‘not to be thought’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(वयं) तव एतत् अचिन्त्यं रूपं किं वर्णयाम? असुरक्षयकारि भूरि अतिवीर्यं च किम्? देवि, सर्वेषु असुरदेवगणादिकेषु आहवेषु तव अद्भुतानि चरितानि च किम् (वर्णयाम)?

(vayaṃ) tava etat acintyaṃ rūpaṃ kiṃ varṇayāma? asurakṣayakāri bhūri ativīryaṃ ca kim? devi, sarveṣu asuradevagaṇādikeṣu āhaveṣu tava adbhutāni caritāni ca kim (varṇayāma)?

Three questions with one verb (वर्णयाम), each introduced by किम्: the form, the valour, the deeds. The locative सर्वेषु … असुरदेवगणादिकेषु is taken as ‘among all the hosts’ (the battles fought among them).

6Translation

LiteralHow should we describe this unthinkable form of yours? And (how) the abundant, surpassing valour that destroys the asuras? And (how), O Devī, your wondrous deeds in battles, among all the hosts of asuras, gods and the rest?

ReadableHow can we describe this form of yours, beyond all thought? Or your boundless valour that destroys the asuras? Or the wonders you have done in battle, O Devī, among all the hosts of gods, asuras and others?

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods confess that their praise cannot match its object: three questions, each beginning with किम्, about her form, her valour and her deeds. The hymn returns to the same three — form (रूप), valour (वीर्य) and conduct — in 4.21.

Traditional interpretationThe confession of inadequacy is a convention of Sanskrit hymns, but here it follows directly on 4.4: if Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva cannot describe her power, the lesser gods can only wonder at it.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: worship begins where description fails. The questions are not a refusal to speak but a way of speaking that keeps the mystery open.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The locative सर्वेषु … असुरदेवगणादिकेषु can be read ‘(in battles) among all the hosts of asuras, gods and others’ or, as a locative of reference, ‘(wondrous) to all the hosts …’. The first is followed here.

4.7Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

हेतुः समस्तजगतां त्रिगुणापि दोषै-
र्न ज्ञायसे हरिहरादिभिरप्यपारा।
सर्वाश्रयाखिलमिदं जगदंशभूत-
मव्याकृता हि परमा प्रकृतिस्त्वमाद्या ॥ ७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

हेतुः | समस्तजगताम् | त्रिगुणा | अपि | दोषैःन | ज्ञायसे | हरिहरादिभिः | अपि | अपारासर्वाश्रया | अखिलम् | इदम् | जगत् | अंशभूतम्अव्याकृता | हि | परमा | प्रकृतिः | त्वम् | आद्या

3IAST

As recitedhetuḥ samastajagatāṃ triguṇāpi doṣai-
rna jñāyase hariharādibhirapyapārā |
sarvāśrayākhilamidaṃ jagadaṃśabhūta-
mavyākṛtā hi paramā prakṛtistvamādyā ||

Separatedhetuḥ | samastajagatām | triguṇā | api | doṣaiḥ
na | jñāyase | hariharādibhiḥ | api | apārā
sarvāśrayā | akhilam | idam | jagat | aṃśabhūtam
avyākṛtā | hi | paramā | prakṛtiḥ | tvam | ādyā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
हेतुःhetuḥthe causem. nom. sg. of हेतु (u-stem); predicate of the implied त्वम् (a masculine noun predicated of the Goddess)
समस्तजगताम्samastajagatāmof all the worldsn. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
त्रिगुणाtriguṇāpossessed of the three guṇasf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
अपिapithoughindeclinable (concessive)
दोषैःdoṣaiḥby faults, by defectsm. instr. pl. of दोष; with the passive ज्ञायसे
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
ज्ञायसेjñāyaseyou are known√ज्ञा (kryādi, 9U ‘to know’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana
हरिहरादिभिःhariharādibhiḥby Hari, Hara and the othersm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
अपिapievenindeclinable (emphatic)
अपाराapārāwithout a farther shore, unfathomablef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
सर्वाश्रयाsarvāśrayāthe support of allf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
अखिलम्akhilamwholen. nom. sg. of अखिल; qualifies जगत्
इदम्idamthispronoun इदम्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies जगत्
जगत्jagatworldn. nom. sg.; subject
अंशभूतम्aṃśabhūtamis a portion (of you)n. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of जगत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्रिगुणापि triguṇāpi → त्रिगुणा + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • दोषैर्न doṣairna → दोषैः + न
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before the voiced n. The printed text breaks the line between दोषै- and र्न; the word दोषैः belongs to the first line.
  • हरिहरादिभिरप्यपारा hariharādibhirapyapārā → हरिहरादिभिः + अपि + अपारा
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    -iḥ before a vowel is sounded r; the i of अपि becomes y before a.
  • सर्वाश्रयाखिलमिदं sarvāśrayākhilamidaṃ → सर्वाश्रया + अखिलम् + इदम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); final m · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    ā + a → ā; अखिलम् keeps its m before the vowel of इदम्, and इदम् shows anusvāra before j.
  • जगदंशभूत jagadaṃśabhūta → जगत् + अंशभूतम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel. अंशभूतम् runs over the line-end (…भूत- / मव्याकृता) and keeps its m before the vowel of अव्याकृता.
  • प्रकृतिस्त्वमाद्या prakṛtistvamādyā → प्रकृतिः + त्वम् + आद्या
    visarga sandhi; final m before a vowel · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the m of त्वम् is written with the following ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • समस्तजगताम् samastajagatām — karmadhāraya
    Members: समस्त + जगत् · Vigraha: समस्तानि जगन्ति, तेषाम् samastāni jaganti, teṣām
    Literally “of the entire worlds”; in the verse: of every world
  • त्रिगुणा triguṇā — bahuvrīhi (with a numeral first member)
    Members: त्रि + गुण · Vigraha: त्रयः गुणाः यस्याः सा trayaḥ guṇāḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she who has three guṇas”; in the verse: made of sattva, rajas and tamas
  • हरिहरादिभिः hariharādibhiḥ — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (हरिहरौ)
    Members: हरि + हर + आदि · Vigraha: हरिः च हरः च हरिहरौ; हरिहरौ आदी येषां ते, तैः hariḥ ca haraḥ ca hariharau; hariharau ādī yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally “by those of whom Hari and Hara are first”; in the verse: by Viṣṇu, Śiva and the other gods
  • अपारा apārā — nañ-bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ + पार · Vigraha: न विद्यते पारः यस्याः सा na vidyate pāraḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she who has no farther shore”; in the verse: boundless, beyond being crossed or fathomed
  • सर्वाश्रया sarvāśrayā — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, made feminine to agree with the Goddess
    Members: सर्व + आश्रय · Vigraha: सर्वस्य आश्रयः (सर्वाश्रयभूता) sarvasya āśrayaḥ (sarvāśrayabhūtā)
    Literally “the resting-place of all”; in the verse: that on which everything depends
  • अंशभूतम् aṃśabhūtam — karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, become’ as the second member: ‘being a part’)
    Members: अंश + भूत · Vigraha: अंशः भूतम् (अंशरूपम्) aṃśaḥ bhūtam (aṃśarūpam)
    Literally “become a part”; in the verse: a mere portion (of you)
  • अव्याकृता avyākṛtā — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + व्याकृत · Vigraha: न व्याकृता na vyākṛtā
    Literally “not made distinct”; in the verse: the unmanifest, before name and form

C. Morphology

  • ज्ञायसे jñāyase — Passive present of √ज्ञा: stem ज्ञाय- (ज्ञा + yak) with the ātmanepada ending -से, second singular: ‘you are known’. The agent of a passive is put in the instrumental, so दोषैः can be read as agent (see flags).
  • अव्याकृता avyākṛtā — Past participle of वि + आ + √कृ (व्याकृत ‘separated out, made distinct’) with the negative अ-: the Sāṃkhya-Vedānta term for the undifferentiated cause before creation.
  • आद्या ādyā — आद्य is आदि ‘beginning’ + the taddhita -य (the final i dropped): ‘being at the beginning, primal’; feminine आद्या.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(त्वं) समस्तजगतां हेतुः (असि)। त्रिगुणा अपि दोषैः न ज्ञायसे। हरिहरादिभिः अपि अपारा (असि)। सर्वाश्रया (असि); इदम् अखिलं जगत् (तव) अंशभूतम्; त्वं हि आद्या परमा अव्याकृता प्रकृतिः (असि)।

(tvaṃ) samastajagatāṃ hetuḥ (asi) | triguṇā api doṣaiḥ na jñāyase | hariharādibhiḥ api apārā (asi) | sarvāśrayā (asi); idam akhilaṃ jagat (tava) aṃśabhūtam; tvaṃ hi ādyā paramā avyākṛtā prakṛtiḥ (asi) |

A string of predicates of an unexpressed त्वम्, one finite verb (ज्ञायसे, passive), a nominal clause about the world (जगत् अंशभूतम्), and the reason, introduced by हि: you are the primal Prakṛti.

6Translation

Literal(You are) the cause of all the worlds; though possessed of the three guṇas, you are not known by faults; (you are) unfathomable even by Hari, Hara and the others; the support of all — this whole world is (your) portion; for you are the unmanifest, supreme, primal Prakṛti.

ReadableYou are the cause of all the worlds. Though you bear the three guṇas, no fault of theirs is found in you; even Viṣṇu, Śiva and the other gods cannot reach your farther shore. You are the support of all, and this whole world is but a portion of you, for you are the primal Prakṛti, supreme and unmanifest.

‘No fault of theirs is found in you’ follows the usual reading of दोषैः न ज्ञायसे (‘you are not known (to be touched) by faults’); see flags for the other.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn turns from her deeds to her nature: she is the cause of the worlds and the primal, unmanifest Prakṛti, of which the world is only a part. The paradox of the first line: she has the three guṇas, yet their faults do not cling to her. Even Hari and Hara do not reach her farther shore.

Traditional interpretationThe vocabulary is Sāṃkhya (prakṛti, the three guṇas, avyākṛta), but the Prakṛti of this hymn is not an unconscious matter set against spirit: she is the Goddess herself, the cause and support of all. Brahmā’s hymn says the same (1.78: प्रकृतिस्त्वं च सर्वस्य गुणत्रयविभाविनी).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess मूलप्रकृतिः (name 397) and त्रिगुणा (name 984) — the same two ideas in a different text.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: दोषैः न ज्ञायसे admits two readings: ‘you are not known (to be touched) by faults’ (the usual one, followed in tr) or, with दोषैः as the agent of the passive, ‘you are not known by (those who have) faults’. Both are grammatical.

4.8Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

यस्याः समस्तसुरता समुदीरणेन
तृप्तिं प्रयाति सकलेषु मखेषु देवि।
स्वाहासि वै पितृगणस्य च तृप्तिहेतु-
रुच्चार्यसे त्वमत एव जनैः स्वधा च ॥ ८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यस्याः | समस्तसुरता | समुदीरणेनतृप्तिम् | प्रयाति | सकलेषु | मखेषु | देविस्वाहा | असि | वै | पितृगणस्य | च | तृप्तिहेतुःउच्चार्यसे | त्वम् | अतः | एव | जनैः | स्वधा | च

3IAST

As recitedyasyāḥ samastasuratā samudīraṇena
tṛptiṃ prayāti sakaleṣu makheṣu devi |
svāhāsi vai pitṛgaṇasya ca tṛptihetu-
ruccāryase tvamata eva janaiḥ svadhā ca ||

tṛptim | prayāti | sakaleṣu | makheṣu | devi
svāhā | asi | vai | pitṛgaṇasya | ca | tṛptihetuḥ
uccāryase | tvam | ataḥ | eva | janaiḥ | svadhā | ca

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तृप्तिं प्रयाति tṛptiṃ prayāti → तृप्तिम् + प्रयाति
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • स्वाहासि svāhāsi → स्वाहा + असि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • तृप्तिहेतुरुच्चार्यसे tṛptiheturuccāryase → तृप्तिहेतुः + उच्चार्यसे
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After u the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel. The line is broken between तृप्तिहेतु- and रुच्चार्यसे; the word तृप्तिहेतुः belongs to the third line.
  • त्वमत एव tvamata eva → त्वम् + अतः + एव
    final m before a vowel; visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    त्वम् keeps its m before a; -aḥ before a vowel other than a becomes y, the y is dropped, and अत | एव stand apart.
  • उच्चार्यसे uccāryase → उत् + चार्यसे
    consonant sandhi with a verbal prefix · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    The t of the prefix उत् becomes c beside the palatal c.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • समस्तसुरता samastasuratā — karmadhāraya
    Members: समस्त + सुरता · Vigraha: समस्ता सुरता samastā suratā
    Literally “the whole god-assembly”; in the verse: all the gods together
  • पितृगणस्य pitṛgaṇasya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पितृ + गण · Vigraha: पितॄणां गणः, तस्य pitṝṇāṃ gaṇaḥ, tasya
    Literally “of the host of the fathers”; in the verse: of the ancestors, to whom śrāddha offerings are made
  • तृप्तिहेतुः tṛptihetuḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: तृप्ति + हेतु · Vigraha: तृप्तेः हेतुः tṛpteḥ hetuḥ
    Literally “cause of satisfaction”; in the verse: she who satisfies them

C. Morphology

  • समस्तसुरता samastasuratā — सुरता is सुर ‘god’ with the taddhita -ता in the collective sense (‘a body of gods’), formed like जनता ‘a body of people’.
  • उच्चार्यसे uccāryase — Passive of the causative: उत् + चारि (√चर् + ṇic) + yak + से; the causative’s -i- is lost before the passive -य-. ‘You are made to sound, are pronounced’.
  • स्वाहा svāhā — स्वाहा and स्वधा are indeclinable ritual words, the calls that accompany offerings to the gods and to the ancestors. The hymn treats them as names of the Goddess and makes them predicates (‘you are Svāhā’).

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवि, सकलेषु मखेषु यस्याः समुदीरणेन समस्तसुरता तृप्तिं प्रयाति, (सा त्वं) स्वाहा असि वै। पितृगणस्य च तृप्तिहेतुः (त्वम्), अतः एव त्वं जनैः स्वधा च उच्चार्यसे।

devi, sakaleṣu makheṣu yasyāḥ samudīraṇena samastasuratā tṛptiṃ prayāti, (sā tvaṃ) svāhā asi vai | pitṛgaṇasya ca tṛptihetuḥ (tvam), ataḥ eva tvaṃ janaiḥ svadhā ca uccāryase |

A relative clause (यस्याः … प्रयाति) and its main clause (स्वाहा असि वै), then a second sentence with a passive verb whose agent is जनैः and whose predicate nominative is स्वधा.

6Translation

LiteralO Devī, you by whose utterance in all sacrifices the whole company of the gods attains satisfaction — you are indeed Svāhā; and, being the cause of the satisfaction of the host of ancestors, for that very reason you are pronounced by people as Svadhā too.

ReadableO Devī, at every sacrifice it is by the uttering of you that all the gods together are satisfied: you are Svāhā. And because you satisfy the host of the ancestors, people utter you as Svadhā.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningSvāhā is spoken as an oblation is poured into the fire for the gods; Svadhā when it is offered to the ancestors. The hymn identifies the Goddess with these sacred words themselves: the gods and the ancestors are fed through her.

Traditional interpretationBrahmā’s hymn in chapter 1 already said त्वं स्वाहा त्वं स्वधा (1.73). Here the identification is explained: she is Svāhā because the gods are satisfied by her utterance, Svadhā because the ancestors are.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same pair of names side by side: स्वाहा (name 535) and स्वधा (name 536).

4.9Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

या मुक्तिहेतुरविचिन्त्यमहाव्रता त्व-
मभ्यस्यसे सुनियतेन्द्रियतत्त्वसारैः।
मोक्षार्थिभिर्मुनिभिरस्तसमस्तदोषै-
र्विद्यासि सा भगवती परमा हि देवि ॥ ९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | मुक्तिहेतुः | अविचिन्त्यमहाव्रता | त्वम्अभ्यस्यसे | सुनियतेन्द्रियतत्त्वसारैःमोक्षार्थिभिः | मुनिभिः | अस्तसमस्तदोषैःविद्या | असि | सा | भगवती | परमा | हि | देवि

3IAST

As recitedyā muktiheturavicintyamahāvratā tva-
mabhyasyase suniyatendriyatattvasāraiḥ |
mokṣārthibhirmunibhirastasamastadoṣai-
rvidyāsi sā bhagavatī paramā hi devi ||

Separatedyā | muktihetuḥ | avicintyamahāvratā | tvam
abhyasyase | suniyatendriyatattvasāraiḥ
mokṣārthibhiḥ | munibhiḥ | astasamastadoṣaiḥ
vidyā | asi | sā | bhagavatī | paramā | hi | devi

याyā(you) whorelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.
मुक्तिहेतुःmuktihetuḥthe cause of liberationm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
अविचिन्त्यमहाव्रताavicintyamahāvratāwhose great vow is beyond thoughtf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of अभ्यस्यसे

मोक्षार्थिभिःmokṣārthibhiḥby seekers of liberationm. instr. pl. of the in-stem मोक्षार्थिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मुनिभिः
मुनिभिःmunibhiḥby sagesm. instr. pl. of मुनि; agent of the passive अभ्यस्यसे
अस्तसमस्तदोषैःastasamastadoṣaiḥwho have cast off all faultsm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मुनिभिः
विद्याvidyāknowledge, the liberating wisdomf. nom. sg. of विद्या; predicate
असिasiyou are√अस् (adādi, 2P), laṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
साsāthatpronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; correlative of या
भगवतीbhagavatīblessedf. nom. sg. of भगवती (feminine of भगवत्); qualifies विद्या
परमाparamāsupremef. nom. sg. of परम; qualifies विद्या
हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मुक्तिहेतुरविचिन्त्य muktiheturavicintya → मुक्तिहेतुः + अविचिन्त्य…
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After u the visarga is sounded r before a vowel.
  • त्वमभ्यस्यसे tvamabhyasyase → त्वम् + अभ्यस्यसे
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The line breaks between त्व- and मभ्यस्यसे; त्वम् belongs to the first line and its m is joined to the vowel of the next word.
  • सुनियतेन्द्रिय suniyatendriya → सुनियत + इन्द्रिय
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + i → e.
  • मोक्षार्थिभिर्मुनिभिरस्त mokṣārthibhirmunibhirasta → मोक्षार्थिभिः + मुनिभिः + अस्त…
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga is sounded r before the voiced m and before the vowel a.
  • दोषैर्विद्यासि doṣairvidyāsi → दोषैः + विद्या + असि
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    After ai the visarga is r before v (the line breaks after दोषै-); ā + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मुक्तिहेतुः muktihetuḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मुक्ति + हेतु · Vigraha: मुक्तेः हेतुः mukteḥ hetuḥ
    Literally “cause of release”; in the verse: she who brings liberation
  • अविचिन्त्यमहाव्रता avicintyamahāvratā — bahuvrīhi containing karmadhārayas (अविचिन्त्यमहाव्रत; महाव्रत = महत् व्रतम्)
    Members: अविचिन्त्य + महाव्रत · Vigraha: अविचिन्त्यं महाव्रतं यस्याः सा avicintyaṃ mahāvrataṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose great vow is unthinkable”; in the verse: she whose great observance is beyond thought
    Alternative: The feminine ending (व्रत is neuter) shows a bahuvrīhi. Whether the ‘great vow’ is the Goddess’s own or the discipline by which the sages reach her is left open by the compound; the next words (अभ्यस्यसे … मुनिभिः) favour the second.
  • सुनियतेन्द्रियतत्त्वसारैः suniyatendriyatattvasāraiḥ — karmadhāraya of two bahuvrīhis
    Members: सुनियत + इन्द्रिय + तत्त्व + सार · Vigraha: सुनियतानि इन्द्रियाणि येषां ते सुनियतेन्द्रियाः; तत्त्वं सारः येषां ते तत्त्वसाराः; सुनियतेन्द्रियाः च ते तत्त्वसाराः च, तैः suniyatāni indriyāṇi yeṣāṃ te suniyatendriyāḥ; tattvaṃ sāraḥ yeṣāṃ te tattvasārāḥ; suniyatendriyāḥ ca te tattvasārāḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “by those whose senses are well restrained and whose essence (chief concern) is reality”; in the verse: disciplined, intent on the truth
    Alternative: Also read as a single bahuvrīhi, सुनियतेन्द्रियत्वम् एव तत्त्वसारः येषाम् ‘for whom the restraint of the senses is the heart of the matter’; the first analysis is simpler.
  • मोक्षार्थिभिः mokṣārthibhiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मोक्ष + अर्थिन् · Vigraha: मोक्षस्य अर्थिनः, तैः mokṣasya arthinaḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by seekers of release”; in the verse: by those who desire liberation
    Alternative: Also upapada: मोक्षम् अर्थयन्ते इति मोक्षार्थिनः.
  • अस्तसमस्तदोषैः astasamastadoṣaiḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: अस्त + समस्त + दोष · Vigraha: अस्ताः समस्ताः दोषाः यैः ते, तैः astāḥ samastāḥ doṣāḥ yaiḥ te, taiḥ
    Literally “by those by whom all faults are thrown off”; in the verse: free of every flaw

C. Morphology

  • अभ्यस्यसे abhyasyase — Passive present of अभि + √अस् ‘to throw’ (divādi अस्यति): with अभि it means ‘to practise, repeat, apply oneself to’. The agent is मुनिभिः: the Goddess is the object of the sages’ discipline.
  • मोक्षार्थिभिः mokṣārthibhiḥ — Instrumental plural of the in-stem अर्थिन् ‘one who seeks’ (from अर्थ ‘aim’): -इभिः.
  • विद्या vidyā — With the correlative सा and the adjectives परमा, भगवती, the predicate picks up 1.57 (सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता सनातनी).

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवि, या त्वं मुक्तिहेतुः अविचिन्त्यमहाव्रता (असि), सुनियतेन्द्रियतत्त्वसारैः अस्तसमस्तदोषैः मोक्षार्थिभिः मुनिभिः अभ्यस्यसे, सा (त्वं) परमा भगवती विद्या असि हि।

devi, yā tvaṃ muktihetuḥ avicintyamahāvratā (asi), suniyatendriyatattvasāraiḥ astasamastadoṣaiḥ mokṣārthibhiḥ munibhiḥ abhyasyase, sā (tvaṃ) paramā bhagavatī vidyā asi hi |

A relative clause (या … अभ्यस्यसे) with two predicates and a passive verb whose agent is मुनिभिः and its three epithets; the main clause is सा … विद्या असि.

6Translation

LiteralYou who are the cause of liberation, whose great vow is unthinkable, who are practised by sages seeking liberation, their senses well restrained, holding reality as the essence, all faults cast off — you are that supreme, blessed Knowledge, O Devī.

ReadableYou are the cause of liberation, your great discipline beyond thought. Sages who seek release — senses held in check, intent on the truth, every fault cast off — make you their practice. You, O Devī, are that blessed and supreme Knowledge.

‘Make you their practice’ turns the passive अभ्यस्यसे (‘you are practised’) active.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAfter the ritual words of 4.8 the hymn names the Goddess as the goal of contemplative practice: the knowledge (vidyā) that liberates, pursued by disciplined sages.

Traditional interpretationThe stanza echoes Medhas’s teaching in chapter 1: सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता सनातनी (1.57), and the gods’ hymn in chapter 11 calls her मुक्तिहेतुः again (11.5). The same Goddess who binds as Mahāmāyā (1.55) is the knowledge that releases.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names the Goddess विद्या (name 549) and मुक्तिरूपिणी (name 737).

4.10Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

शब्दात्मिका सुविमलर्ग्यजुषां निधान-
मुद्गीथरम्यपदपाठवतां च साम्नाम्।
देवी त्रयी भगवती भवभावनाय
वार्ता च सर्वजगतां परमार्तिहन्त्री ॥ १० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शब्दात्मिका | सुविमलर्ग्यजुषाम् | निधानम्उद्गीथरम्यपदपाठवताम् | च | साम्नाम्देवी | त्रयी | भगवती | भवभावनायवार्ता | च | सर्वजगताम् | परमार्तिहन्त्री

3IAST

As recitedśabdātmikā suvimalargyajuṣāṃ nidhāna-
mudgītharamyapadapāṭhavatāṃ ca sāmnām |
devī trayī bhagavatī bhavabhāvanāya
vārtā ca sarvajagatāṃ paramārtihantrī ||

Separatedśabdātmikā | suvimalargyajuṣām | nidhānam
udgītharamyapadapāṭhavatām | ca | sāmnām
devī | trayī | bhagavatī | bhavabhāvanāya
vārtā | ca | sarvajagatām | paramārtihantrī

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शब्दात्मिका śabdātmikā → शब्द + आत्मिका
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā. Likewise परमार्ति (परम + आर्ति).
  • सुविमलर्ग्यजुषां suvimalargyajuṣāṃ → सुविमल + ऋक् + यजुषाम्
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) and consonant sandhi (voicing) inside a compound · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    a + ṛ → ar; the stem ऋच् ends in k when it stands before another member, and the k is voiced to g before y. The final m becomes anusvāra before n (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • निधानमुद्गीथ nidhānamudgītha → निधानम् + उद्गीथ…
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The line breaks between निधान- and मुद्गीथ…; निधानम् belongs to the first line and its m joins the vowel of the next word.
  • उद्गीथ udgītha → उत् + गीथ
    consonant sandhi with a prefix (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of उत् is voiced to d before g.
  • पाठवतां च pāṭhavatāṃ ca → पाठवताम् + च
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; likewise सर्वजगतां परमार्ति…

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शब्दात्मिका śabdātmikā — bahuvrīhi (feminine -इका from आत्मक)
    Members: शब्द + आत्मन् (+ -क) · Vigraha: शब्दः आत्मा यस्याः सा śabdaḥ ātmā yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose self is sound”; in the verse: the Goddess as the Word, the substance of the Veda
  • सुविमलर्ग्यजुषाम् suvimalargyajuṣām — karmadhāraya containing an itaretara dvandva (ऋग्यजुस्); सुविमल is a prādi compound (सुष्ठु विमलम्)
    Members: सुविमल + ऋच् + यजुस् · Vigraha: ऋचः च यजूंषि च ऋग्यजूंषि; सुविमलानि ऋग्यजूंषि, तेषाम् ṛcaḥ ca yajūṃṣi ca ṛgyajūṃṣi; suvimalāni ṛgyajūṃṣi, teṣām
    Literally “of the very pure Ṛc-verses and Yajus-formulas”; in the verse: of the Ṛgveda and the Yajurveda
  • उद्गीथरम्यपदपाठवताम् udgītharamyapadapāṭhavatām — possessive (matup) on a karmadhāraya that contains tatpuruṣas (tṛtīyā उद्गीथरम्य, ṣaṣṭhī पदपाठ)
    Members: उद्गीथ + रम्य + पद + पाठ + -वत् · Vigraha: पदानां पाठः पदपाठः; उद्गीथेन रम्यः पदपाठः उद्गीथरम्यपदपाठः; सः एषाम् अस्ति इति उद्गीथरम्यपदपाठवन्ति, तेषाम् padānāṃ pāṭhaḥ padapāṭhaḥ; udgīthena ramyaḥ padapāṭhaḥ udgītharamyapadapāṭhaḥ; saḥ eṣām asti iti udgītharamyapadapāṭhavanti, teṣām
    Literally “possessing a recitation of words delightful with the udgītha”; in the verse: the Sāman chants, sung with the udgītha
  • भवभावनाय bhavabhāvanāya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: भव + भावन · Vigraha: भवस्य भावनम्, तस्मै bhavasya bhāvanam, tasmai
    Literally “for the causing-to-be of the world”; in the verse: for the world’s coming into being and welfare
  • सर्वजगताम् sarvajagatām — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + जगत् · Vigraha: सर्वाणि जगन्ति, तेषाम् sarvāṇi jaganti, teṣām
    Literally “of all worlds”; in the verse: of every world
  • परमार्तिहन्त्री paramārtihantrī — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (परमार्ति)
    Members: परम + आर्ति + हन्त्री · Vigraha: परमा आर्तिः परमार्तिः; परमार्तेः हन्त्री paramā ārtiḥ paramārtiḥ; paramārteḥ hantrī
    Literally “slayer of the supreme pain”; in the verse: she who ends the worst distress
    Alternative: परम may also qualify the whole (‘the supreme destroyer of distress’); the karmadhāraya परमा आर्तिः is the natural reading.

C. Morphology

  • साम्नाम् sāmnām — Genitive plural of the n-stem सामन्: the a of -man is dropped before the ending (साम्नाम्).
  • परमार्तिहन्त्री paramārtihantrī — हन्तृ is the agent noun of √हन् (kṛt tṛc); its feminine takes ṅīp: हन्त्री ‘she who slays’. In the compound the outside genitive सर्वजगताम् goes with आर्ति: ‘the distress of all worlds’.
  • उद्गीथरम्यपदपाठवताम् udgītharamyapadapāṭhavatām — Genitive plural of a possessive stem in -वत् (matup): -वताम्. It agrees with साम्नाम्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(त्वं) शब्दात्मिका सुविमलर्ग्यजुषाम् उद्गीथरम्यपदपाठवतां साम्नां च निधानम् (असि)। भगवती देवी (त्वं) भवभावनाय त्रयी (असि), सर्वजगतां परमार्तिहन्त्री वार्ता च (असि)।

(tvaṃ) śabdātmikā suvimalargyajuṣām udgītharamyapadapāṭhavatāṃ sāmnāṃ ca nidhānam (asi) | bhagavatī devī (tvaṃ) bhavabhāvanāya trayī (asi), sarvajagatāṃ paramārtihantrī vārtā ca (asi) |

Nominal sentences with the verb ‘are’ understood. भवभावनाय is taken with त्रयी (the Veda for the world’s sustenance); it may also go with both त्रयी and वार्ता.

6Translation

LiteralHaving sound as your self, you are the repository of the very pure Ṛk and Yajus and of the Sāmans, which possess a recitation of words delightful with the udgītha. You, the Blessed Devī, are the Triple (Veda) for the bringing-forth of the world, and Vārttā, the destroyer of the supreme distress of all the worlds.

ReadableYour very self is sound: you are the treasure-house of the spotless Ṛk and Yajus and of the Sāman chants, lovely in their words with the udgītha. Blessed Devī, you are the Triple Veda, for the sustaining of the world, and you are Vārttā, the livelihood that ends the deepest distress of all the worlds.

Vārttā, a technical name, is glossed as ‘livelihood’ (agriculture, cattle and trade); ‘sustaining’ interprets भावन ‘causing to be’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is the Veda as sound: the Ṛk, the Yajus and the sung Sāman, whose central chant is the udgītha. She is also Vārttā, the knowledge by which people earn their living: the sacred and the practical are both her forms.

Traditional interpretationTrayī (the threefold Veda) and Vārttā (agriculture, cattle-rearing and trade) are two of the four branches of knowledge in the classical list known from the Arthaśāstra, with ānvīkṣikī (reasoning) and daṇḍanīti (government). Brahmā’s hymn had called her स्वरात्मिका, ‘whose self is the sounded syllable’ (1.73); here she is शब्दात्मिका.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess त्रयी (name 872).

4.11Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

मेधासि देवि विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारा
दुर्गासि दुर्गभवसागरनौरसङ्गा।
श्रीः कैटभारिहृदयैककृताधिवासा
गौरी त्वमेव शशिमौलिकृतप्रतिष्ठा ॥ ११ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मेधा | असि | देवि | विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारादुर्गा | असि | दुर्गभवसागरनौः | असङ्गाश्रीः | कैटभारिहृदयैककृताधिवासागौरी | त्वम् | एव | शशिमौलिकृतप्रतिष्ठा

3IAST

As recitedmedhāsi devi viditākhilaśāstrasārā
durgāsi durgabhavasāgaranaurasaṅgā |
śrīḥ kaiṭabhārihṛdayaikakṛtādhivāsā
gaurī tvameva śaśimaulikṛtapratiṣṭhā ||

Separatedmedhā | asi | devi | viditākhilaśāstrasārā
durgā | asi | durgabhavasāgaranauḥ | asaṅgā
śrīḥ | kaiṭabhārihṛdayaikakṛtādhivāsā
gaurī | tvam | eva | śaśimaulikṛtapratiṣṭhā

विदिताखिलशास्त्रसाराviditākhilaśāstrasārāby whom the essence of all the śāstras is knownf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मेधा
दुर्गाdurgāDurgā, the Hard-to-reachf. nom. sg. of दुर्गा; predicate
असिasiyou are√अस्, laṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
दुर्गभवसागरनौःdurgabhavasāgaranauḥthe boat across the hard-to-cross ocean of worldly existencef. nom. sg. of the au-stem नौ at the end of a compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to दुर्गा
असङ्गाasaṅgāunattachedf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
श्रीःśrīḥŚrī (Lakṣmī)f. nom. sg. of श्री; predicate
कैटभारिहृदयैककृताधिवासाkaiṭabhārihṛdayaikakṛtādhivāsāwho has made her dwelling in the heart of Kaiṭabha’s foe alonef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies श्रीः
गौरीgaurīGaurī, the Fair One (Pārvatī)f. nom. sg. of गौरी; predicate
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
एवevaindeed, you aloneindeclinable (restrictive particle)
शशिमौलिकृतप्रतिष्ठाśaśimaulikṛtapratiṣṭhāwho has her place established with the moon-crested one (Śiva)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies गौरी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मेधासि medhāsi → मेधा + असि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā; the same junction gives दुर्गासि.
  • विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारा viditākhilaśāstrasārā → विदित + अखिल + शास्त्र + सारा
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā. Likewise कैटभारि (कैटभ + अरि) and कृताधिवासा (कृत + अधिवासा).
  • नौरसङ्गा naurasaṅgā → नौः + असङ्गा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After au the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel.
  • हृदयैक hṛdayaika → हृदय + एक
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai.
  • त्वमेव tvameva → त्वम् + एव
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m is written together with the following vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारा viditākhilaśāstrasārā — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (…शास्त्रसार) and a karmadhāraya (अखिलशास्त्र)
    Members: विदित + अखिल + शास्त्र + सार · Vigraha: अखिलानि शास्त्राणि अखिलशास्त्राणि; तेषां सारः अखिलशास्त्रसारः; विदितः अखिलशास्त्रसारः यया सा akhilāni śāstrāṇi akhilaśāstrāṇi; teṣāṃ sāraḥ akhilaśāstrasāraḥ; viditaḥ akhilaśāstrasāraḥ yayā sā
    Literally “she by whom the essence of all treatises is known”; in the verse: the intelligence that has grasped the heart of all learning
  • दुर्गभवसागरनौः durgabhavasāgaranauḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing karmadhārayas (दुर्गभवसागर; भवसागर, a rūpaka: the world as an ocean)
    Members: दुर्ग + भवसागर + नौ · Vigraha: भवः एव सागरः भवसागरः; दुर्गः भवसागरः दुर्गभवसागरः; तस्य नौः bhavaḥ eva sāgaraḥ bhavasāgaraḥ; durgaḥ bhavasāgaraḥ durgabhavasāgaraḥ; tasya nauḥ
    Literally “the boat of the hard-to-cross ocean of becoming”; in the verse: the boat that carries one across saṃsāra
  • असङ्गा asaṅgā — nañ-bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ + सङ्ग · Vigraha: न विद्यते सङ्गः यस्याः सा na vidyate saṅgaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she who has no attachment”; in the verse: untouched by what she carries across
  • कैटभारिहृदयैककृताधिवासा kaiṭabhārihṛdayaikakṛtādhivāsā — bahuvrīhi containing a karmadhāraya (कृताधिवास) and ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas (कैटभारि, कैटभारिहृदय)
    Members: कैटभारि + हृदय + एक + कृत + अधिवास · Vigraha: कैटभस्य अरिः कैटभारिः; कैटभारेः हृदयं कैटभारिहृदयम्; तद् एव एकम् (स्थानम्) कैटभारिहृदयैकम्; तस्मिन् कृतः अधिवासः यया सा kaiṭabhasya ariḥ kaiṭabhāriḥ; kaiṭabhāreḥ hṛdayaṃ kaiṭabhārihṛdayam; tad eva ekam (sthānam) kaiṭabhārihṛdayaikam; tasmin kṛtaḥ adhivāsaḥ yayā sā
    Literally “she by whom a dwelling has been made in the one (place), the heart of Kaiṭabha’s foe”; in the verse: Lakṣmī, who lives in Viṣṇu’s heart alone
  • शशिमौलिकृतप्रतिष्ठा śaśimaulikṛtapratiṣṭhā — bahuvrīhi containing a vyadhikaraṇa bahuvrīhi (शशिमौलि)
    Members: शशिमौलि + कृत + प्रतिष्ठा · Vigraha: शशी मौलौ यस्य सः शशिमौलिः; शशिमौलौ कृता प्रतिष्ठा यया सा śaśī maulau yasya saḥ śaśimauliḥ; śaśimaulau kṛtā pratiṣṭhā yayā sā
    Literally “she by whom a standing-place has been made in the moon-crested one”; in the verse: Gaurī, established as Śiva’s consort
    Alternative: Also read with शशिमौलि as agent, शशिमौलिना कृता प्रतिष्ठा यस्याः सा, ‘she who has been given her place (honoured) by Śiva’. The parallel with कृताधिवासा in the line before favours the first.

C. Morphology

  • दुर्गभवसागरनौः durgabhavasāgaranauḥ — नौ ‘boat’ is an au-stem: nominative singular नौः, which keeps its form at the end of the compound. Before the vowel of असङ्गा its visarga is sounded r.
  • दुर्गा durgā — A play on words: दुर्गा ‘the hard-to-reach’ is the boat across the दुर्ग, the hard-to-cross, ocean of existence. दुर्ग is दुर् + √गम् (‘hard to go’).
  • असि asi — Present second singular of √अस्: the root’s final s is dropped before the ending -सि (अस् + सि → असि). Repeated in the first two lines, understood in the last two.

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवि, (त्वं) विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारा मेधा असि; असङ्गा दुर्गभवसागरनौः दुर्गा असि; कैटभारिहृदयैककृताधिवासा श्रीः (असि); शशिमौलिकृतप्रतिष्ठा गौरी (च) त्वम् एव (असि)।

devi, (tvaṃ) viditākhilaśāstrasārā medhā asi; asaṅgā durgabhavasāgaranauḥ durgā asi; kaiṭabhārihṛdayaikakṛtādhivāsā śrīḥ (asi); śaśimaulikṛtapratiṣṭhā gaurī (ca) tvam eva (asi) |

Four identifications, each name with its epithet: Medhā, Durgā, Śrī, Gaurī. त्वम् एव in the last line sums them up: you alone are all of them.

6Translation

LiteralYou are Medhā, O Devī, by whom the essence of all the śāstras is known; you are Durgā, the boat across the hard-to-cross ocean of existence, unattached; Śrī, who has made her dwelling in the heart of Kaiṭabha’s foe alone; you alone are Gaurī, who has made her place in the moon-crested one.

ReadableO Devī, you are Medhā, who knows the essence of every scripture; you are Durgā, the boat across the perilous ocean of worldly life, yourself untouched by attachment. You are Śrī, whose one dwelling is the heart of Viṣṇu, Kaiṭabha’s foe; and you alone are Gaurī, established with the moon-crowned Śiva.

Kaiṭabha’s foe and the moon-crested one are named as Viṣṇu and Śiva.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningFour goddesses are named — Medhā (learning), Durgā, Śrī (Lakṣmī, consort of Viṣṇu, who killed Kaiṭabha in chapter 1) and Gaurī (consort of Śiva) — and the last line says that the Devī alone is all of them. The word-play दुर्गा / दुर्ग makes Durgā the boat across the ocean that is hard to cross.

Traditional interpretationThe Devī herself later gives another account of the name Durgā: she will slay the asura Durgama and be known as Durgā (11.50). The stanza is often read as a statement of the Śākta teaching that the consorts of the gods are forms of the one Goddess.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has three of these names: मेधा (name 538), दुर्गा (name 190) and गौरी (name 635).

4.12Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

ईषत्सहासममलं परिपूर्णचन्द्र-
बिम्बानुकारि कनकोत्तमकान्तिकान्तम्।
अत्यद्भुतं प्रहृतमात्तरुषा तथापि
वक्त्रं विलोक्य सहसा महिषासुरेण ॥ १२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ईषत्सहासम् | अमलम् | परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बानुकारिकनकोत्तमकान्तिकान्तम्अत्यद्भुतम् | प्रहृतम् | आत्तरुषा | तथा | अपिवक्त्रम् | विलोक्य | सहसा | महिषासुरेण

3IAST

As recitedīṣatsahāsamamalaṃ paripūrṇacandra-
bimbānukāri kanakottamakāntikāntam |
atyadbhutaṃ prahṛtamāttaruṣā tathāpi
vaktraṃ vilokya sahasā mahiṣāsureṇa ||

Separatedīṣatsahāsam | amalam | paripūrṇacandrabimbānukāri
kanakottamakāntikāntam
atyadbhutam | prahṛtam | āttaruṣā | tathā | api
vaktram | vilokya | sahasā | mahiṣāsureṇa

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ईषत्सहासममलं īṣatsahāsamamalaṃ → ईषत्सहासम् + अमलम्
    final m before a vowel and before a consonant · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    ईषत्सहासम् keeps its m before the vowel of अमलम्; अमलम् shows anusvāra before p.
  • परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बानुकारि paripūrṇacandrabimbānukāri → परिपूर्ण + चन्द्र + बिम्ब + अनुकारि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā. The compound runs over the line-end (…चन्द्र- / बिम्बानुकारि) and belongs to the first line.
  • कनकोत्तम kanakottama → कनक + उत्तम
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • अत्यद्भुतं प्रहृतमात्तरुषा atyadbhutaṃ prahṛtamāttaruṣā → अति + अद्भुतम् + प्रहृतम् + आत्तरुषा
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) inside a compound; final m · इको यणचि (6.1.77); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The i of अति becomes y before a; अत्यद्भुतम् shows anusvāra before p; प्रहृतम् keeps its m before the vowel ā.
  • तथापि tathāpi → तथा + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • महिषासुरेण mahiṣāsureṇa → महिष + असुरेन
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha); retroflexion · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā; the n of the ending -ena becomes ṇ after r and ṣ in the same word with no dental or palatal between (ṇatva).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • ईषत्सहासम् īṣatsahāsam — saha-bahuvrīhi (सह → स) with the adverb ईषत्
    Members: ईषत् + स + हास · Vigraha: ईषत् हासेन सह वर्तते इति (तत्) īṣat hāsena saha vartate iti (tat)
    Literally “being with a slight laugh”; in the verse: softly smiling
  • अमलम् amalam — nañ-bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ + मल · Vigraha: न विद्यते मलं यस्य तत् na vidyate malaṃ yasya tat
    Literally “having no stain”; in the verse: spotless, pure
  • परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बानुकारि paripūrṇacandrabimbānukāri — upapada tatpuruṣa (agent noun अनुकारिन्) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa and a karmadhāraya (परिपूर्णचन्द्र)
    Members: परिपूर्णचन्द्र + बिम्ब + अनुकारिन् · Vigraha: परिपूर्णः चन्द्रः परिपूर्णचन्द्रः; तस्य बिम्बं परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बम्; तत् अनुकरोति इति paripūrṇaḥ candraḥ paripūrṇacandraḥ; tasya bimbaṃ paripūrṇacandrabimbam; tat anukaroti iti
    Literally “imitating the disc of the full moon”; in the verse: round and radiant as the full moon
  • कनकोत्तमकान्तिकान्तम् kanakottamakāntikāntam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa and a saptamī tatpuruṣa (कनकोत्तम)
    Members: कनकोत्तम + कान्ति + कान्त · Vigraha: कनकेषु उत्तमं कनकोत्तमम्; कनकोत्तमस्य कान्तिः कनकोत्तमकान्तिः; तया कान्तम् kanakeṣu uttamaṃ kanakottamam; kanakottamasya kāntiḥ kanakottamakāntiḥ; tayā kāntam
    Literally “lovely with the lustre of the best among golds”; in the verse: glowing like the finest gold
    Alternative: कनकोत्तम may also be taken as a karmadhāraya with the qualifier placed second, उत्तमं कनकम्; the sense is the same.
  • अत्यद्भुतम् atyadbhutam — prādi tatpuruṣa
    Members: अति + अद्भुत · Vigraha: अतिशयेन अद्भुतम् atiśayena adbhutam
    Literally “excessively wonderful”; in the verse: a great marvel
  • आत्तरुषा āttaruṣā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: आत्त + रुष् · Vigraha: आत्ता रुट् येन सः, तेन āttā ruṭ yena saḥ, tena
    Literally “by one by whom wrath was seized”; in the verse: in a fury
  • महिषासुरेण mahiṣāsureṇa — karmadhāraya
    Members: महिष + असुर · Vigraha: महिषः च असौ असुरः च, तेन mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca, tena
    Literally “by the buffalo who is an asura”; in the verse: by the buffalo-demon

C. Morphology

  • प्रहृतम् prahṛtam — An impersonal (bhāve) past participle: neuter singular with the agent in the instrumental, ‘it was struck by Mahiṣāsura’ = ‘Mahiṣāsura struck’. The fact itself is the subject of अत्यद्भुतम्.
  • विलोक्य vilokya — Absolutive in -य (lyap), used because the root has a prefix (वि + √लोक्): ‘having seen’.
  • आत्तरुषा āttaruṣā — आत्त is the past participle of आ + √दा ‘to take’ (आ + दत्त → आत्त); रुष् ‘anger’ is a feminine consonant stem whose instrumental is रुषा, and the bahuvrīhi आत्तरुष् declines the same way.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(देवि,) ईषत्सहासम् अमलं परिपूर्णचन्द्रबिम्बानुकारि कनकोत्तमकान्तिकान्तं (तव) वक्त्रं विलोक्य तथा अपि आत्तरुषा महिषासुरेण सहसा प्रहृतम् (इति यत्, तत्) अत्यद्भुतम्।

(devi,) īṣatsahāsam amalaṃ paripūrṇacandrabimbānukāri kanakottamakāntikāntaṃ (tava) vaktraṃ vilokya tathā api āttaruṣā mahiṣāsureṇa sahasā prahṛtam (iti yat, tat) atyadbhutam |

The statement ‘Mahiṣāsura, having seen your face, struck’ (an impersonal passive with the agent in the instrumental) is the subject of the predicate अत्यद्भुतम्. The stanza is answered by 4.13 (‘but that, seeing it angry, he did not die at once — that is more wonderful’).

6Translation

LiteralHaving looked at (your) face — gently smiling, spotless, resembling the orb of the full moon, lovely with the lustre of the finest gold — that even so a blow was suddenly struck by Mahiṣāsura, seized with wrath, (is) exceedingly wonderful.

ReadableYour face was gently smiling and spotless, like the orb of the full moon, lovely with the glow of finest gold; that Mahiṣāsura could look on it and even so, seized by fury, strike out at once — that is a great wonder.

‘Your’ is supplied (the face is the Devī’s); the impersonal passive is turned active.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn turns to the battle of chapter 3 and to the Devī’s face. Its beauty — smiling, pure, moon-like, golden — should have disarmed the asura; that he could see it and still strike is the first wonder. The second, greater wonder follows in 4.13: that seeing the same face in anger he did not die on the spot.

Traditional interpretationThe pair 4.12–4.13 sets the Devī’s gentle (saumya) and terrible (raudra) aspects side by side: the same face is lovely and dreadful. The gods’ astonishment is also a judgement on Mahiṣa’s blindness: the asura sees beauty and answers it with violence.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the stanza asks what it takes to look at goodness and still choose to attack it. Anger (रुष्) is named as what blinds.

4.13Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

दृष्ट्वा तु देवि कुपितं भ्रुकुटीकराल-
मुद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि यन्न सद्यः।
प्राणान्मुमोच महिषस्तदतीव चित्रं
कैर्जीव्यते हि कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन ॥ १३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दृष्ट्वा | तु | देवि | कुपितम् | भ्रुकुटीकरालम्उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि | यत् | न | सद्यःप्राणान् | मुमोच | महिषः | तत् | अतीव | चित्रम्कैः | जीव्यते | हि | कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन

3IAST

As reciteddṛṣṭvā tu devi kupitaṃ bhrukuṭīkarāla-
mudyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi yanna sadyaḥ |
prāṇānmumoca mahiṣastadatīva citraṃ
kairjīvyate hi kupitāntakadarśanena ||

Separateddṛṣṭvā | tu | devi | kupitam | bhrukuṭīkarālam
udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi | yat | na | sadyaḥ
prāṇān | mumoca | mahiṣaḥ | tat | atīva | citram
kaiḥ | jīvyate | hi | kupitāntakadarśanena
