5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • उद्यच्छशाङ्क udyacchaśāṅka → उद्यत् + शशाङ्क
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (palatal assimilation; ś → ch) · स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)
    The t of उद्यत् becomes c beside the palatal ś, and the ś after the stop becomes ch: उद्यत् + शशाङ्क → उद्यच्छशाङ्क.
  • सदृशच्छवि sadṛśacchavi → सदृश + छवि
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (doubling of ch) · छे च (6.1.73)
    After a short vowel, ch is doubled (written च्छ). Inside a compound the doubling is obligatory.
  • यन्न yanna → यत् + न
    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before the nasal n becomes n.
  • महिषस्तदतीव mahiṣastadatīva → महिषः + तत् + अतीव
    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (voicing) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the t of तत् is voiced to d before the vowel of अतीव.
  • कैर्जीव्यते kairjīvyate → कैः + जीव्यते
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The final s of कैः becomes ru; after a vowel other than a/ā it stands as r before a voiced sound (here j).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भ्रुकुटीकरालम् bhrukuṭīkarālam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: भ्रुकुटी + कराल · Vigraha: भ्रुकुट्या करालम् bhrukuṭyā karālam
    Literally “dreadful through the frown”; in the verse: a face made terrible by knitted brows
  • उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi — bahuvrīhi, containing a karmadhāraya (उद्यच्छशाङ्क) and a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशी)
    Members: उद्यत् + शशाङ्क + सदृश + छवि · Vigraha: उद्यन् शशाङ्कः उद्यच्छशाङ्कः; उद्यच्छशाङ्केन सदृशी छविः यस्य तत् udyan śaśāṅkaḥ udyacchaśāṅkaḥ; udyacchaśāṅkena sadṛśī chaviḥ yasya tat
    Literally “whose hue is like the rising hare-marked (moon)”; in the verse: flushed red, as the moon is when it rises: the colour of anger, kept in the moon-image of 4.12
    Alternative: शशाङ्क is itself a bahuvrīhi (शशः अङ्के यस्य सः, ‘the one with a hare on its lap’), the moon.
  • कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन kupitāntakadarśanena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (कुपितान्तक)
    Members: कुपित + अन्तक + दर्शन · Vigraha: कुपितः अन्तकः कुपितान्तकः; कुपितान्तकस्य दर्शनम्, तेन kupitaḥ antakaḥ kupitāntakaḥ; kupitāntakasya darśanam, tena
    Literally “by the sight of the angry Ender”; in the verse: after looking on Death himself in wrath

C. Morphology

  • मुमोच mumoca — Perfect of √मुच्: reduplication मु- and guṇa of the root vowel in the singular (मोच्). With प्राणान्: ‘gave up his life’.
  • जीव्यते jīvyate — Impersonal passive (bhāve prayoga) of the intransitive √जीव्: there is no grammatical subject, and the one who lives is put in the instrumental (कैः). The question is rhetorical: no one lives.
  • उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi — A bahuvrīhi takes the gender of what it qualifies. Ending in the feminine छवि, it becomes a neuter i-stem to agree with वक्त्रम्; its acc. sg. is the bare stem.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) देवि, कुपितं भ्रुकुटीकरालम् उद्यच्छशाङ्कसदृशच्छवि (तव वक्त्रं) दृष्ट्वा तु महिषः यत् सद्यः प्राणान् न मुमोच, तत् अतीव चित्रम्। हि कुपितान्तकदर्शनेन कैः जीव्यते?

(he) devi, kupitaṃ bhrukuṭīkarālam udyacchaśāṅkasadṛśacchavi (tava vaktraṃ) dṛṣṭvā tu mahiṣaḥ yat sadyaḥ prāṇān na mumoca, tat atīva citram | hi kupitāntakadarśanena kaiḥ jīvyate?

The face of 4.12 (वक्त्रम्) is the understood object of दृष्ट्वा. The यत्-clause is the subject that तत् resumes: ‘that he did not die — that is most strange’. The last line is a rhetorical question in the impersonal passive.

6Translation

LiteralBut having seen (your face), O Devī, angry, dreadful with its frown, its hue like the rising moon — that Mahiṣa did not at once give up his life, that is exceedingly strange; for by whom is it lived after the sight of Death enraged?

ReadableBut, O Devī, Mahiṣa saw your face in anger — dreadful with its frown, flushed like the rising moon — and did not die on the spot: that is the stranger wonder. For who can live once he has looked on Death enraged?

‘Your face’ is supplied from वक्त्रम् in 4.12. ‘The stranger wonder’ renders अतीव चित्रम् ‘exceedingly strange’, which answers the अत्यद्भुतम् of 4.12.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods continue the paradox of 4.12. That Mahiṣa could strike the gentle, smiling face was strange; that he survived the sight of the same face in anger is stranger still, for the angry Goddess is Death (Antaka) himself.

Traditional interpretationThe two verses are read as a pair: the Goddess is seen first as gentle and then as terrible, the two kinds of form that 4.26 names together (सौम्यानि … अत्यर्थघोराणि). The rising moon is red, so the comparison gives the colour of anger while keeping the moon of 4.12.

4.14Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

देवि प्रसीद परमा भवती भवाय
सद्यो विनाशयसि कोपवती कुलानि।
विज्ञातमेतदधुनैव यदस्तमेत-
न्नीतं बलं सुविपुलं महिषासुरस्य ॥ १४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवि | प्रसीद | परमा | भवती | भवायसद्यः | विनाशयसि | कोपवती | कुलानिविज्ञातम् | एतत् | अधुना | एव | यत् | अस्तम् | एतत्नीतम् | बलम् | सुविपुलम् | महिषासुरस्य

3IAST

As reciteddevi prasīda paramā bhavatī bhavāya
sadyo vināśayasi kopavatī kulāni |
vijñātametadadhunaiva yadastameta-
nnītaṃ balaṃ suvipulaṃ mahiṣāsurasya ||

Separateddevi | prasīda | paramā | bhavatī | bhavāya
sadyaḥ | vināśayasi | kopavatī | kulāni
vijñātam | etat | adhunā | eva | yat | astam | etat
nītam | balam | suvipulam | mahiṣāsurasya

देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi 1P ‘to sit, settle’; present stem सीद-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सद्यो विनाशयसि sadyo vināśayasi → सद्यः + विनाशयसि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.
  • अधुनैव adhunaiva → अधुना + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • यदस्तमेत yadastameta → यत् + अस्तम् + एतत्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel; the m of अस्तम् simply joins the next vowel.
  • एतन्नीतं etannītaṃ → एतत् + नीतम्
    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) across the line break · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    t before n becomes n. The word is broken across the printed lines (एत- / न्नीतं) but recited as one junction; एतत् belongs to the third line, where it begins.
  • महिषासुरस्य mahiṣāsurasya → महिष + असुरस्य
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सुविपुलम् suvipulam — tatpuruṣa of the prādi kind (the prefix सु ‘well, very’ with an adjective)
    Members: सु + विपुल · Vigraha: सुष्ठु विपुलम् suṣṭhu vipulam
    Literally “well-vast”; in the verse: immense
  • महिषासुरस्य mahiṣāsurasya — karmadhāraya
    Members: महिष + असुर · Vigraha: महिषः च असौ असुरः च, तस्य mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca, tasya
    Literally “of the asura who is Mahiṣa”; in the verse: of the buffalo-demon
    Alternative: Also analysed with the middle member dropped: महिषरूपः असुरः, ‘the asura in buffalo form’. Since महिष is his name in 4.13 and throughout, the plain appositional reading fits.

C. Morphology

  • प्रसीद prasīda — √सद् takes the stem सीद- in the present system: प्र-सीद ‘settle down, become clear’, and so ‘be pleased, be gracious’.
  • विनाशयसि vināśayasi — Causative of √नश् ‘perish’: the root vowel is lengthened (नाश्) and -अय- added — ‘cause to perish’, destroy.
  • नीतम् nītam — With अस्तम् this is an idiom: अस्तं नी ‘lead to the setting (as of the sun)’, i.e. bring to an end. The agent, ‘by you’, is understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) देवि, प्रसीद। परमा भवती भवाय (भवति); कोपवती (सती त्वं) सद्यः कुलानि विनाशयसि। यत् एतत् सुविपुलं महिषासुरस्य बलम् अस्तं नीतम्, एतत् अधुना एव विज्ञातम्।

(he) devi, prasīda | paramā bhavatī bhavāya (bhavati); kopavatī (satī tvaṃ) sadyaḥ kulāni vināśayasi | yat etat suvipulaṃ mahiṣāsurasya balam astaṃ nītam, etat adhunā eva vijñātam |

Line 1 is a prayer followed by a nominal clause (‘you are for welfare’); line 2 states the opposite (‘angry, you destroy’). In lines 3–4 the first एतत् ‘this’ is explained by the यत्-clause.

6Translation

LiteralO Devī, be gracious. You, supreme, are for (our) welfare; angry, you at once destroy whole families. This has been known just now, that this very vast army of Mahiṣāsura has been brought to its end.

ReadableBe gracious, O Devī! Supreme, you are there for our flourishing; in anger you destroy whole races in a moment. We have seen it just now: the vast army of Mahiṣāsura has been brought to nothing.

The contrast of line 1 with कोपवती ‘when angry’ is left implicit, as in the Sanskrit. ‘We have seen it’ renders the impersonal विज्ञातम् ‘it has been known’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA plea, and the lesson of the battle just ended. The same Goddess who is supreme for the world’s welfare destroys whole races when angered; the gods have just watched Mahiṣa’s army go down.

Traditional interpretationThe sound-play भवती / भवाय (‘you’ / ‘for welfare’) is in the Sanskrit. The verse is read as the reason for the prayer प्रसीद: the face of anger has done its work, and the gods ask her to turn to them the face of grace.

4.15Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

ते सम्मता जनपदेषु धनानि तेषां
तेषां यशांसि न च सीदति धर्मवर्गः।
धन्यास्त एव निभृतात्मजभृत्यदारा
येषां सदाभ्युदयदा भवती प्रसन्ना ॥ १५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

3IAST

As recitedte sammatā janapadeṣu dhanāni teṣāṃ

dhanyāsta eva nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārā
yeṣāṃ sadābhyudayadā bhavatī prasannā ||

प्रसन्नाprasannāgracious, pleasedpast passive participle (kta) of प्र + √सद्, f. nom. sg.; predicate (अस्ति understood)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सम्मता जनपदेषु sammatā janapadeṣu → सम्मताः + जनपदेषु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, and the y is dropped before the consonant; the long ā remains.
  • तेषां तेषां teṣāṃ teṣāṃ → तेषाम् + तेषाम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra (here also at the line’s end, as printed).
  • धन्यास्त एव dhanyāsta eva → धन्याः + ते + एव
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (ayādi with loss of y) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    Visarga before t becomes s. Then -e before e becomes -ay, the y is dropped, and the vowels stand apart: त एव.
  • सदाभ्युदयदा sadābhyudayadā → सदा + अभ्युदयदा
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • धर्मवर्गः dharmavargaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: धर्म + वर्ग · Vigraha: धर्मस्य वर्गः dharmasya vargaḥ
    Literally “the group of dharma”; in the verse: their righteous life as a whole — duties, rites and conduct
    Alternative: वर्ग may point to the trivarga (dharma, artha, kāma) with dharma at its head; the plain sense, that their dharma never falters, is the same.
  • निभृतात्मजभृत्यदाराः nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (आत्मजभृत्यदाराः)
    Members: निभृत + आत्मज + भृत्य + दार · Vigraha: आत्मजाः च भृत्याः च दाराः च आत्मजभृत्यदाराः; निभृताः आत्मजभृत्यदाराः येषां ते ātmajāḥ ca bhṛtyāḥ ca dārāḥ ca ātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ; nibhṛtāḥ ātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “whose sons, servants and wives are steady (devoted)”; in the verse: men whose households are loyal and at peace
    Alternative: आत्मज is itself an upapada tatpuruṣa (आत्मनः जायते इति, ‘born of oneself’), a son. दार ‘wife’ is masculine and used in the plural.
  • अभ्युदयदा abhyudayadā — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: अभ्युदय + दा · Vigraha: अभ्युदयं ददाति इति abhyudayaṃ dadāti iti
    Literally “prosperity-giving”; in the verse: she who grants worldly flourishing

C. Morphology

  • सम्मताः sammatāḥ — Past participle of सम् + √मन्: the root’s nasal drops before -त (मन् + त → मत). ‘Thought well of’, honoured.
  • सीदति sīdati — Present of √सद् ‘sit, sink’ (stem सीद-). The same root with प्र gives प्रसन्ना in line 4: their dharma does not sink, because she is ‘settled’ in grace towards them.
  • प्रसन्ना prasannā — Past participle of प्र + √सद्: the d of the root and the t of the suffix both become n (सद् + त → सन्न). With the genitive येषाम्: ‘gracious to whom’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

येषां (प्रति) अभ्युदयदा भवती सदा प्रसन्ना (भवति), ते जनपदेषु सम्मताः, तेषां धनानि, तेषां यशांसि, (तेषां) धर्मवर्गः च न सीदति; निभृतात्मजभृत्यदाराः ते एव धन्याः।

yeṣāṃ (prati) abhyudayadā bhavatī sadā prasannā (bhavati), te janapadeṣu sammatāḥ, teṣāṃ dhanāni, teṣāṃ yaśāṃsi, (teṣāṃ) dharmavargaḥ ca na sīdati; nibhṛtātmajabhṛtyadārāḥ te eva dhanyāḥ |

The relative clause of line 4 (येषां … प्रसन्ना) defines the ते of lines 1 and 3. The main clauses are nominal (‘theirs are riches’) except सीदति.

6Translation

LiteralThey are honoured in the lands; theirs are riches, theirs are glories, and their body of dharma does not sink; blessed indeed are they, whose sons, servants and wives are loyal — to whom you, giver of prosperity, are always gracious.

ReadableThose to whom you, the giver of prosperity, are always gracious are honoured in every land. Wealth is theirs, and fame, and their righteousness never fails. Blessed are they, and loyal their sons, their servants and their wives.

The relative clause of line 4 is placed first in English.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe fruits of her grace in this world are listed: honour, wealth, fame, a dharma that does not fail and a household at peace. All of them hang on the condition of the last line: that she is gracious (प्रसन्ना).

Traditional interpretationThe verse turns from the battle to the worshipper. Its blessings are the householder’s abhyudaya, worldly flourishing; the next verse carries them on to heaven, and the hymn’s closing requests (4.37) ask for the same wealth, family and prosperity for whoever recites it.

4.16Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

धर्म्याणि देवि सकलानि सदैव कर्मा-
ण्यत्यादृतः प्रतिदिनं सुकृती करोति।
स्वर्गं प्रयाति च ततो भवतीप्रसादा-
ल्लोकत्रयेऽपि फलदा ननु देवि तेन ॥ १६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

धर्म्याणि | देवि | सकलानि | सदा | एव | कर्माणिअत्यादृतः | प्रतिदिनम् | सुकृती | करोतिस्वर्गम् | प्रयाति | च | ततः | भवतीप्रसादात्लोकत्रये | अपि | फलदा | ननु | देवि | तेन

3IAST

As reciteddharmyāṇi devi sakalāni sadaiva karmā-
ṇyatyādṛtaḥ pratidinaṃ sukṛtī karoti |
svargaṃ prayāti ca tato bhavatīprasādā-
llokatraye'pi phaladā nanu devi tena ||

Separateddharmyāṇi | devi | sakalāni | sadā | eva | karmāṇi
atyādṛtaḥ | pratidinam | sukṛtī | karoti
svargam | prayāti | ca | tataḥ | bhavatīprasādāt
lokatraye | api | phaladā | nanu | devi | tena

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सदैव sadaiva → सदा + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • कर्माण्यत्यादृतः karmāṇyatyādṛtaḥ → कर्माणि + अत्यादृतः
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) across the line break · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before a vowel becomes y. The word is broken across the printed lines (कर्मा- / ण्यत्यादृतः); कर्माणि belongs to the first line, where it begins. Inside अत्यादृतः the same rule joins अति + आ.
  • ततो भवती tato bhavatī → ततः + भवतीप्रसादात्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.
  • भवतीप्रसादाल्लोकत्रये bhavatīprasādāllokatraye → भवतीप्रसादात् + लोकत्रये
    consonant sandhi (t + l → ll) across the line break · तोर्लि (8.4.60)
    Final t before l becomes l. Printed भवतीप्रसादा- / ल्लोकत्रये.
  • लोकत्रयेऽपि lokatraye'pi → लोकत्रये + अपि
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रतिदिनम् pratidinam — avyayībhāva
    Members: प्रति + दिन · Vigraha: दिनं दिनं प्रति dinaṃ dinaṃ prati
    Literally “day by day”; in the verse: every day, without a break
  • भवतीप्रसादात् bhavatīprasādāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: भवती + प्रसाद · Vigraha: भवत्याः प्रसादः, तस्मात् bhavatyāḥ prasādaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from Your Ladyship’s grace”; in the verse: by your favour
  • लोकत्रये lokatraye — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: लोक + त्रय · Vigraha: लोकानां त्रयम्, तस्मिन् lokānāṃ trayam, tasmin
    Literally “in the triad of worlds”; in the verse: in earth, the middle region and heaven alike
  • फलदा phaladā — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: फल + दा · Vigraha: फलं ददाति इति phalaṃ dadāti iti
    Literally “fruit-giving”; in the verse: she who gives deeds their reward

C. Morphology

  • अत्यादृतः atyādṛtaḥ — Past participle of आ + √दृ ‘to heed’, here active in sense, as often with this verb: ‘one who has given heed’, careful, devoted. अति intensifies it.
  • सुकृती sukṛtī — Possessive -इन् on सुकृत ‘a good deed’: सुकृतिन्, ‘one who has good deeds’; nom. sg. सुकृती.
  • ननु nanu — A particle that asserts while inviting assent: ‘surely you are …, are you not?’

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) देवि, सुकृती अत्यादृतः (सन्) प्रतिदिनं सकलानि धर्म्याणि कर्माणि सदा एव करोति, ततः च भवतीप्रसादात् स्वर्गं प्रयाति। तेन (हे) देवि, (त्वं) लोकत्रये अपि फलदा ननु।

(he) devi, sukṛtī atyādṛtaḥ (san) pratidinaṃ sakalāni dharmyāṇi karmāṇi sadā eva karoti, tataḥ ca bhavatīprasādāt svargaṃ prayāti | tena (he) devi, (tvaṃ) lokatraye api phaladā nanu |

Two clauses with one subject (सुकृती): he does the deeds, and then reaches heaven through her grace. The conclusion (तेन … ननु) is a nominal sentence with ‘you’ understood.

6Translation

LiteralO Devī, the man of merit, with great care, every day always performs all righteous deeds; and then, through your grace, he goes to heaven. Therefore, O Devī, are you not the giver of fruit even in the three worlds?

ReadableO Devī, the virtuous man performs every righteous deed, day after day, with devoted care, and then by your grace he goes to heaven. So is it not you, O Devī, who give the fruit of action in all three worlds?

ननु is rendered as a question expecting ‘yes’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe good man’s deeds bear their fruit only through her grace (भवतीप्रसादात्). The gods conclude that she is the giver of reward in all three worlds, not in heaven alone.

Traditional interpretationRitual duty is not set aside: it is done daily and with care. But its fruit comes from her favour, and this is why, in the Śākta tradition, devotion is joined to duty rather than replacing it.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess स्वर्गापवर्गदा (name 764), ‘giver of heaven and of release’. The Devī Māhātmya speaks here of heaven and of fruit in the three worlds; release is the subject of 4.9 (मुक्तिहेतुः).

4.17Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

दुर्गे स्मृता हरसि भीतिमशेषजन्तोः
स्वस्थैः स्मृता मतिमतीव शुभां ददासि।
दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि का त्वदन्या
सर्वोपकारकरणाय सदाऽऽर्द्रचित्ता ॥ १७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दुर्गे | स्मृता | हरसि | भीतिम् | अशेषजन्तोःस्वस्थैः | स्मृता | मतिम् | अतीव | शुभाम् | ददासिदारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि | का | त्वदन्यासर्वोपकारकरणाय | सदा | आर्द्रचित्ता

3IAST

As reciteddurge smṛtā harasi bhītimaśeṣajantoḥ
svasthaiḥ smṛtā matimatīva śubhāṃ dadāsi |
dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi kā tvadanyā
sarvopakārakaraṇāya sadā''rdracittā ||

Separateddurge | smṛtā | harasi | bhītim | aśeṣajantoḥ
svasthaiḥ | smṛtā | matim | atīva | śubhām | dadāsi
dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi | kā | tvadanyā
sarvopakārakaraṇāya | sadā | ārdracittā

दुर्गेdurgeO Durgāf. voc. sg. of दुर्गा
स्मृताsmṛtā(when) remembered, called to mindpast passive participle (kta) of √स्मृ (bhvādi 1P ‘to remember’), f. nom. sg.; agrees with the understood ‘you’

भीतिम्bhītimfearf. acc. sg. of भीति; object of हरसि

स्मृताsmṛtā(when) rememberedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्मृ, f. nom. sg.
मतिम्matimunderstanding, a frame of mindf. acc. sg. of मति; object of ददासि

आर्द्रचित्ताārdracittātender-hearted (lit. ‘moist-minded’)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of का

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स्वस्थैः स्मृता svasthaiḥ smṛtā → स्वस्थैः + स्मृता
    visarga sandhi (visarga kept before a sibilant) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before s the visarga may stand (as printed here) or be assimilated to the sibilant (स्वस्थैस्स्मृता); the Gita Press keeps the visarga.
  • शुभां ददासि śubhāṃ dadāsi → शुभाम् + ददासि
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.
  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā → त्वत् + अन्या
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The pronoun stem त्वत् ends in d before a vowel.
  • सर्वोपकार sarvopakāra → सर्व + उपकार
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • सदाऽऽर्द्रचित्ता sadā''rdracittā → सदा + आर्द्रचित्ता
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press marks the absorbed long vowel with a double avagraha (ऽऽ), a reading aid only.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अशेषजन्तोः aśeṣajantoḥ — karmadhāraya (its first member अशेष is a nañ-bahuvrīhi: न विद्यते शेषः यस्य)
    Members: अशेष + जन्तु · Vigraha: अशेषः जन्तुः, तस्य aśeṣaḥ jantuḥ, tasya
    Literally “of a creature without remainder”; in the verse: of every living being, none excepted
    Alternative: It can also be taken as a collective, ‘of all creatures’ (अशेषाणां जन्तूनाम्); the singular is then distributive. The sense is the same.
  • स्वस्थैः svasthaiḥ — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: स्व + स्थ · Vigraha: स्वे तिष्ठन्ति इति स्वस्थाः, तैः sve tiṣṭhanti iti svasthāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by those who stand in themselves”; in the verse: by the healthy and untroubled, those not in distress
  • दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi — upapada tatpuruṣa whose first member is an itaretara dvandva
    Members: दारिद्र्य + दुःख + भय + हारिणी · Vigraha: दारिद्र्यं च दुःखं च भयं च दारिद्र्यदुःखभयानि; तानि हरति इति दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ dāridryaṃ ca duḥkhaṃ ca bhayaṃ ca dāridryaduḥkhabhayāni; tāni harati iti dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “O you who take away poverty, sorrow and fear”; in the verse: O remover of want, grief and fear
    Alternative: दारिद्र्यदुःख may be read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, ‘the sorrow of poverty’ (दारिद्र्यस्य दुःखम्), giving two things removed instead of three; the list of three is the usual reading.
  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā — pañcamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: त्वत् + अन्या · Vigraha: त्वत् अन्या tvat anyā
    Literally “other than you”; in the verse: anyone but you
  • सर्वोपकारकरणाय sarvopakārakaraṇāya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वोपकार)
    Members: सर्व + उपकार + करण · Vigraha: सर्वेषाम् उपकारः सर्वोपकारः; तस्य करणम्, तस्मै sarveṣām upakāraḥ sarvopakāraḥ; tasya karaṇam, tasmai
    Literally “for the doing of help to all”; in the verse: to do good to everyone
  • आर्द्रचित्ता ārdracittā — bahuvrīhi
    Members: आर्द्र + चित्त · Vigraha: आर्द्रं चित्तं यस्याः सा ārdraṃ cittaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose mind is moist”; in the verse: soft-hearted, compassionate

C. Morphology

  • स्मृता smṛtā — A passive participle with the force of a condition: ‘(when you are) remembered’. In line 2 its agent is स्वस्थैः; in line 1 the agent is left open, so anyone at all who remembers her.
  • ददासि dadāsi — Reduplicated present of √दा (juhotyādi class): द-दा-सि ‘you give’.
  • दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi — Vocative of the feminine of हारिन् (√हृ + -इन्, ‘taking away’), which adds -ई: हारिणी, voc. हारिणि.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) दुर्गे, स्मृता (त्वम्) अशेषजन्तोः भीतिं हरसि; स्वस्थैः स्मृता (त्वम्) अतीव शुभां मतिं ददासि। (हे) दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि, सर्वोपकारकरणाय सदा आर्द्रचित्ता त्वदन्या का (अस्ति)?

(he) durge, smṛtā (tvam) aśeṣajantoḥ bhītiṃ harasi; svasthaiḥ smṛtā (tvam) atīva śubhāṃ matiṃ dadāsi | (he) dāridryaduḥkhabhayahāriṇi, sarvopakārakaraṇāya sadā ārdracittā tvadanyā kā (asti)?

Two parallel clauses, each conditioned by स्मृता; then a rhetorical question, ‘who other than you …?’, whose answer is ‘no one’.

6Translation

LiteralO Durgā, remembered, you take away the fear of every creature; remembered by those at ease, you give an exceedingly auspicious understanding. O remover of poverty, sorrow and fear, who other than you is always tender-hearted for doing good to all?

ReadableO Durgā, when you are remembered you take away the fear of every creature; remembered by those at ease, you give a mind turned wholly to the good. Remover of poverty, sorrow and fear — who but you has a heart ever tender, ready to help all?

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo kinds of people remember her: any creature in fear, whose fear she removes, and those at ease (स्वस्थैः), to whom she gives a good understanding. The verse ends by asking who else is so tender towards all; the unspoken answer is no one.

Traditional interpretationRemembrance alone (स्मृता) is enough; no rite or offering is named. The verse is one of the seven gathered in the Saptaślokī Durgā, a short devotional selection from the Saptaśatī, and it is among the best known of the whole text.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names दुर्गा (190), दुःखहन्त्री (191) and भयापहा (121), close to this verse’s दुर्गे and दारिद्र्यदुःखभयहारिणि. The parallel is one of epithets; the two hymns are separate works.

4.18Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

एभिर्हतैर्जगदुपैति सुखं तथैते
कुर्वन्तु नाम नरकाय चिराय पापम्।
संग्राममृत्युमधिगम्य दिवं प्रयान्तु
मत्वेति नूनमहितान् विनिहंसि देवि ॥ १८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एभिः | हतैः | जगत् | उपैति | सुखम् | तथा | एतेकुर्वन्तु | नाम | नरकाय | चिराय | पापम्संग्राममृत्युम् | अधिगम्य | दिवम् | प्रयान्तुमत्वा | इति | नूनम् | अहितान् | विनिहंसि | देवि

3IAST

As recitedebhirhatairjagadupaiti sukhaṃ tathaite
kurvantu nāma narakāya cirāya pāpam |
saṃgrāmamṛtyumadhigamya divaṃ prayāntu
matveti nūnamahitān vinihaṃsi devi ||

Separatedebhiḥ | hataiḥ | jagat | upaiti | sukham | tathā | ete
kurvantu | nāma | narakāya | cirāya | pāpam
saṃgrāmamṛtyum | adhigamya | divam | prayāntu
matvā | iti | nūnam | ahitān | vinihaṃsi | devi

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एभिःebhiḥthese (asuras)pronoun इदम्, m. instr. pl.; with हतैः
हतैःhataiḥslainpast passive participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. instr. pl.; with एभिः an instrumental of cause: ‘by these being slain’
जगत्jagatthe worldn. nom. sg. of जगत्; subject of उपैति
उपैतिupaitiattains, comes toउप + √इ (adādi 2P ‘to go’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
सुखम्sukhamhappinessn. acc. sg. of सुख; object of उपैति
तथाtathāand so, likewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner, connective)
एतेetethese (asuras)pronoun एतद्, m. nom. pl.; subject of कुर्वन्तु and प्रयान्तु
कुर्वन्तुkurvantulet them do — granted they have done√कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada; concessive with नाम

नरकायnarakāya(such as leads) to hellm. dat. sg. of नरक; dative of result
चिरायcirāyafor a long timeindeclinable (dative of चिर used as an adverb)
पापम्pāpamsinn. acc. sg. of पाप; object of कुर्वन्तु

दिवम्divamto heavenf. acc. sg. of दिव् (‘sky, heaven’); accusative of the goal

मत्वाmatvāhaving thoughtabsolutive (ktvā) of √मन् (divādi 4Ā ‘to think’)
इतिitithus (closing the thought)indeclinable (quotative particle); closes the thought of lines 1–3
नूनम्nūnamsurelyindeclinable (particle of certainty)
अहितान्ahitānthe hostile, enemiesm. acc. pl. of अहित; compound (see Samāsa); object of विनिहंसि
विनिहंसिvinihaṃsiyou strike downवि + नि + √हन् (adādi 2P), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एभिर्हतैर्जगदुपैति ebhirhatairjagadupaiti → एभिः + हतैः + जगत् + उपैति
    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (voicing) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    After i and ai, the visarga (as ru) stands as r before the voiced h and j. The t of जगत् becomes d before the vowel of उपैति.
  • उपैति upaiti → उप + एति
    vowel sandhi between prefix and verb (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai. With most verbs beginning in e a prefix in a gives e (the a is absorbed), but forms of √इ ‘go’ keep the vṛddhi: उप + एति → उपैति.
  • तथैते tathaite → तथा + एते
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • मत्वेति matveti → मत्वा + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • संग्राममृत्युम् saṃgrāmamṛtyum — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: संग्राम + मृत्यु · Vigraha: संग्रामे मृत्युः, तम् saṃgrāme mṛtyuḥ, tam
    Literally “death in battle”; in the verse: a warrior’s death on the field
  • अहितान् ahitān — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + हित · Vigraha: न हिताः, तान् na hitāḥ, tān
    Literally “the not-friendly”; in the verse: enemies

C. Morphology

  • कुर्वन्तु kurvantu — Imperative with the particle नाम has concessive force: ‘let them indeed do (sin)’, i.e. ‘granted that they sin’. It sets up the contrast with प्रयान्तु ‘(yet) let them go (to heaven)’.
  • दिवम् divam — Accusative singular of दिव् (nominative द्यौः), ‘sky, heaven’, feminine in this sense.
  • विनिहंसि vinihaṃsi — Present of √हन् with two prefixes: हन् + सि → हंसि (the n becomes anusvāra before s). The 2nd person addresses the Goddess directly.

D. Anvaya — prose order

एभिः हतैः जगत् सुखम् उपैति; तथा एते चिराय नरकाय पापं कुर्वन्तु नाम, (तथापि) संग्राममृत्युम् अधिगम्य दिवं प्रयान्तु — इति मत्वा नूनं (हे) देवि, (त्वम्) अहितान् विनिहंसि।

ebhiḥ hataiḥ jagat sukham upaiti; tathā ete cirāya narakāya pāpaṃ kurvantu nāma, (tathāpi) saṃgrāmamṛtyum adhigamya divaṃ prayāntu — iti matvā nūnaṃ (he) devi, (tvam) ahitān vinihaṃsi |

Lines 1–3 are the Goddess’s thought as the gods imagine it, closed by इति; the main clause is मत्वा … विनिहंसि.

6Translation

Literal‘By these being slain the world attains happiness; and likewise these — granted they do sin (leading) to hell for long — having met death in battle, let them go to heaven’: having thought thus, surely, you strike down the enemies, O Devī.

Readable‘With these slain, the world will find happiness; and though they have earned a long hell by their sins, let them meet death in battle and go to heaven’ — surely it is with this thought, O Devī, that you strike your enemies down.

कुर्वन्तु नाम (‘let them do, granted’) is rendered as a concession, ‘though they have earned … by their sins’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods explain why she kills: the world is freed, and the slain asuras themselves, whatever their sins, reach heaven through death in battle at her hands. One act serves both the world and the killed.

Traditional interpretationThat death in battle opens heaven belongs to the warrior ethic of the epics and Purāṇas; the hymn applies it to the Goddess’s enemies. With 4.19–20 it builds the theme the gods state outright in 4.21: her compassion reaches even her enemies (वैरिष्वपि … दया).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the verse refuses to divide beings into those who deserve good and those who do not. Even her destruction is imagined as a form of care.

4.19Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

दृष्ट्वैव किं न भवती प्रकरोति भस्म
सर्वासुरानरिषु यत्प्रहिणोषि शस्त्रम्।
लोकान् प्रयान्तु रिपवोऽपि हि शस्त्रपूता
इत्थं मतिर्भवति तेष्वपि तेऽतिसाध्वी ॥ १९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दृष्ट्वा | एव | किम् | न | भवती | प्रकरोति | भस्मसर्वासुरान् | अरिषु | यत् | प्रहिणोषि | शस्त्रम्लोकान् | प्रयान्तु | रिपवः | अपि | हि | शस्त्रपूताःइत्थम् | मतिः | भवति | तेषु | अपि | ते | अतिसाध्वी

3IAST

As reciteddṛṣṭvaiva kiṃ na bhavatī prakaroti bhasma
sarvāsurānariṣu yatprahiṇoṣi śastram |
lokān prayāntu ripavo'pi hi śastrapūtā
itthaṃ matirbhavati teṣvapi te'tisādhvī ||

Separateddṛṣṭvā | eva | kim | na | bhavatī | prakaroti | bhasma
sarvāsurān | ariṣu | yat | prahiṇoṣi | śastram
lokān | prayāntu | ripavaḥ | api | hi | śastrapūtāḥ
ittham | matiḥ | bhavati | teṣu | api | te | atisādhvī

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāby looking (lit. having looked)absolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् (bhvādi 1P ‘to see’)
एवevamerely, justindeclinable (restrictive particle)
किम्kimwhy?interrogative किम् used as an indeclinable adverb
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
भवतीbhavatīyou (Your Ladyship)f. nom. sg. of the honorific pronoun भवत्; takes a third-person verb
प्रकरोतिprakarotimake (into)प्र + √कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do, make’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; agrees with the honorific भवती
भस्मbhasmaashesn. acc. sg. of भस्मन्; predicative accusative with प्रकरोति (‘make … ash’)
सर्वासुरान्sarvāsurānall the asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of प्रकरोति
अरिषुariṣuat the enemiesm. loc. pl. of अरि (i-stem); locative of the target
यत्yatthat (instead)indeclinable conjunction (यद् ‘that’); its clause states what the question is about
प्रहिणोषिprahiṇoṣiyou hurl, you send forthप्र + √हि (svādi 5P ‘to impel, send’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
शस्त्रम्śastramweaponn. acc. sg. of शस्त्र; object of प्रहिणोषि
लोकान्lokānto the (higher) worldsm. acc. pl. of लोक; accusative of the goal
प्रयान्तुprayāntulet them goप्र + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
रिपवःripavaḥthe enemiesm. nom. pl. of रिपु (u-stem); subject of प्रयान्तु
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
हिhifor (this is the reason)indeclinable (causal particle)
शस्त्रपूताःśastrapūtāḥpurified by the weaponm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रिपवः
इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
मतिःmatiḥthought, intentionf. nom. sg. of मति; subject of भवति
भवतिbhavatiis√भू (bhvādi 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेषुteṣutowards thempronoun तद्, m. loc. pl.; locative of the object of feeling
अपिapievenindeclinable (particle of inclusion)
तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
अतिसाध्वीatisādhvīexceedingly kind (good)f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of मतिः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • दृष्ट्वैव dṛṣṭvaiva → दृष्ट्वा + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • सर्वासुरान sarvāsurāna → सर्व + असुरान्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā. The final n then joins the vowel of अरिषु unchanged; it is not doubled, because the vowel before it is long.
  • रिपवोऽपि ripavo'pi → रिपवः + अपि
    visarga sandhi with elision of a · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • शस्त्रपूता इत्थं śastrapūtā itthaṃ → शस्त्रपूताः + इत्थम्
    visarga sandhi across the line break · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a vowel: the visarga becomes y and the y is dropped; the vowels stand apart. The sandhi is applied across the end of the third line.
  • मतिर्भवति matirbhavati → मतिः + भवति
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga (as ru) stands as r before the voiced bh.
  • तेष्वपि तेऽतिसाध्वी teṣvapi te'tisādhvī → तेषु + अपि + ते + अतिसाध्वी
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ; pūrvarūpa) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    u before a vowel becomes v; -e + a- → -e’- with avagraha.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वासुरान् sarvāsurān — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + असुर · Vigraha: सर्वे च ते असुराः च, तान् sarve ca te asurāḥ ca, tān
    Literally “all-asuras”; in the verse: the whole host of the asuras
  • शस्त्रपूताः śastrapūtāḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: शस्त्र + पूत · Vigraha: शस्त्रेण पूताः śastreṇa pūtāḥ
    Literally “purified by the weapon”; in the verse: cleansed of their sin by the stroke of her weapon
  • अतिसाध्वी atisādhvī — tatpuruṣa of the prādi kind (the prefix अति ‘exceedingly’ with an adjective)
    Members: अति + साध्वी · Vigraha: अतिशयेन साध्वी atiśayena sādhvī
    Literally “exceedingly good”; in the verse: supremely kind

C. Morphology

  • प्रहिणोषि prahiṇoṣi — Present of प्र + √हि (svādi class, stem हिनो-): ‘you send forth’. After the r of the prefix प्र, the n of the stem becomes ṇ (हिनोषि → प्रहिणोषि).
  • प्रकरोति prakaroti — Third person with the honorific भवती. The next clause turns to the direct second person (प्रहिणोषि): both address the Goddess.
  • भस्म bhasma — Neuter of भस्मन् ‘ash’. With √कृ it is a predicative accusative: ‘make (them) ash’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे देवि) भवती दृष्ट्वा एव सर्वासुरान् भस्म किं न प्रकरोति, यत् अरिषु शस्त्रं प्रहिणोषि? ‘रिपवः अपि शस्त्रपूताः (सन्तः) लोकान् प्रयान्तु’ हि — इत्थं तेषु अपि ते मतिः अतिसाध्वी भवति।

(he devi) bhavatī dṛṣṭvā eva sarvāsurān bhasma kiṃ na prakaroti, yat ariṣu śastraṃ prahiṇoṣi? ‘ripavaḥ api śastrapūtāḥ (santaḥ) lokān prayāntu’ hi — itthaṃ teṣu api te matiḥ atisādhvī bhavati |

A question (किं न … प्रकरोति), whose occasion is the यत्-clause (‘that you hurl a weapon’). Line 3 gives the Goddess’s own wish as the answer; line 4 is the gods’ verdict on it.

6Translation

LiteralWhy do you not make all the asuras ash merely by looking, that you hurl a weapon at the enemies? ‘Let even the enemies, purified by the weapon, go to the worlds’ — for thus is your thought, exceedingly kind even towards them.

ReadableWhy do you not burn all the asuras to ash with a single glance, but hurl your weapons at them instead? Because you would have even your enemies, made pure by your weapons, reach the higher worlds. So kind is your intent, even towards them.

‘Because’ renders हि; ‘the higher worlds’ interprets लोकान् ‘the worlds’, as 4.18 (दिवं प्रयान्तु) shows.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods answer their own question. She could destroy the asuras by sight alone, but she uses weapons so that their touch may purify her enemies and send them to the higher worlds. Even her choice of weapon is an act of kindness.

Traditional interpretationThe verse carries 4.18 further: death at the Goddess’s hand is read as purification (पूत), not only punishment. The tradition draws from it that her anger and her grace are one act seen from two sides.

4.20Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैस्तथोग्रैः
शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन दृशोऽसुराणाम्।
यन्नागता विलयमंशुमदिन्दुखण्ड-
योग्याननं तव विलोकयतां तदेतत् ॥ २० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः | तथा | उग्रैःशूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन | दृशः | असुराणाम्यत् | न | आगताः | विलयम् | अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननम्तव | विलोकयताम् | तत् | एतत्

3IAST

As recitedkhaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaistathograiḥ
śūlāgrakāntinivahena dṛśo'surāṇām |
yannāgatā vilayamaṃśumadindukhaṇḍa-
yogyānanaṃ tava vilokayatāṃ tadetat ||

Separatedkhaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥ | tathā | ugraiḥ
śūlāgrakāntinivahena | dṛśaḥ | asurāṇām
yat | na | āgatāḥ | vilayam | aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanam
tava | vilokayatām | tat | etat

तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. nom. sg.; resumes the यत्-clause
एतत्etat(is) thispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; points to the reason just given, their gazing at your face

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विस्फुरणैस्तथोग्रैः visphuraṇaistathograiḥ → …विस्फुरणैः + तथा + उग्रैः
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi (guṇa) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Visarga before t becomes s; ā + u → o.
  • दृशोऽसुराणाम् dṛśo'surāṇām → दृशः + असुराणाम्
    visarga sandhi with elision of a · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o, and the following a is elided (avagraha).
  • यन्नागता yannāgatā → यत् + न + आगताः
    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation); vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    t before n becomes n; a + ā → ā.
  • आगता विलयम् āgatā vilayam → आगताः + विलयम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, which is dropped before the consonant.
  • अंशुमदिन्दु aṃśumadindu → अंशुमत् + इन्दु
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of अंशुमत् becomes d before a vowel. The compound runs across the line break (…खण्ड- / योग्याननं) and belongs to the third line, where it begins.
  • तदेतत् tadetat → तत् + एतत्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः khaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, nested three times
    Members: खड्ग + प्रभा + निकर + विस्फुरण · Vigraha: खड्गस्य प्रभा खड्गप्रभा; तस्याः निकरः खड्गप्रभानिकरः; तस्य विस्फुरणानि, तैः khaḍgasya prabhā khaḍgaprabhā; tasyāḥ nikaraḥ khaḍgaprabhānikaraḥ; tasya visphuraṇāni, taiḥ
    Literally “by the flashings of the mass of the sword’s light”; in the verse: by the blinding flashes of her sword
  • शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन śūlāgrakāntinivahena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, nested three times
    Members: शूल + अग्र + कान्ति + निवह · Vigraha: शूलस्य अग्रम् शूलाग्रम्; तस्य कान्तिः शूलाग्रकान्तिः; तस्याः निवहः, तेन śūlasya agram śūlāgram; tasya kāntiḥ śūlāgrakāntiḥ; tasyāḥ nivahaḥ, tena
    Literally “by the stream of lustre of the trident’s tip”; in the verse: by the glare from the point of her trident
  • अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननम् aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanam — karmadhāraya; its first member is a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (…योग्य) built on a karmadhāraya (अंशुमदिन्दुखण्ड) that contains a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (इन्दुखण्ड)
    Members: अंशुमत् + इन्दु + खण्ड + योग्य + आनन · Vigraha: इन्दोः खण्डम् इन्दुखण्डम्; अंशुमत् इन्दुखण्डम् अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डम्; तस्य योग्यम् अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्यम्; तत् च तत् आननम् indoḥ khaṇḍam indukhaṇḍam; aṃśumat indukhaṇḍam aṃśumadindukhaṇḍam; tasya yogyam aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyam; tat ca tat ānanam
    Literally “the face fit for a ray-bearing piece of the moon”; in the verse: your face, like the radiant crescent moon
    Alternative: अंशुमत् ‘ray-bearing’ is also a name of the sun; as a dvandva (अंशुमान् च इन्दुखण्डं च) the face would be ‘worthy of the sun and the crescent’. The verse needs an image that spares the eyes after the glare of the weapons, which points to the moon; so अंशुमत् is taken as its qualifier.

C. Morphology

  • विलोकयताम् vilokayatām — Present participle of the curādi stem लोकय- with वि, gen. pl.: ‘of (them) gazing at’. It agrees with असुराणाम्; it is not an imperative, which would not fit the sentence.
  • आगताः āgatāḥ — Past participle of a verb of motion, active in sense: ‘(they) went (to)’. Feminine plural, to agree with दृशः.
  • दृशः dṛśaḥ — Nominative plural of the root-noun दृश् (feminine), ‘eye’, ‘sight’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

खड्गप्रभानिकरविस्फुरणैः उग्रैः तथा शूलाग्रकान्तिनिवहेन तव अंशुमदिन्दुखण्डयोग्याननं विलोकयताम् असुराणां दृशः यत् विलयं न आगताः, तत् एतत् (तव आननस्य दर्शनस्य फलम्)।

khaḍgaprabhānikaravisphuraṇaiḥ ugraiḥ tathā śūlāgrakāntinivahena tava aṃśumadindukhaṇḍayogyānanaṃ vilokayatām asurāṇāṃ dṛśaḥ yat vilayaṃ na āgatāḥ, tat etat (tava ānanasya darśanasya phalam) |

The यत्-clause carries the fact: the asuras’ eyes were not destroyed. तत् एतत् ‘that is this’ points to the participle phrase as its reason: they were gazing at your face.

6Translation

LiteralThat, by the fierce flashings of the mass of the sword’s light and likewise by the stream of lustre from the trident’s point, the eyes of the asuras did not go to destruction, (they) gazing at your face fit for the radiant crescent of the moon — that is this.

ReadableThe blazing flashes of your sword and the flood of light from the point of your trident did not destroy the asuras’ eyes — because they were gazing at your face, like the radiant crescent of the moon.

तत् एतत् (‘that is this’) points to the participle विलोकयताम् as the reason; English makes it ‘because’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAnother wonder of the battle: the glare of her weapons should have blinded the asuras, but their eyes were not destroyed. The gods give the reason: they were looking at her face, which is like the moon.

Traditional interpretationThe moon is the standing image of what cools and soothes; the verse sets it against the fierce light of sword and trident. The same face that terrifies in 4.13 here protects even her enemies, the theme of 4.18–21.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also likens her face to the moon: शरच्चन्द्रनिभानना (name 129), ‘whose face is like the autumn moon’. The image is shared devotional language; the works are separate.

4.21Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनं तव देवि शीलं
रूपं तथैतदविचिन्त्यमतुल्यमन्यैः।
वीर्यं च हन्तृ हृतदेवपराक्रमाणां
वैरिष्वपि प्रकटितैव दया त्वयेत्थम् ॥ २१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनम् | तव | देवि | शीलम्रूपम् | तथा | एतत् | अविचिन्त्यम् | अतुल्यम् | अन्यैःवीर्यम् | च | हन्तृ | हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम्वैरिषु | अपि | प्रकटिता | एव | दया | त्वया | इत्थम्

3IAST

As reciteddurvṛttavṛttaśamanaṃ tava devi śīlaṃ
rūpaṃ tathaitadavicintyamatulyamanyaiḥ |
vīryaṃ ca hantṛ hṛtadevaparākramāṇāṃ
vairiṣvapi prakaṭitaiva dayā tvayettham ||

Separateddurvṛttavṛttaśamanam | tava | devi | śīlam
rūpam | tathā | etat | avicintyam | atulyam | anyaiḥ
vīryam | ca | hantṛ | hṛtadevaparākramāṇām
vairiṣu | api | prakaṭitā | eva | dayā | tvayā | ittham

अतुल्यम्atulyamunequalledn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate
अन्यैःanyaiḥwith others, by otherspronominal adjective अन्य, m. instr. pl.; instrumental of comparison with अतुल्यम्

चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)

हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम्hṛtadevaparākramāṇāmof those who had robbed the gods of their mightm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); objective genitive with हन्तृ

एवevaindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
दयाdayācompassionf. nom. sg. of दया; subject

इत्थम्itthamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • दुर्वृत्त durvṛtta → दुस् + वृत्त
    visarga (s) sandhi inside a word · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The s of the prefix दुस् ‘ill’ becomes ru, which stands as r before the voiced v.
  • तथैतदविचिन्त्यमतुल्यमन्यैः tathaitadavicintyamatulyamanyaiḥ → तथा + एतत् + अविचिन्त्यम् + अतुल्यम् + अन्यैः
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); consonant sandhi (voicing) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    ā + e → ai; the t of एतत् becomes d before a vowel; the final m of the next two words simply joins the following vowel.
  • वैरिष्वपि vairiṣvapi → वैरिषु + अपि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    u before a vowel becomes v.
  • प्रकटितैव prakaṭitaiva → प्रकटिता + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • त्वयेत्थम् tvayettham → त्वया + इत्थम्
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनम् durvṛttavṛttaśamanam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दुर्वृत्तवृत्त)
    Members: दुर्वृत्त + वृत्त + शमन · Vigraha: दुर्वृत्तानां वृत्तम् दुर्वृत्तवृत्तम्; तस्य शमनम् durvṛttānāṃ vṛttam durvṛttavṛttam; tasya śamanam
    Literally “the quieting of the conduct of the ill-behaved”; in the verse: putting an end to the deeds of the wicked
    Alternative: दुर्वृत्त is itself a bahuvrīhi, दुष्टं वृत्तं येषां ते, ‘those whose conduct is bad’.
  • अविचिन्त्यम् avicintyam — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + विचिन्त्य · Vigraha: न विचिन्त्यम् na vicintyam
    Literally “not to be thought out”; in the verse: beyond the reach of thought
  • अतुल्यम् atulyam — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ + तुल्य · Vigraha: न तुल्यम् na tulyam
    Literally “not equal”; in the verse: without a match
  • हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम् hṛtadevaparākramāṇām — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (देवपराक्रम)
    Members: हृत + देव + पराक्रम · Vigraha: देवानां पराक्रमः देवपराक्रमः; हृतः देवपराक्रमः यैः तेषाम् devānāṃ parākramaḥ devaparākramaḥ; hṛtaḥ devaparākramaḥ yaiḥ teṣām
    Literally “of those by whom the gods’ valour was taken”; in the verse: of the asuras who had stripped the gods of their power

C. Morphology

  • हन्तृ hantṛ — Agent noun of √हन् (suffix tṛc), ‘slayer’, here neuter to agree with वीर्यम्. Like other agent nouns it takes its object in the genitive (हृतदेवपराक्रमाणाम्).
  • प्रकटिता prakaṭitā — Past participle of the denominative प्रकटय- ‘make evident’ (from the adjective प्रकट), feminine to agree with दया; त्वया is its agent.
  • अविचिन्त्यम् avicintyam — The negative prefix with the gerundive विचिन्त्य (वि + √चिन्त्, curādi, + -य): ‘not to be conceived’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) देवि, तव शीलं दुर्वृत्तवृत्तशमनम्; तथा एतत् रूपम् अविचिन्त्यम् अन्यैः अतुल्यं (च); वीर्यं च हृतदेवपराक्रमाणां हन्तृ; इत्थं त्वया वैरिषु अपि दया प्रकटिता एव।

(he) devi, tava śīlaṃ durvṛttavṛttaśamanam; tathā etat rūpam avicintyam anyaiḥ atulyaṃ (ca); vīryaṃ ca hṛtadevaparākramāṇāṃ hantṛ; itthaṃ tvayā vairiṣu api dayā prakaṭitā eva |

Three nominal sentences (on शील, रूप and वीर्य), then a passive verdict (दया … प्रकटिता) with त्वया as agent; इत्थम् sums up what 4.18–20 described.

6Translation

LiteralO Devī, your nature is the quelling of the conduct of the wicked; likewise this form is inconceivable and unequalled by others; and (your) valour is the slayer of those who had robbed the gods of their prowess; thus compassion has indeed been shown by you even towards enemies.

ReadableO Devī, it is your nature to put an end to the ways of the wicked; your form is beyond thought, and none can equal it; your valour destroys those who robbed the gods of their might — and in this way you have shown compassion even to your enemies.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods sum up the Goddess under three heads, her nature (शील), her form (रूप) and her valour (वीर्य), and draw the conclusion of 4.18–20: in this very way, by slaying them in battle, she has shown compassion even to her enemies.

Traditional interpretationThe three heads echo the gods’ earlier question in 4.6 (रूपमचिन्त्यमेतत्, अतिवीर्यम्, चरितानि). The point traditionally drawn is that her severity is itself mercy: the act that protects the gods also purifies the asuras.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) calls the Goddess अव्याजकरुणामूर्तिः (name 992), ‘compassion embodied, without a pretext’. The Devī Māhātmya makes a related point by example: compassion shown to those who did nothing to earn it.

4.22Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

केनोपमा भवतु तेऽस्य पराक्रमस्य
रूपं च शत्रुभयकार्यतिहारि कुत्र।
चित्ते कृपा समरनिष्ठुरता च दृष्टा
त्वय्येव देवि वरदे भुवनत्रयेऽपि ॥ २२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

केन | उपमा | भवतु | ते | अस्य | पराक्रमस्यरूपम् | च | शत्रुभयकारि | अतिहारि | कुत्रचित्ते | कृपा | समरनिष्ठुरता | च | दृष्टात्वयि | एव | देवि | वरदे | भुवनत्रये | अपि

3IAST

As recitedkenopamā bhavatu te'sya parākramasya
rūpaṃ ca śatrubhayakāryatihāri kutra |
citte kṛpā samaraniṣṭhuratā ca dṛṣṭā
tvayyeva devi varade bhuvanatraye'pi ||

Separatedkena | upamā | bhavatu | te | asya | parākramasya
rūpam | ca | śatrubhayakāri | atihāri | kutra
citte | kṛpā | samaraniṣṭhuratā | ca | dṛṣṭā

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • केनोपमा kenopamā → केन + उपमा
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • तेऽस्य te'sya → ते + अस्य
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).
  • शत्रुभयकार्यतिहारि śatrubhayakāryatihāri → शत्रुभयकारि + अतिहारि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before a vowel becomes y. Two adjectives, written as one string: शत्रुभयकारि and अतिहारि.
  • त्वय्येव tvayyeva → त्वयि + एव
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before e becomes y; the y after y is written as a doubled य्य.
  • भुवनत्रयेऽपि bhuvanatraye'pi → भुवनत्रये + अपि
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’- with avagraha.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शत्रुभयकारि śatrubhayakāri — upapada tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (शत्रुभय)
    Members: शत्रु + भय + कारिन् · Vigraha: शत्रूणां भयम् शत्रुभयम्; तत् करोति इति śatrūṇāṃ bhayam śatrubhayam; tat karoti iti
    Literally “making the fear of enemies”; in the verse: terrifying to foes
  • अतिहारि atihāri — tatpuruṣa of the prādi kind (अति with the agent adjective हारिन्)
    Members: अति + हारिन् · Vigraha: अतिशयेन हरति (मनः) इति atiśayena harati (manaḥ) iti
    Literally “exceedingly taking (the mind)”; in the verse: ravishingly beautiful
  • समरनिष्ठुरता samaraniṣṭhuratā — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: समर + निष्ठुरता · Vigraha: समरे निष्ठुरता samare niṣṭhuratā
    Literally “hardness in battle”; in the verse: unyielding severity in war
  • वरदे varade — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: वर + दा · Vigraha: वरं ददाति इति वरदा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ varaṃ dadāti iti varadā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “O boon-giving one”; in the verse: O giver of boons
  • भुवनत्रये bhuvanatraye — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: भुवन + त्रय · Vigraha: भुवनानां त्रयम्, तस्मिन् bhuvanānāṃ trayam, tasmin
    Literally “in the triad of worlds”; in the verse: anywhere in the three worlds

C. Morphology

  • भवतु bhavatu — Imperative used in a deliberative question: ‘with what may there be a comparison?’, i.e. ‘what could it be compared with?’. The expected answer is ‘nothing’.
  • शत्रुभयकारि śatrubhayakāri — Neuter nom. sg. of a stem in -इन् (कारिन्): the n drops and the i stays short. अतिहारि is formed the same way.
  • दृष्टा dṛṣṭā — Two subjects (कृपा and समरनिष्ठुरता) with a singular participle: it agrees with the nearer one, as often in verse.
  • समरनिष्ठुरता samaraniṣṭhuratā — निष्ठुर ‘harsh, relentless’ + the abstract suffix -ता (tal), feminine: ‘harshness’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) वरदे देवि, ते अस्य पराक्रमस्य उपमा केन भवतु? शत्रुभयकारि अतिहारि च रूपं कुत्र (अस्ति)? चित्ते कृपा समरनिष्ठुरता च भुवनत्रये अपि त्वयि एव दृष्टा।

(he) varade devi, te asya parākramasya upamā kena bhavatu? śatrubhayakāri atihāri ca rūpaṃ kutra (asti)? citte kṛpā samaraniṣṭhuratā ca bhuvanatraye api tvayi eva dṛṣṭā |

Two rhetorical questions (केन … भवतु; रूपं … कुत्र) and a statement with a passive participle (दृष्टा) whose place is त्वयि एव.

6Translation

LiteralWith what can there be a comparison of this valour of yours? And where (is) a form fear-causing to enemies (and) exceedingly captivating? Compassion in the heart and relentlessness in battle are seen, O Devī, giver of boons, in you alone, even in the three worlds.

ReadableTo what could this valour of yours be compared? Where else is a form that strikes terror into enemies and yet captivates the heart? Compassion within and relentlessness in battle — in all three worlds, O Devī, giver of boons, these are seen together in you alone.

‘The heart’ after ‘captivates’ is supplied: अतिहारि means ‘exceedingly taking (the mind)’. ‘Together’ makes explicit what एव implies: the pair is found nowhere else.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningNothing compares with her valour; no other form is at once terrible to enemies and supremely beautiful; nowhere in the three worlds are tenderness of heart and hardness in battle found together, except in her.

Traditional interpretationThe verse names the paradox the hymn has been describing since 4.13: the gentle and the terrible, mercy and severity, held in one person. In the Śākta tradition this union of opposites is a mark of the supreme Goddess.

4.23Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

त्रैलोक्यमेतदखिलं रिपुनाशनेन
त्रातं त्वया समरमूर्धनि तेऽपि हत्वा।
नीता दिवं रिपुगणा भयमप्यपास्त-
मस्माकमुन्मदसुरारिभवं नमस्ते ॥ २३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

त्रैलोक्यम् | एतत् | अखिलम् | रिपुनाशनेनत्रातम् | त्वया | समरमूर्धनि | ते | अपि | हत्वानीताः | दिवम् | रिपुगणाः | भयम् | अपि | अपास्तम्अस्माकम् | उन्मदसुरारिभवम् | नमः | ते

3IAST

As recitedtrailokyametadakhilaṃ ripunāśanena
trātaṃ tvayā samaramūrdhani te'pi hatvā |
nītā divaṃ ripugaṇā bhayamapyapāsta-
masmākamunmadasurāribhavaṃ namaste ||

Separatedtrailokyam | etat | akhilam | ripunāśanena
trātam | tvayā | samaramūrdhani | te | api | hatvā
nītāḥ | divam | ripugaṇāḥ | bhayam | api | apāstam
asmākam | unmadasurāribhavam | namaḥ | te

त्रैलोक्यम्trailokyamthe three worldsn. nom. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita: ‘the triad of worlds’); subject of त्रातम्
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies त्रैलोक्यम्
अखिलम्akhilamentiren. nom. sg. of अखिल; qualifies त्रैलोक्यम्
रिपुनाशनेनripunāśanenaby the destruction of the enemiesn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of means

तेtethey (the enemies)pronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; anticipates रिपुगणाः (it could also be read as the enclitic युष्मद्, ‘your’, but अपि ‘they too’ favours the pronoun)

रिपुगणाःripugaṇāḥthe hosts of enemiesm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of नीताः
भयम्bhayamfearn. nom. sg. of भय; subject of अपास्तम्
अपिapialsoindeclinable (particle of inclusion)

अस्माकम्asmākamourअस्मद्, gen. pl.
उन्मदसुरारिभवम्unmadasurāribhavamarising from the frenzied enemies of the godsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies भयम्
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्
तेteto youenclitic form of युष्मद्, dat. sg.; dative with नमः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्रैलोक्यमेतदखिलं trailokyametadakhilaṃ → त्रैलोक्यम् + एतत् + अखिलम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of एतत् becomes d before a vowel; the m of त्रैलोक्यम् simply joins the next vowel.
  • तेऽपि te'pi → ते + अपि
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’- with avagraha.
  • नीता दिवं nītā divaṃ → नीताः + दिवम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, which is dropped before the consonant. रिपुगणा भयम् is the same.
  • भयमप्यपास्त bhayamapyapāsta → भयम् + अपि + अपास्तम्
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before a vowel becomes y. अपास्तम् runs across the line break (…पास्त- / मस्माकम्) and belongs to the third line.
  • सुरारि surāri → सुर + अरि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • नमस्ते namaste → नमः + ते
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • रिपुनाशनेन ripunāśanena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: रिपु + नाशन · Vigraha: रिपूणां नाशनम्, तेन ripūṇāṃ nāśanam, tena
    Literally “by the destroying of enemies”; in the verse: by slaying the foes
  • समरमूर्धनि samaramūrdhani — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: समर + मूर्धन् · Vigraha: समरस्य मूर्धा, तस्मिन् samarasya mūrdhā, tasmin
    Literally “at the head of battle”; in the verse: in the forefront of the fight
  • रिपुगणाः ripugaṇāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: रिपु + गण · Vigraha: रिपूणां गणाः ripūṇāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally “hosts of enemies”; in the verse: the enemy armies
  • उन्मदसुरारिभवम् unmadasurāribhavam — pañcamī tatpuruṣa (भव ‘arising’ as the final member) containing a karmadhāraya (उन्मदसुरारि) and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सुरारि)
    Members: उन्मद + सुर + अरि + भव · Vigraha: सुराणाम् अरयः सुरारयः; उन्मदाः सुरारयः उन्मदसुरारयः; तेभ्यः भवम् surāṇām arayaḥ surārayaḥ; unmadāḥ surārayaḥ unmadasurārayaḥ; tebhyaḥ bhavam
    Literally “arising from the maddened foes of the gods”; in the verse: the terror the frenzied asuras had caused
    Alternative: उन्मद is itself a bahuvrīhi: उद्गतः मदः येषां ते, ‘in whom frenzy has risen’.

C. Morphology

  • त्रातम् trātam — Past participle of √त्रै ‘protect’: the root’s ai becomes ā before the suffix (त्रा + त → त्रात).
  • हत्वा hatvā — An absolutive in a passive sentence: its subject is the logical agent of the main verb नीताः, i.e. त्वया. ‘(You) having slain them, they were led (by you) to heaven.’
  • अपास्तम् apāstam — Past participle of अप + √अस् ‘throw’: ‘thrown away’, dispelled.
  • ते te — Two different words: in line 2, the pronoun तद् (m. nom. pl., ‘they’); in line 4, the enclitic of युष्मद् (dat. sg., ‘to you’).

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वया रिपुनाशनेन एतत् अखिलं त्रैलोक्यं त्रातम्; समरमूर्धनि हत्वा ते रिपुगणाः अपि (त्वया) दिवं नीताः; अस्माकम् उन्मदसुरारिभवं भयम् अपि अपास्तम्। ते नमः।

tvayā ripunāśanena etat akhilaṃ trailokyaṃ trātam; samaramūrdhani hatvā te ripugaṇāḥ api (tvayā) divaṃ nītāḥ; asmākam unmadasurāribhavaṃ bhayam api apāstam | te namaḥ |

Three passive clauses with the one agent त्वया (त्रातम्, नीताः, अपास्तम्), then the salutation नमस्ते.

6Translation

LiteralThis entire triple world has been saved by you through the destruction of the enemies; having slain (them) at the head of battle, they too, the hosts of enemies, have been led to heaven; and our fear, arising from the frenzied enemies of the gods, has been cast away. Salutation to you!

ReadableBy destroying the enemies you have saved these three worlds entire. Slaying them at the forefront of battle, you have led even those enemy hosts to heaven; and you have driven away our fear of the frenzied foes of the gods. Salutation to you!

The passives (त्रातम्, नीताः, अपास्तम्) are turned into active sentences with ‘you’ as subject.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn sums up what the Goddess has done: the three worlds saved, the enemies themselves sent to heaven, and the gods’ fear removed. It closes the long vasantatilakā praise with नमस्ते.

Traditional interpretationThree beneficiaries are named in order, the world, the slain enemies and the gods, so the verse gathers the themes of 4.18–22. After the salutation the gods turn from praise to petition: the anuṣṭubh verses that follow (4.24–27) ask for protection.

4.24Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शूलेन पाहि नो देवि पाहि खड्गेन चाम्बिके।
घण्टास्वनेन नः पाहि चापज्यानिःस्वनेन च ॥ २४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शूलेन | पाहि | नः | देवि | पाहि | खड्गेन | च | अम्बिकेघण्टास्वनेन | नः | पाहि | चापज्यानिःस्वनेन | च

As recitedśūlena pāhi no devi pāhi khaḍgena cāmbike |
ghaṇṭāsvanena naḥ pāhi cāpajyāniḥsvanena ca ||
