5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पाहि नो देवि pāhi no devi → पाहि + नः + देवि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.
  • चाम्बिके cāmbike → च + अम्बिके
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • नः पाहि naḥ pāhi → नः + पाहि
    visarga sandhi (visarga kept) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before the unvoiced p the visarga stays. Compare नो देवि in the first line, where the next sound is voiced.
  • चापज्यानिःस्वनेन cāpajyāniḥsvanena → चाप + ज्या + निस् + स्वन
    visarga (s) sandhi inside a compound · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    The s of the prefix निस् becomes visarga before the sibilant s of स्वन.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • घण्टास्वनेन ghaṇṭāsvanena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: घण्टा + स्वन · Vigraha: घण्टायाः स्वनः, तेन ghaṇṭāyāḥ svanaḥ, tena
    Literally “with the sound of the bell”; in the verse: with the ringing of the bell that stunned the asuras in battle (2.56)
  • चापज्यानिःस्वनेन cāpajyāniḥsvanena — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (चापज्या)
    Members: चाप + ज्या + निःस्वन · Vigraha: चापस्य ज्या चापज्या; तस्याः निःस्वनः, तेन cāpasya jyā cāpajyā; tasyāḥ niḥsvanaḥ, tena
    Literally “with the out-sound of the bow’s string”; in the verse: with the twang of her bowstring
    Alternative: निःस्वन is itself a prefixed noun, निस् + स्वन, ‘sound going out’, the resounding twang.

C. Morphology

  • पाहि pāhi — Imperative 2nd sg. of √पा ‘protect’ (adādi class): पा + हि. Said three times in the verse.
  • नः naḥ — Enclitic acc. pl. of अस्मद्, used in place of अस्मान्. As an enclitic it never begins a sentence; here it follows पाहि and घण्टास्वनेन.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) देवि, शूलेन नः पाहि; (हे) अम्बिके, खड्गेन च (नः) पाहि; घण्टास्वनेन चापज्यानिःस्वनेन च नः पाहि।

(he) devi, śūlena naḥ pāhi; (he) ambike, khaḍgena ca (naḥ) pāhi; ghaṇṭāsvanena cāpajyāniḥsvanena ca naḥ pāhi |

Three imperatives (पाहि) with one object (नः) and four instruments: trident, sword, the bell’s sound, the bowstring’s twang.

6Translation

LiteralWith the trident protect us, O Devī; protect (us) with the sword too, O Ambikā; with the sound of the bell protect us, and with the twang of the bowstring.

ReadableProtect us with your trident, O Devī; protect us with your sword, O Mother; protect us with the ringing of your bell and the twang of your bowstring.

‘Your’ is supplied throughout: these are the weapons the gods gave her in 2.20–24.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn changes metre and mood: from the long vasantatilakā praises the gods turn to a short anuṣṭubh prayer for protection. Four of her attributes are invoked: trident, sword, bell and bow, each a god’s gift in 2.20–24 (Śiva’s trident, Vāyu’s bow, Indra’s bell from Airāvata, Kāla’s sword).

Traditional interpretationEven the sounds of her weapons protect: the bell, whose ringing stunned the asuras (घण्टास्वनविमोहितान्, 2.56), and the bowstring, whose twang shook the worlds (धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन, 2.38). The four verses 4.24–27 are often recited on their own as a prayer for protection.

4.25Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

प्राच्यां रक्ष प्रतीच्यां च चण्डिके रक्ष दक्षिणे।
भ्रामणेनात्मशूलस्य उत्तरस्यां तथेश्वरि ॥ २५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

प्राच्याम् | रक्ष | प्रतीच्याम् | च | चण्डिके | रक्ष | दक्षिणेभ्रामणेन | आत्मशूलस्य | उत्तरस्याम् | तथा | ईश्वरि

3IAST

As recitedprācyāṃ rakṣa pratīcyāṃ ca caṇḍike rakṣa dakṣiṇe |
bhrāmaṇenātmaśūlasya uttarasyāṃ tatheśvari ||

Separatedprācyām | rakṣa | pratīcyām | ca | caṇḍike | rakṣa | dakṣiṇe
bhrāmaṇena | ātmaśūlasya | uttarasyām | tathā | īśvari

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
प्राच्याम्prācyāmin the eastf. loc. sg. of प्राची (‘the eastern (quarter)’, दिशि understood)
रक्षrakṣaprotect (us)√रक्ष् (bhvādi 1P ‘to guard’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
प्रतीच्याम्pratīcyāmin the westf. loc. sg. of प्रतीची (‘the western (quarter)’)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
चण्डिकेcaṇḍikeO Caṇḍikāf. voc. sg. of चण्डिका
रक्षrakṣaprotect (us)√रक्ष्, loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
दक्षिणेdakṣiṇein the southloc. sg. of दक्षिण (m./n., ‘the southern side’), with ‘quarter’ or ‘region’ understood
भ्रामणेनbhrāmaṇenaby the whirlingn. instr. sg. of भ्रामण (action noun in -अन from the causative भ्रामय- of √भ्रम्, divādi/bhvādi ‘to wander, whirl’); instrumental of means
आत्मशूलस्यātmaśūlasyaof your own tridentn. (also m.) gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); objective genitive with भ्रामणेन
उत्तरस्याम्uttarasyāmin the northf. loc. sg. of the pronominal उत्तर (‘the northern (quarter)’)
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
ईश्वरिīśvariO Sovereign Ladyf. voc. sg. of ईश्वरी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्राच्यां रक्ष prācyāṃ rakṣa → प्राच्याम् + रक्ष
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra; likewise प्रतीच्यां च.
  • भ्रामणेनात्म bhrāmaṇenātma → भ्रामणेन + आत्मशूलस्य
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • आत्मशूलस्य उत्तरस्यां ātmaśūlasya uttarasyāṃ → आत्मशूलस्य + उत्तरस्याम्
    no sandhi (hiatus at the pāda break) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87) would give …स्योत्तरस्यां; it is not applied here (metrical licence)
    Declared in sandhi_exceptions: the third and fourth pādas are kept apart.
  • तथेश्वरि tatheśvari → तथा + ईश्वरि
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + ī → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • आत्मशूलस्य ātmaśūlasya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: आत्मन् + शूल · Vigraha: आत्मनः शूलम्, तस्य ātmanaḥ śūlam, tasya
    Literally “of the trident of oneself”; in the verse: of your own trident

C. Morphology

  • दक्षिणे dakṣiṇe — The other three quarters are feminine locatives (प्राच्याम्, प्रतीच्याम्, उत्तरस्याम्, with दिशि understood). The south is named with the m./n. locative of दक्षिण, ‘on the southern side’; the feminine would be दक्षिणस्याम्.
  • उत्तरस्याम् uttarasyām — उत्तर in the sense of a direction is declined like a pronoun: feminine loc. sg. -स्याम् (as सर्वस्याम्).
  • भ्रामणेन bhrāmaṇena — Action noun from the causative of √भ्रम्: ‘by causing to whirl’, the trident swung round about.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) चण्डिके, प्राच्यां (नः) रक्ष, प्रतीच्यां च (रक्ष), दक्षिणे रक्ष; तथा (हे) ईश्वरि, आत्मशूलस्य भ्रामणेन उत्तरस्यां (रक्ष)।

(he) caṇḍike, prācyāṃ (naḥ) rakṣa, pratīcyāṃ ca (rakṣa), dakṣiṇe rakṣa; tathā (he) īśvari, ātmaśūlasya bhrāmaṇena uttarasyāṃ (rakṣa) |

A series of imperatives with ‘us’ understood from 4.24. The instrumental भ्रामणेनात्मशूलस्य can go with the north alone or, through तथा ‘likewise’, with all four quarters.

Left unjoined: आत्मशूलस्य | उत्तरस्याम् — The vowels stand apart at the pāda break (भ्रामणेनात्मशूलस्य | उत्तरस्यां): epic and Purāṇic verse often leaves sandhi unapplied between the third and fourth pādas, and joining them (…शूलस्योत्तरस्यां) would take a syllable from the half-line, leaving seven where the metre needs eight. A metrical licence, printed so in the Gita Press text.

6Translation

LiteralIn the east protect (us), and in the west; O Caṇḍikā, protect (us) in the south; by the whirling of your own trident, likewise in the north, O Īśvarī.

ReadableProtect us in the east and in the west, O Caṇḍikā; protect us in the south; and in the north as well, O Sovereign Lady, by the whirling of your trident.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe prayer now covers space: the four quarters are named one by one, and her own trident, whirled about her, is the guard.

Traditional interpretationGuarding the quarters is the usual plan of protective prayers (kavacas), in which each direction is given a guardian; the Devī Kavaca recited before the Saptaśatī in the Gita Press pāṭha follows that plan. Here one Goddess guards them all.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name चण्डिका (755), the name by which the Devī Māhātmya addresses her here and throughout. The shared name belongs to a shared Śākta vocabulary; the works are separate.

4.26Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सौम्यानि यानि रूपाणि त्रैलोक्ये विचरन्ति ते।
यानि चात्यर्थघोराणि तै रक्षास्मांस्तथा भुवम् ॥ २६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सौम्यानि | यानि | रूपाणि | त्रैलोक्ये | विचरन्ति | तेयानि | च | अत्यर्थघोराणि | तैः | रक्ष | अस्मान् | तथा | भुवम्

3IAST

As recitedsaumyāni yāni rūpāṇi trailokye vicaranti te |
yāni cātyarthaghorāṇi tai rakṣāsmāṃstathā bhuvam ||

Separatedsaumyāni | yāni | rūpāṇi | trailokye | vicaranti | te
yāni | ca | atyarthaghorāṇi | taiḥ | rakṣa | asmān | tathā | bhuvam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सौम्यानिsaumyānigentle, benignn. nom. pl. of सौम्य (taddhita from सोम, ‘moon-like’, mild); qualifies रूपाणि
यानिyāniwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.
रूपाणिrūpāṇiformsn. nom. pl. of रूप; subject of विचरन्ति
त्रैलोक्येtrailokyein the three worldsn. loc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य
विचरन्तिvicarantimove about, roamवि + √चर् (bhvādi 1P ‘to move’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
यानिyāniwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अत्यर्थघोराणिatyarthaghorāṇiterrible beyond measuren. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
तैःtaiḥwith thempronoun तद्, n. instr. pl.; correlative of the two यानि
रक्षrakṣaprotect√रक्ष् (bhvādi 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अस्मान्asmānusअस्मद्, acc. pl.; object of रक्ष
तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (connective)
भुवम्bhuvamthe earthf. acc. sg. of the root-noun भू; object of रक्ष

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चात्यर्थघोराणि cātyarthaghorāṇi → च + अत्यर्थघोराणि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); yaṇ inside the compound · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    a + a → ā; inside the compound अति + अर्थ → अत्यर्थ (i before a vowel becomes y).
  • तै रक्षा tai rakṣā → तैः + रक्ष
    visarga sandhi (r before r) · रो रि (8.3.14), ढ्रलोपे पूर्वस्य दीर्घोऽणः (6.3.111)
    The visarga of तैः (as r) is dropped before the r of रक्ष. The rule lengthens the vowel before it, but ai is already long, so nothing more changes.
  • रक्षास्मांस्तथा rakṣāsmāṃstathā → रक्ष + अस्मान् + तथा
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); consonant sandhi (n + t → ṃst) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7)
    a + a → ā; final n before t becomes anusvāra + s (अस्मांस्तथा).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अत्यर्थघोराणि atyarthaghorāṇi — karmadhāraya (the first member, अत्यर्थ ‘beyond measure’, qualifies adverbially)
    Members: अत्यर्थ + घोर · Vigraha: अत्यर्थं घोराणि atyarthaṃ ghorāṇi
    Literally “excessively terrible”; in the verse: fearsome beyond all measure
    Alternative: अत्यर्थ is itself a prādi compound, अर्थम् अतिक्रान्तम्, ‘passing the measure’.

C. Morphology

  • सौम्यानि saumyāni — Taddhita from सोम (the moon, or Soma): ‘moon-like’, hence mild, gentle; the opposite of घोर.
  • तैः taiḥ — The correlative of both relative clauses (यानि … यानि च …): ‘with those (forms)’.
  • भुवम् bhuvam — Accusative singular of the feminine root-noun भू ‘earth’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यानि ते सौम्यानि रूपाणि त्रैलोक्ये विचरन्ति, यानि च अत्यर्थघोराणि (रूपाणि त्रैलोक्ये विचरन्ति), तैः अस्मान् तथा भुवं रक्ष।

yāni te saumyāni rūpāṇi trailokye vicaranti, yāni ca atyarthaghorāṇi (rūpāṇi trailokye vicaranti), taiḥ asmān tathā bhuvaṃ rakṣa |

Two relative clauses (यानि … यानि च …) taken up by one correlative (तैः), then the imperative with two objects.

6Translation

LiteralYour gentle forms which move about in the three worlds, and those which are terrible beyond measure — with them protect us and the earth as well.

ReadableWith all your forms that roam the three worlds, the gentle ones and those terrible beyond measure, protect us, and the earth as well.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAfter the quarters of space, the forms of the Goddess: her gentle forms and her terrible ones alike are asked to guard the gods and the earth.

Traditional interpretationThe pairing of gentle (सौम्य) and terrible (घोर) forms is basic to Śākta thought; both are hers, and both protect. The hymn has shown both: the moon-like face of 4.12 and 4.20 and the face of wrath in 4.13.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) includes the name सौम्या (910), ‘the gentle one’. The Devī Māhātmya asks for the protection of the gentle and the terrible forms together.

4.27Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

खड्गशूलगदादीनि यानि चास्त्राणि तेऽम्बिके।
करपल्लवसङ्गीनि तैरस्मान् रक्ष सर्वतः ॥ २७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

खड्गशूलगदादीनि | यानि | च | अस्त्राणि | ते | अम्बिकेकरपल्लवसङ्गीनि | तैः | अस्मान् | रक्ष | सर्वतः

3IAST

As recitedkhaḍgaśūlagadādīni yāni cāstrāṇi te'mbike |
karapallavasaṅgīni tairasmān rakṣa sarvataḥ ||

Separatedkhaḍgaśūlagadādīni | yāni | ca | astrāṇi | te | ambike
karapallavasaṅgīni | taiḥ | asmān | rakṣa | sarvataḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
खड्गशूलगदादीनिkhaḍgaśūlagadādīnisword, trident, mace and the restn. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अस्त्राणि
यानिyāniwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अस्त्राणिastrāṇiweaponsn. nom. pl. of अस्त्र (‘missile, weapon’)
तेteyourenclitic form of युष्मद्, gen. sg.
अम्बिकेambikeO Ambikā, O Motherf. voc. sg. of अम्बिका
करपल्लवसङ्गीनिkarapallavasaṅgīniresting in your tender hands (lit. clinging to your sprout-like hands)n. nom. pl. of करपल्लवसङ्गिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies अस्त्राणि
तैःtaiḥwith thempronoun तद्, n. instr. pl.; correlative of यानि
अस्मान्asmānusअस्मद्, acc. pl.; object of रक्ष
रक्षrakṣaprotect√रक्ष् (bhvādi 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
सर्वतःsarvataḥon every sideindeclinable (सर्व + the suffix -तस्)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • खड्गशूलगदादीनि khaḍgaśūlagadādīni → खड्गशूलगदा + आदीनि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā.
  • चास्त्राणि cāstrāṇi → च + अस्त्राणि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • तेऽम्बिके te'mbike → ते + अम्बिके
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).
  • तैरस्मान् tairasmān → तैः + अस्मान्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai the visarga (as ru) stands as r before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • खड्गशूलगदादीनि khaḍgaśūlagadādīni — bahuvrīhi with आदि, whose first member is an itaretara dvandva
    Members: खड्ग + शूल + गदा + आदि · Vigraha: खड्गः च शूलं च गदा च खड्गशूलगदाः; ताः आदौ येषां तानि khaḍgaḥ ca śūlaṃ ca gadā ca khaḍgaśūlagadāḥ; tāḥ ādau yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally “having sword, trident and mace at their head”; in the verse: the sword, the trident, the mace and all the rest
  • करपल्लवसङ्गीनि karapallavasaṅgīni — saptamī tatpuruṣa whose first member is a karmadhāraya of comparison (करपल्लव, with the standard of comparison पल्लव placed second)
    Members: कर + पल्लव + सङ्गिन् · Vigraha: करः पल्लवः इव करपल्लवः; करपल्लवेषु सङ्गीनि karaḥ pallavaḥ iva karapallavaḥ; karapallaveṣu saṅgīni
    Literally “clinging to the sprout-like hands”; in the verse: held in her soft, leaf-tender hands
    Alternative: करपल्लव can also be read as a metaphor, ‘the hand that is a tender shoot’ (करः एव पल्लवः); the meaning is the same.

C. Morphology

  • करपल्लवसङ्गीनि karapallavasaṅgīni — The last member सङ्गिन् (√सञ्ज् ‘cling’ + -इन्) is declined as a neuter -इन् stem: nom. pl. -ईनि.
  • सर्वतः sarvataḥ — सर्व + the suffix -तस्, ‘from all sides’, hence ‘on every side’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) अम्बिके, खड्गशूलगदादीनि करपल्लवसङ्गीनि यानि च ते अस्त्राणि (सन्ति), तैः अस्मान् सर्वतः रक्ष।

(he) ambike, khaḍgaśūlagadādīni karapallavasaṅgīni yāni ca te astrāṇi (santi), taiḥ asmān sarvataḥ rakṣa |

A relative clause (यानि … अस्त्राणि) taken up by the correlative तैः; the imperative रक्ष ends the hymn’s petitions.

6Translation

LiteralAnd your weapons, O Ambikā — sword, trident, mace and the rest — which cling to your sprout-like hands: with them protect us on every side.

ReadableAnd with all your weapons, O Mother — sword, trident, mace and the rest — that rest in your tender hands, protect us on every side.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last petition of the hymn: all her weapons, held in her soft hands, are asked to guard the gods from every side. It completes the prayer begun in 4.24 with trident and sword.

Traditional interpretationTerrible weapons in leaf-tender hands repeat in small the hymn’s theme of the gentle and the fierce in one Goddess. With this verse the Śakrādi Stuti ends; the narrative resumes at 4.28, and the gods worship her with flowers and incense (4.29–30).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name शूलाद्यायुधसम्पन्ना (506), ‘endowed with the trident and other weapons’, built with the same ‘X and the rest’ (आदि) pattern as खड्गशूलगदादीनि. The works are separate.

4.28Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ऋषिरुवाच ॥ २८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The final s of ऋषिस् becomes ru. After a vowel other than a/ā and before a vowel it is not turned into visarga (खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः, 8.3.15, applies only at a pause or before an unvoiced consonant), so it stands as r and joins the next word: ṛṣir uvāca.

C. Morphology

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicating syllable (व → उ): ‘(he) said’, the narrative past.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe seer said:

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn of Indra and the gods (4.2–27) is over, and Medhas, the seer, resumes his narration to King Suratha and the merchant Samādhi. What follows is the close of the Madhyama Carita: the Goddess’s answer to the hymn and the boons the gods ask.

4.29Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवं स्तुता सुरैर्दिव्यैः कुसुमैर्नन्दनोद्भवैः।
अर्चिता जगतां धात्री तथा गन्धानुलेपनैः ॥ २९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | स्तुता | सुरैः | दिव्यैः | कुसुमैः | नन्दनोद्भवैःअर्चिता | जगताम् | धात्री | तथा | गन्धानुलेपनैः

3IAST

As recitedevaṃ stutā surairdivyaiḥ kusumairnandanodbhavaiḥ |
arcitā jagatāṃ dhātrī tathā gandhānulepanaiḥ ||

Separatedevam | stutā | suraiḥ | divyaiḥ | kusumaiḥ | nandanodbhavaiḥ
arcitā | jagatām | dhātrī | tathā | gandhānulepanaiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner); refers back to the hymn 4.2–27
स्तुताstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies धात्री
सुरैःsuraiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of सुर; agent of स्तुता (and, understood, of अर्चिता)
दिव्यैःdivyaiḥheavenlyn. instr. pl. of दिव्य; qualifies कुसुमैः
कुसुमैःkusumaiḥwith flowersn. instr. pl. of कुसुम; instrument of अर्चिता
नन्दनोद्भवैःnandanodbhavaiḥgrown in Nandana, Indra’s gardenn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कुसुमैः
अर्चिताarcitāworshipped, honouredpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of √अर्च् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to honour’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies धात्री
जगताम्jagatāmof the worldsn. gen. pl. of जगत्; depends on धात्री
धात्रीdhātrīsustainer, nurse-motherf. nom. sg. of धात्री (√धा + tṛc, feminine in -ī); the subject, carried into 4.30 (प्राह)
तथाtathālikewise, and alsoindeclinable (connective adverb)
गन्धानुलेपनैःgandhānulepanaiḥwith perfumes and unguentsn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of अर्चिता

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं स्तुता evaṃ stutā → एवम् + स्तुता
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    A word-final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra. The same gives जगतां धात्री.
  • सुरैर्दिव्यैः surairdivyaiḥ → सुरैः + दिव्यैः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai (a vowel other than a/ā) the final s becomes ru and stays r before the voiced d. The same in कुसुमैर्नन्दनोद्भवैः.
  • नन्दनोद्भवैः nandanodbhavaiḥ → नन्दन + उद्भवैः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • गन्धानुलेपनैः gandhānulepanaiḥ → गन्ध + अनुलेपनैः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • नन्दनोद्भवैः nandanodbhavaiḥ — bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa: the members are in different cases)
    Members: नन्दन + उद्भव · Vigraha: नन्दने उद्भवः येषां तानि, तैः nandane udbhavaḥ yeṣāṃ tāni, taiḥ
    Literally “(flowers) whose origin is in Nandana”; in the verse: flowers from Nandana, the garden of Indra’s heaven
    Alternative: It could be taken as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa with उद्भव in the sense ‘sprung’ (नन्दनात् उद्भवः); but उद्भव is an action noun ‘origin’, so the bahuvrīhi is the natural analysis. The sense is the same.
  • गन्धानुलेपनैः gandhānulepanaiḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: गन्ध + अनुलेपन · Vigraha: गन्धाः च अनुलेपनानि च, तैः gandhāḥ ca anulepanāni ca, taiḥ
    Literally “with perfumes and unguents”; in the verse: fragrant substances and anointing pastes, two of the offerings of worship
    Alternative: Also possible as a tatpuruṣa, ‘with fragrant unguents’ (गन्धयुक्तानि अनुलेपनानि); with तथा marking a further class of offering, the dvandva reads more naturally.

C. Morphology

  • स्तुता stutā — √स्तु is aniṭ: the participle takes -ta directly (स्तुत), feminine स्तुता.
  • अर्चिता arcitā — √अर्च् takes the connecting -i- (iṭ): अर्चित, feminine अर्चिता. Both participles are passive and agree with the Goddess as subject.
  • धात्री dhātrī — Agent noun धातृ ‘one who holds, supports’ (√धा + tṛc) made feminine with -ī: ‘sustainer’; in common usage also ‘nurse, foster-mother’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

एवं सुरैः स्तुता, दिव्यैः नन्दनोद्भवैः कुसुमैः तथा गन्धानुलेपनैः अर्चिता जगतां धात्री (4.30 … प्राह)।

evaṃ suraiḥ stutā, divyaiḥ nandanodbhavaiḥ kusumaiḥ tathā gandhānulepanaiḥ arcitā jagatāṃ dhātrī (4.30 … prāha) |

The sentence runs into 4.30: two participial phrases (स्तुता …, अर्चिता …) describe the subject जगतां धात्री, whose verb is प्राह in the next verse.

6Translation

LiteralThus praised by the gods, worshipped with heavenly flowers grown in Nandana, and likewise with perfumes and unguents, the sustainer of the worlds —

ReadablePraised in this way by the gods, and honoured with heavenly flowers from the garden of Nandana, with perfumes and with fragrant pastes, the Mother of the worlds —

धात्री is rendered ‘Mother’, its common sense of nurse and sustainer; the sentence is completed in 4.30.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas describes what followed the hymn: the gods do not stop at words but worship the Goddess with flowers from Nandana, Indra’s pleasure-garden, and with perfumes and unguents. The incense follows in 4.30.

Traditional interpretationFlowers, fragrance and (in the next verse) incense are among the standard upacāras of pūjā. In the story the gods perform for the Goddess the worship that her devotees go on to offer when the text is recited.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name जगद्धात्री (935), ‘sustainer of the world’; the Devī Māhātmya says the same in two words, जगतां धात्री. The two texts remain separate works.

4.30Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भक्त्या समस्तैस्त्रिदशैर्दिव्यैर्धूपैस्तु धूपिता।
प्राह प्रसादसुमुखी समस्तान् प्रणतान् सुरान् ॥ ३० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भक्त्या | समस्तैः | त्रिदशैः | दिव्यैः | धूपैः | तु | धूपिताप्राह | प्रसादसुमुखी | समस्तान् | प्रणतान् | सुरान्

3IAST

As recitedbhaktyā samastaistridaśairdivyairdhūpaistu dhūpitā |
prāha prasādasumukhī samastān praṇatān surān ||

Separatedbhaktyā | samastaiḥ | tridaśaiḥ | divyaiḥ | dhūpaiḥ | tu | dhūpitā
prāha | prasādasumukhī | samastān | praṇatān | surān

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भक्त्याbhaktyāwith devotionf. instr. sg. of भक्ति (√भज् + ktin); instrumental of manner
समस्तैःsamastaiḥby allm. instr. pl. of समस्त (सम् + √अस् ‘to throw’ + kta: ‘thrown together’, hence ‘all’); qualifies त्रिदशैः
त्रिदशैःtridaśaiḥby the gods (lit. ‘the Thirty’)m. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of धूपिता
दिव्यैःdivyaiḥheavenlym. instr. pl. of दिव्य; qualifies धूपैः
धूपैःdhūpaiḥwith incensesm. instr. pl. of धूप; instrument of धूपिता
तुtuand, moreoverindeclinable (connective particle, adding a further act)
धूपिताdhūpitācensed, perfumed with incensepast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of the stem धूपय- (√धूप् ‘to fumigate’, curādi or causative), f. nom. sg.; qualifies the Goddess
प्राहprāhaspoke, saidप्र + √ब्रू (adādi, 2U ‘to speak’) with the substitute stem आह्; laṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; used as a narrative past
प्रसादसुमुखीprasādasumukhīher face bright with favourf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the subject (जगतां धात्री, 4.29)
समस्तान्samastānallm. acc. pl. of समस्त; qualifies सुरान्
प्रणतान्praṇatānbowed downpast participle (kta) of प्र + √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. acc. pl.; qualifies सुरान्
सुरान्surānto the godsm. acc. pl. of सुर; the person addressed, object of प्राह

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • समस्तैस्त्रिदशैर्दिव्यैर्धूपैस्तु samastaistridaśairdivyairdhūpaistu → समस्तैः + त्रिदशैः + दिव्यैः + धूपैः + तु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    One run of four instrumentals shows both outcomes of a visarga after ai: before the unvoiced t it becomes s (समस्तैस्, धूपैस्); before the voiced d and dh the ru stays r (त्रिदशैर्, दिव्यैर्).
  • प्राह prāha → प्र + आह
    vowel sandhi of prefix and verb (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    The prefix प्र joins the verb आह: a + ā → ā.
  • समस्तान् प्रणतान् सुरान् samastān praṇatān surān → समस्तान् + प्रणतान् + सुरान्
    no change · —
    Final n before p and s needs no change; the Gita Press prints the n with a virāma and a space.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • त्रिदशैः tridaśaiḥ — bahuvrīhi (of number)
    Members: त्रि + दश · Vigraha: त्रिः आवृत्ताः दश परिमाणम् एषाम्, तैः triḥ āvṛttāḥ daśa parimāṇam eṣām, taiḥ
    Literally “those whose count is three tens”; in the verse: the gods, the ‘Thirty’, a round figure for the thirty-three gods of the Veda
    Alternative: A traditional etymology reads तृतीया दशा (यौवनम्) येषां ते, ‘those who are ever in the third stage of life (youth)’, i.e. the ageless gods; also a bahuvrīhi. The numerical reading is the usual one.
  • प्रसादसुमुखी prasādasumukhī — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, its second member a bahuvrīhi
    Members: प्रसाद + सुमुखी · Vigraha: प्रसादेन सुमुखी (सुमुखी = शोभनं मुखं यस्याः सा) prasādena sumukhī (sumukhī = śobhanaṃ mukhaṃ yasyāḥ sā)
    Literally “fair-faced through favour”; in the verse: her face showing her pleasure and grace
    Alternative: Also read as a pañcamī (cause) relation, प्रसादात् सुमुखी, ‘fair-faced because pleased’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • प्राह prāha — आह is the substitute that √ब्रू takes in the present (laṭ) parasmaipada, first five endings (आह, आहतुः, आहुः, आत्थ, आहथुः). It is used in narrative for ‘said’. Some grammarians instead treat आह as a perfect of a defective root अह्; either way it means ‘said’.
  • धूपिता dhūpitā — Denominative-causative stem धूपय- ‘to perfume with incense’ + kta with iṭ: धूपित, ‘censed’. The cognate noun धूपैः stands beside it (‘censed with incense’).
  • प्रणतान् praṇatān — प्र + √नम् + kta: the nasal is lost before -ta (नत), and n becomes ṇ after प्र: प्रणत, ‘bowed’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(एवं स्तुता अर्चिता जगतां धात्री) समस्तैः त्रिदशैः भक्त्या दिव्यैः धूपैः तु धूपिता प्रसादसुमुखी (सती) समस्तान् प्रणतान् सुरान् प्राह।

(evaṃ stutā arcitā jagatāṃ dhātrī) samastaiḥ tridaśaiḥ bhaktyā divyaiḥ dhūpaiḥ tu dhūpitā prasādasumukhī (satī) samastān praṇatān surān prāha |

The main verb प्राह completes the sentence begun in 4.29; its subject is जगतां धात्री, described by a third participle (धूपिता) and the adjective प्रसादसुमुखी. सुरान् is the person addressed.

6Translation

Literaland censed with devotion by all the Thirty with heavenly incenses, she, fair-faced with favour, spoke to all the gods, who were bowed down.

Readableand when all the gods had devoutly offered her heavenly incense, she, her face bright with grace, spoke to the whole company of gods as they bowed before her.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe sentence that began in 4.29 ends here. Praise, flowers, unguents and incense are followed by the Goddess’s favour, which shows on her face (प्रसादसुमुखी). The fierce face that terrified Mahiṣa (4.13) is now a gracious one.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess सुमुखी (459) and सद्यःप्रसादिनी (383, ‘quickly gracious’). The Devī Māhātmya joins the two ideas in one epithet, प्रसादसुमुखी.

    Final ī before the unlike vowel u becomes y: devy uvāca.

4.32Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

व्रियतां त्रिदशाः सर्वे यदस्मत्तोऽभिवाञ्छितम् ॥ ३२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

व्रियताम् | त्रिदशाः | सर्वे | यत् | अस्मत्तः | अभिवाञ्छितम्

3IAST

As recitedvriyatāṃ tridaśāḥ sarve yadasmatto'bhivāñchitam ||

Separatedvriyatām | tridaśāḥ | sarve | yat | asmattaḥ | abhivāñchitam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
व्रियताम्vriyatāmlet (it) be chosen — choose√वृ ‘to choose’ (वृङ्, kryādi, 9Ā; also svādi, 5U), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
त्रिदशाःtridaśāḥO gods (the Thirty)m. voc. pl. of त्रिदश; compound (see 4.30)
सर्वेsarveallm. voc. pl. of सर्व; qualifies त्रिदशाः
यत्yatwhateverrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; the grammatical subject of the passive व्रियताम्
अस्मत्तःasmattaḥfrom us — from meablative in -tas (tasil) of अस्मद्, plural stem; the plural of majesty, the Goddess speaking of herself
अभिवाञ्छितम्abhivāñchitamdesiredpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of अभि + √वाञ्छ् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to wish’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of यत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • व्रियतां त्रिदशाः vriyatāṃ tridaśāḥ → व्रियताम् + त्रिदशाः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • यदस्मत्तो yadasmatto → यत् + अस्मत्तः
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.
  • अस्मत्तोऽभिवाञ्छितम् asmatto'bhivāñchitam → अस्मत्तः + अभिवाञ्छितम्
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a: the ru becomes u, a + u gives o, and the following a is absorbed into the o (avagraha).

C. Morphology

  • व्रियताम् vriyatām — Passive imperative: √वृ + yak, with ṛ becoming ri before the passive -ya- (व्रिय-), and the ātmanepada imperative ending -ताम्. ‘Let (whatever is desired) be chosen’ is the courteous form of ‘choose!’.
  • अस्मत्तः asmattaḥ — The suffix -tas (tasil) gives ablative sense to pronouns: मत्तः ‘from me’, अस्मत्तः ‘from us’. The Goddess uses the plural of herself.
  • अभिवाञ्छितम् abhivāñchitam — अभि + √वाञ्छ् + kta with iṭ: ‘wished for’. Neuter, agreeing with यत्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) सर्वे त्रिदशाः, यत् अस्मत्तः अभिवाञ्छितम् (तत्) व्रियताम्।

(he) sarve tridaśāḥ, yat asmattaḥ abhivāñchitam (tat) vriyatām |

A passive imperative with a relative clause as its subject: ‘let (that) be chosen which is desired from us’; the correlative तत् is understood.

6Translation

LiteralLet it be chosen, O all you Thirty, whatever is desired from us.

ReadableAll you gods, choose whatever you desire of me.

The passive imperative is rendered as an active ‘choose’, and the plural of majesty (अस्मत्तः) as ‘me’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA half-verse: the Goddess, pleased by the hymn and the worship, offers the gods a boon. This is the classical response of a deity to praise, and it sets up the request that follows (4.34–37).

Traditional interpretationThe same pattern returns at the end of the Uttama Carita: after the hymn to Nārāyaṇī the Goddess says वरदाहं सुरगणा वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ (11.37), and the gods again ask for the destruction of their enemies and the removal of every affliction.

4.33Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

देवा ऊचुः ॥ ३३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवाः | ऊचुः

3IAST

As reciteddevā ūcuḥ ||

Separateddevāḥ | ūcuḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
ऊचुःūcuḥsaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • देवा ऊचुः devā ūcuḥ → देवाः + ऊचुः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a vowel: the ru becomes y, the y is dropped, and the two vowels stand apart (देवा | ऊचुः) without further joining.

C. Morphology

  • ऊचुः ūcuḥ — Perfect plural of √वच्: in the weak forms the root takes samprasāraṇa (वच् → उच्), and the reduplicating उ + उच् gives ऊच्: ऊचुः ‘they said’. Compare the singular उवाच (4.28).

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवाः ऊचुः।

devāḥ ūcuḥ |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe gods said:

ReadableThe gods said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods answer the Goddess’s offer of a boon (4.32). Their reply runs to 4.37, the last speech of the Madhyama Carita before Medhas closes the story.

4.34Half-ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भगवत्या कृतं सर्वं न किंचिदवशिष्यते ॥ ३४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भगवत्या | कृतम् | सर्वम् | न | किंचित् | अवशिष्यते

3IAST

As recitedbhagavatyā kṛtaṃ sarvaṃ na kiṃcidavaśiṣyate ||

Separatedbhagavatyā | kṛtam | sarvam | na | kiṃcit | avaśiṣyate

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भगवत्याbhagavatyāby the Blessed One — by youf. instr. sg. of भगवती; agent of the passive कृतम्; the respectful third person used for ‘you’
कृतम्kṛtamdonepast passive participle (kta) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of सर्वम्
सर्वम्sarvameverythingn. nom. sg. of सर्व; subject
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
किंचित्kiṃcitanything at allindefinite pronoun: किम् (n. nom. sg.) + the particle चित्; with न, ‘nothing’
अवशिष्यतेavaśiṣyateremains, is left overअव + √शिष् (rudhādi, 7P ‘to leave’), karmaṇi form, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कृतं सर्वं न kṛtaṃ sarvaṃ na → कृतम् + सर्वम् + न
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, twice.
  • किंचिदवशिष्यते kiṃcidavaśiṣyate → किंचित् + अवशिष्यते
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

C. Morphology

  • अवशिष्यते avaśiṣyate — Passive of अव + √शिष् ‘to leave’ (stem शिष्य- with yak): ‘is left’, used in the intransitive sense ‘remains’.
  • भगवत्या bhagavatyā — Instrumental of भगवती, the feminine of भगवत् (भग + matup, ‘possessing fortune’). Speaking of the person addressed in the third person is a mark of reverence.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भगवत्या सर्वं कृतम्; किंचित् न अवशिष्यते।

bhagavatyā sarvaṃ kṛtam; kiṃcit na avaśiṣyate |

Two short clauses: a passive statement with the agent in the instrumental (भगवत्या … कृतम्), then a negated present.

6Translation

LiteralEverything has been done by the Blessed One; nothing at all remains.

ReadableYou, Blessed One, have done everything; nothing is left to be done.

The respectful third person भगवत्या is turned into the second person ‘you’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA half-verse. The gods’ first answer is that they need nothing more: what they asked for has been accomplished. The reason is given in the next line (4.35): Mahiṣa is dead.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the gods begin by declaring themselves content before they ask anything, and when they do ask, it is for protection and for others (4.36–37).

4.35Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

यदयं निहतः शत्रुरस्माकं महिषासुरः।
यदि चापि वरो देयस्त्वयास्माकं महेश्वरि ॥ ३५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यत् | अयम् | निहतः | शत्रुः | अस्माकम् | महिषासुरःयदि | च | अपि | वरः | देयः | त्वया | अस्माकम् | महेश्वरि

3IAST

As recitedyadayaṃ nihataḥ śatrurasmākaṃ mahiṣāsuraḥ |
yadi cāpi varo deyastvayāsmākaṃ maheśvari ||

Separatedyat | ayam | nihataḥ | śatruḥ | asmākam | mahiṣāsuraḥ
yadi | ca | api | varaḥ | deyaḥ | tvayā | asmākam | maheśvari

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
यत्yatin that, sinceindeclinable use of the relative यद् (conjunction ‘that, because’); gives the reason for 4.34
अयम्ayamthisdemonstrative pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies महिषासुरः
निहतःnihataḥslainpast passive participle (kta) of नि + √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. nom. sg.; predicate
शत्रुःśatruḥenemym. nom. sg. of शत्रु (u-stem); in apposition to महिषासुरः
अस्माकम्asmākamourfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. pl.; depends on शत्रुः
महिषासुरःmahiṣāsuraḥMahiṣāsura, the buffalo-demonm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
यदिyadiifindeclinable (conditional conjunction)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अपिapistill, even soindeclinable (particle); with यदि च, ‘and if even so’
वरःvaraḥa boonm. nom. sg. of वर; subject of the gerundive देयः
देयःdeyaḥis to be givengerundive (kṛtya affix yat) of √दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), m. nom. sg.; agrees with वरः
त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of देयः
अस्माकम्asmākamto usअस्मद्, gen. pl., in the sense of the recipient (genitive for dative)
महेश्वरिmaheśvariO Great Sovereignf. voc. sg. of महेश्वरी; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यदयं yadayaṃ → यत् + अयम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel; the m of अयम् becomes anusvāra before n (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • निहतः शत्रुरस्माकं nihataḥ śatrurasmākaṃ → निहतः + शत्रुः + अस्माकम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Before the sibilant ś the visarga of निहतः may stand unchanged (an optional rule; the alternative would be निहतश्शत्रुः). After u and before a vowel the ru of शत्रुः stays r: शत्रुरस्माकम्.
  • चापि वरो देयस्त्वयास्माकं cāpi varo deyastvayāsmākaṃ → च + अपि + वरः + देयः + त्वया + अस्माकम्
    vowel and visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    a + a → ā (चापि) and ā + a → ā (त्वयास्माकम्); -aḥ before the voiced d → -o (वरो); visarga before t → s (देयस्त्वया).
  • महेश्वरि maheśvari → महा + ईश्वरि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + ī → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महिषासुरः mahiṣāsuraḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महिष + असुर · Vigraha: महिषः च असौ असुरः च mahiṣaḥ ca asau asuraḥ ca
    Literally “the asura who is Mahiṣa (the buffalo)”; in the verse: Mahiṣa, the buffalo-demon
    Alternative: Also analysed as a madhyamapadalopī karmadhāraya, महिषरूपः असुरः, ‘the asura in buffalo form’; the sense is the same.
  • महेश्वरि maheśvari — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: महती च असौ ईश्वरी च mahatī ca asau īśvarī ca
    Literally “great sovereign lady”; in the verse: Supreme Ruler — the feminine of Śiva’s title Maheśvara
    Alternative: महत् takes the form महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya, then महा + ईश्वरी → महेश्वरी.

C. Morphology

  • निहतः nihataḥ — नि + √हन् + kta: the root’s nasal is lost before -ta (हत): ‘struck down, slain’.
  • देयः deyaḥ — Gerundive of √दा with the kṛtya affix yat; the ā becomes e before it: देय, ‘to be given’. With the agent in the instrumental (त्वया) it forms an impersonal ‘must/should be given by you’.
  • अस्माकम् asmākam — The genitive plural appears twice: first possessive (‘our enemy’), then for the recipient (‘to us’), where classical prose would often use the dative.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यत् अयम् अस्माकं शत्रुः महिषासुरः निहतः, (अतः सर्वं कृतम्)। (हे) महेश्वरि, यदि च अपि त्वया अस्माकं वरः देयः, (4.36 …)

yat ayam asmākaṃ śatruḥ mahiṣāsuraḥ nihataḥ, (ataḥ sarvaṃ kṛtam) | (he) maheśvari, yadi ca api tvayā asmākaṃ varaḥ deyaḥ, (4.36 …)

The first line is a causal clause (यत् … निहतः) explaining 4.34. The second line is a conditional (यदि …) whose main clause comes in 4.36: ‘if a boon is still to be given, (then) …’.

6Translation

Literalin that this Mahiṣāsura, our enemy, has been slain. And if even so a boon is to be given to us by you, O Great Sovereign, —

Readablefor Mahiṣāsura, our enemy, lies slain. Yet if you would still grant us a boon, O Supreme Sovereign, —

The gerundive ‘is to be given by you’ is rendered ‘if you would grant’; the sentence continues in 4.36.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first line gives the reason for 4.34: the enemy who drove the gods from heaven (2.1–8) is dead. The second line turns, politely and conditionally, to the request; the boon itself is named in 4.36–37.

Traditional interpretationमहेश्वरी, the feminine of Maheśvara, Śiva’s title, names the Goddess as sovereign in her own right. The gods’ courtesy — ‘if a boon is still to be given’ — is the tone in which the text has them ask.

4.36Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

संस्मृता संस्मृता त्वं नो हिंसेथाः परमापदः।
यश्च मर्त्यः स्तवैरेभिस्त्वां स्तोष्यत्यमलानने ॥ ३६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

संस्मृता | संस्मृता | त्वम् | नः | हिंसेथाः | परमापदःयः | च | मर्त्यः | स्तवैः | एभिः | त्वाम् | स्तोष्यति | अमलानने

3IAST

As recitedsaṃsmṛtā saṃsmṛtā tvaṃ no hiṃsethāḥ paramāpadaḥ |
yaśca martyaḥ stavairebhistvāṃ stoṣyatyamalānane ||

Separatedsaṃsmṛtā | saṃsmṛtā | tvam | naḥ | hiṃsethāḥ | paramāpadaḥ
yaḥ | ca | martyaḥ | stavaiḥ | ebhiḥ | tvām | stoṣyati | amalānane

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
संस्मृताsaṃsmṛtārememberedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √स्मृ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to remember’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies त्वम्
संस्मृताsaṃsmṛtā(each time) rememberedthe same form repeated (āmreḍita): ‘whenever remembered’
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of हिंसेथाः
नःnaḥourfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, enclitic gen. pl. (= अस्माकम्); depends on परमापदः
हिंसेथाःhiṃsethāḥmay you destroy√हिंस् ‘to injure, destroy’ (classically rudhādi, 7P हिनस्ति; here conjugated on a thematic stem हिंस-), vidhiliṅ (optative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada — an epic (ārṣa) form
परमापदःparamāpadaḥthe gravest calamitiesf. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of हिंसेथाः
यःyaḥwhoeverrelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.; its correlative is तस्य in 4.37
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
मर्त्यःmartyaḥmortal, human beingm. nom. sg. of मर्त्य; qualifies यः
स्तवैःstavaiḥwith hymnsm. instr. pl. of स्तव (√स्तु + ap); instrument of स्तोष्यति
एभिःebhiḥthesedemonstrative pronoun इदम्, m. instr. pl.; qualifies स्तवैः
त्वाम्tvāmyouयुष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of स्तोष्यति
स्तोष्यतिstoṣyatiwill praise√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), lṛṭ (simple future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अमलाननेamalānaneO you of stainless facef. voc. sg. of अमलानना; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नो हिंसेथाः no hiṃsethāḥ → नः + हिंसेथाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced h becomes -o.
  • यश्च yaśca → यः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and the s becomes the palatal ś next to the palatal c.
  • स्तवैरेभिस्त्वां stavairebhistvāṃ → स्तवैः + एभिः + त्वाम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    After ai and before a vowel the ru stays r (स्तवैरेभिः); after i and before t the visarga becomes s (एभिस्त्वाम्).
  • स्तोष्यत्यमलानने stoṣyatyamalānane → स्तोष्यति + अमलानने
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before the unlike vowel a becomes y.
  • परमापदः paramāpadaḥ → परम + आपदः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā; likewise अमल + आनने → अमलानने.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • परमापदः paramāpadaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: परम + आपद् · Vigraha: परमाः च ताः आपदः च, ताः paramāḥ ca tāḥ āpadaḥ ca, tāḥ
    Literally “supreme calamities”; in the verse: the worst of misfortunes
  • अमलानने amalānane — bahuvrīhi (its first member अमल is itself a nañ-bahuvrīhi)
    Members: अमल + आनन · Vigraha: अमलम् आननं यस्याः सा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ (अमल = न विद्यते मलं यस्य तत्) amalam ānanaṃ yasyāḥ sā, tatsambuddhau (amala = na vidyate malaṃ yasya tat)
    Literally “she whose face is without stain”; in the verse: O Goddess of the spotless, radiant face

C. Morphology

  • संस्मृता saṃsmṛtā — Repetition of a word (āmreḍita) expresses ‘again and again, every time’: संस्मृता संस्मृता, ‘remembered each time’. The participle has a conditional force: ‘(when) remembered’.
  • हिंसेथाः hiṃsethāḥ — Optative in -ethāḥ (stem in -a + -īthāḥ), the ātmanepada ending, from a root classically conjugated in the seventh class and parasmaipada (optative हिंस्याः). Such forms are common in epic and Purāṇic Sanskrit (ārṣa). Compare भवेथाः in 4.37.
  • स्तोष्यति stoṣyati — Simple future of √स्तु: guṇa of u (स्तो-) + -sya-, with s becoming ṣ after o: स्तोष्यति. √स्तु is aniṭ, so no connecting -i-.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) अमलानने, संस्मृता संस्मृता त्वं नः परमापदः हिंसेथाः। यः च मर्त्यः एभिः स्तवैः त्वां स्तोष्यति, (4.37 तस्य …)

(he) amalānane, saṃsmṛtā saṃsmṛtā tvaṃ naḥ paramāpadaḥ hiṃsethāḥ | yaḥ ca martyaḥ ebhiḥ stavaiḥ tvāṃ stoṣyati, (4.37 tasya …)

The first line is the main clause of the conditional begun in 4.35 (‘if a boon is to be given, may you destroy …’). The second line opens a relative clause (यः …) whose correlative तस्य comes in 4.37.

6Translation

LiteralRemembered, remembered, may you destroy our gravest calamities; and whatever mortal will praise you with these hymns, O you of stainless face, —

Readablewhenever we call you to mind, destroy our worst calamities. And whoever among mortals praises you with these hymns, O Goddess of the stainless face, —

The repeated participle is rendered ‘whenever we call you to mind’; the sentence of the second line continues in 4.37.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods ask two boons. The first is for themselves: that the Goddess, whenever remembered, should destroy their gravest calamities. The second, begun here and completed in 4.37, is for any human being who praises her with ‘these hymns’, the hymn of 4.2–27.

Traditional interpretationWith these two verses the hymn acquires its fruit (phala) for later reciters. The Goddess herself confirms the promise at length in chapter 12, the Phalastuti, which opens in almost the same words: एभिः स्तवैश्च मां नित्यं स्तोष्यते यः समाहितः (12.2).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the gods, once rescued, ask for help not only for themselves but for every person who will ever say their words. Gratitude turns into intercession.

4.37Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तस्य वित्तर्द्धिविभवैर्धनदारादिसम्पदाम्।
वृद्धयेऽस्मत्प्रसन्ना त्वं भवेथाः सर्वदाम्बिके ॥ ३७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तस्य | वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः | धनदारादिसम्पदाम्वृद्धये | अस्मत्प्रसन्ना | त्वम् | भवेथाः | सर्वदा | अम्बिके

3IAST

As recitedtasya vittarddhivibhavairdhanadārādisampadām |
vṛddhaye'smatprasannā tvaṃ bhavethāḥ sarvadāmbike ||

Separatedtasya | vittarddhivibhavaiḥ | dhanadārādisampadām
vṛddhaye | asmatprasannā | tvam | bhavethāḥ | sarvadā | ambike

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तस्यtasyaof him (that mortal)pronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.; correlative of यः in 4.36; depends on धनदारादिसम्पदाम्
वित्तर्द्धिविभवैःvittarddhivibhavaiḥwith wealth, prosperity and splendourm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of respect with वृद्धये (‘increase in …’)
धनदारादिसम्पदाम्dhanadārādisampadāmof his possessions — riches, wife and the restf. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on वृद्धये
वृद्धयेvṛddhayefor the increasef. dat. sg. of वृद्धि (√वृध् + ktin); dative of purpose
अस्मत्प्रसन्नाasmatprasannāgracious to usf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम्
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of भवेथाः
भवेथाःbhavethāḥmay you be√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be’), vidhiliṅ (optative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada — an epic (ārṣa) form for classical भवेः
सर्वदाsarvadāalwaysindeclinable (adverb of time: सर्व + -dā)
अम्बिकेambikeO Mother, O Ambikāf. voc. sg. of अम्बिका

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वित्तर्द्धि vittarddhi → वित्त + ऋद्धि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ṛ → ar: vitta + ṛddhi → vittarddhi.
  • विभवैर्धन vibhavairdhana → विभवैः + धन…
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai and before the voiced dh the ru stays r.
  • वृद्धयेऽस्मत्प्रसन्ना vṛddhaye'smatprasannā → वृद्धये + अस्मत्प्रसन्ना
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Final e before initial a: the a is absorbed (avagraha). Inside the compound the d of अस्मद् is unvoiced before p (खरि च, 8.4.55).
  • सर्वदाम्बिके sarvadāmbike → सर्वदा + अम्बिके
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā; the same inside the compound धनदार + आदि → धनदारादि.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः vittarddhivibhavaiḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: वित्त + ऋद्धि + विभव · Vigraha: वित्तं च ऋद्धिः च विभवः च, तैः vittaṃ ca ṛddhiḥ ca vibhavaḥ ca, taiḥ
    Literally “with wealth, prosperity and splendour”; in the verse: every kind of worldly fortune
  • धनदारादिसम्पदाम् dhanadārādisampadām — karmadhāraya containing a bahuvrīhi in आदि, which contains a dvandva
    Members: धन + दार + आदि + सम्पद् · Vigraha: धनं च दाराः च धनदाराः (itaretara dvandva); धनदाराः आदयः यासां ताः धनदारादयः (bahuvrīhi); धनदारादयः च ताः सम्पदः च, तासाम् (karmadhāraya) dhanaṃ ca dārāḥ ca dhanadārāḥ (itaretara dvandva); dhanadārāḥ ādayaḥ yāsāṃ tāḥ dhanadārādayaḥ (bahuvrīhi); dhanadārādayaḥ ca tāḥ sampadaḥ ca, tāsām (karmadhāraya)
    Literally “of the possessions that begin with wealth and wife”; in the verse: of his riches, his wife and family, and all he has
    Alternative: Also possible as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, धनदारादीनां सम्पदः, ‘the prosperity of his wealth, wife and the rest’; the sense differs little.
  • अस्मत्प्रसन्ना asmatprasannā — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अस्मद् + प्रसन्ना · Vigraha: अस्मासु प्रसन्ना asmāsu prasannā
    Literally “gracious towards us”; in the verse: favourable to the gods who ask
    Alternative: अस्मत् could also be read as a separate ablative plural, ‘pleased (by our praise) from us’; the printed text joins it, and ‘gracious to us’ is the natural sense.

C. Morphology

  • भवेथाः bhavethāḥ — Optative of √भू with the ātmanepada ending -ethāḥ; classical Sanskrit uses only the parasmaipada (भवेः). Like हिंसेथाः in 4.36, an epic (ārṣa) form, perhaps chosen for the metre.
  • वृद्धये vṛddhaye — Dative of वृद्धि expressing purpose (tādarthya): ‘for the growth of …’.
  • सर्वदा sarvadā — The suffix -dā forms adverbs of time from pronouns: सर्वदा ‘at all times’, यदा, तदा, कदा.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) अम्बिके, अस्मत्प्रसन्ना त्वं सर्वदा तस्य वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः धनदारादिसम्पदां वृद्धये भवेथाः।

(he) ambike, asmatprasannā tvaṃ sarvadā tasya vittarddhivibhavaiḥ dhanadārādisampadāṃ vṛddhaye bhavethāḥ |

The main clause of the relative sentence begun in 4.36 (यः … स्तोष्यति — तस्य …). The genitive तस्य goes with धनदारादिसम्पदाम्; the instrumental वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः is loosely attached, best taken as ‘in respect of wealth …’ with वृद्धये.

6Translation

Literalfor the increase of his possessions — riches, wife and the rest — in wealth, prosperity and splendour, may you, gracious to us, always be (present), O Ambikā.

Readablemay you, O Mother, ever gracious to us, be with that person always, to increase his wealth, prosperity and fortune, his riches, his wife and family, and all he has.

The loose instrumental and the genitive are rendered together as a list of what increases; ‘be with that person’ makes explicit the sense of भवेथाः … वृद्धये, ‘be (there) for the increase’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second boon, completed: whoever recites ‘these hymns’ should be granted increase in every worldly good — wealth, prosperity, splendour, riches, wife and household. The gods ask the Goddess to be always gracious to them, and so to him.

Traditional interpretationThe two boons together — protection from calamity when remembered (4.36) and prosperity for the reciter (4.37) — are the fruits the tradition attaches to the Śakrādi stuti. They are the first statement in the text of the reward of recitation, set out in full by the Goddess in chapter 12.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the text does not separate devotion from ordinary life. Wealth, home and family are asked for openly, as things the Goddess is glad to increase.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The construction of the instrumental वित्तर्द्धिविभवैः is loose: it may go with वृद्धये (‘increase in wealth …’) or be read as a list parallel to धनदारादिसम्पदाम्. The meaning is the same either way; the reading of the text is not in doubt.

2Padaccheda — the words separated

Direct textual meaningMedhas takes up the narration again after the dialogue of the Goddess and the gods (4.31–37), to tell Suratha how the episode ended and to announce the story still to come.

4.39Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

इति प्रसादिता देवैर्जगतोऽर्थे तथाऽऽत्मनः।
तथेत्युक्त्वा भद्रकाली बभूवान्तर्हिता नृप ॥ ३९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

इति | प्रसादिता | देवैः | जगतः | अर्थे | तथा | आत्मनःतथा | इति | उक्त्वा | भद्रकाली | बभूव | अन्तर्हिता | नृप

3IAST

As recitediti prasāditā devairjagato'rthe tathā''tmanaḥ |
tathetyuktvā bhadrakālī babhūvāntarhitā nṛpa ||

Separatediti | prasāditā | devaiḥ | jagataḥ | arthe | tathā | ātmanaḥ
tathā | iti | uktvā | bhadrakālī | babhūva | antarhitā | nṛpa

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
इतिitithusindeclinable (closing the gods’ request)
प्रसादिताprasāditāpropitiated, entreatedpast passive participle (kta, with iṭ) of the causative प्रसादय- (प्र + √सद् ‘to be gracious’; causative ‘to make gracious’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies भद्रकाली
देवैःdevaiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of देव; agent of प्रसादिता
जगतःjagataḥof the worldn. gen. sg. of जगत्; depends on अर्थे
अर्थेarthefor the sake (of)m. loc. sg. of अर्थ; with a genitive, ‘for the sake of’
तथाtathāand likewiseindeclinable (connective adverb)
आत्मनःātmanaḥof themselvesm. gen. sg. of आत्मन्, the reflexive (singular in form, referring to the gods); depends on अर्थे understood
तथाtathāso (be it)indeclinable; here a word of assent, quoted
इतिiti(marks the quotation)indeclinable (quotative particle)
उक्त्वाuktvāhaving saidabsolutive (ktvā) of √वच् (adādi, 2P), with samprasāraṇa
भद्रकालीbhadrakālīBhadrakālī, the auspicious Kālīf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject

अन्तर्हिताantarhitāvanished, hidden from sightpast participle (kta) of अन्तर् + √धा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to place’), f. nom. sg.; predicate with बभूव
नृपnṛpaO kingm. voc. sg. of नृप (Suratha); compound in origin (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • देवैर्जगतोऽर्थे devairjagato'rthe → देवैः + जगतः + अर्थे
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    After ai and before voiced j the ru stays r (देवैर्). -aḥ before a becomes -o and the a is absorbed (जगतोऽर्थे).
  • तथाऽऽत्मनः tathā''tmanaḥ → तथा + आत्मनः
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press writes a double avagraha (ऽऽ) to show that the long ā of आत्मनः has merged into the ā of तथा.
  • तथेत्युक्त्वा tathetyuktvā → तथा + इति + उक्त्वा
    vowel sandhi (guṇa, then yaṇ) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + i → e (तथेति); then i before u becomes y (इत्युक्त्वा).
  • बभूवान्तर्हिता babhūvāntarhitā → बभूव + अन्तर्हिता
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भद्रकाली bhadrakālī — karmadhāraya
    Members: भद्रा + काली · Vigraha: भद्रा च असौ काली च bhadrā ca asau kālī ca
    Literally “the auspicious Dark One”; in the verse: the Goddess as Bhadrakālī, fierce in form and auspicious in effect
    Alternative: In a karmadhāraya a feminine adjective takes its masculine form as first member (भद्रा → भद्र-), hence भद्रकाली.
  • नृप nṛpa — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: नृ + प · Vigraha: नॄन् पाति इति nṝn pāti iti
    Literally “protector of men”; in the verse: king

C. Morphology

  • प्रसादिता prasāditā — Causative stem प्रसादय- (प्र + √सद् ‘to be clear, gracious’; causative ‘to make gracious, to propitiate’) + kta with iṭ, the causative -ay- dropped: प्रसादित.
  • उक्त्वा uktvā — Absolutive of √वच् with samprasāraṇa (वच् → उच्) and c → k before t: उक्त्वा, ‘having said’.
  • अन्तर्हिता antarhitā — अन्तर् is treated as a prefix of √धा; in the past participle धा becomes हि (हित): अन्तर्हित, ‘placed within, concealed’. With बभूव it forms a periphrasis ‘became hidden’, i.e. vanished.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) नृप, इति देवैः जगतः अर्थे तथा आत्मनः (अर्थे) प्रसादिता भद्रकाली ‘तथा’ इति उक्त्वा अन्तर्हिता बभूव।

(he) nṛpa, iti devaiḥ jagataḥ arthe tathā ātmanaḥ (arthe) prasāditā bhadrakālī ‘tathā’ iti uktvā antarhitā babhūva |

The subject भद्रकाली is described by the participle प्रसादिता; the absolutive उक्त्वा precedes the main verb बभूव with its predicate अन्तर्हिता.

6Translation

LiteralThus propitiated by the gods for the sake of the world and likewise of themselves, Bhadrakālī said ‘So be it’ and became vanished, O king.

ReadableSo the gods entreated her, for the world’s sake and for their own; and Bhadrakālī said, ‘So be it,’ and vanished from their sight, O king.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess grants the boons with one word, तथा, ‘so be it’, and disappears. Medhas reminds his listener (नृप, King Suratha) that he is telling a story. The gods asked both for the world (जगतः) and for themselves (आत्मनः), and both requests were granted.

Traditional interpretationHere the narrative names her Bhadrakālī, the auspicious Kālī. The liturgical tradition of the Gita Press pāṭha assigns the Madhyama Carita to Mahālakṣmī; the Purāṇa itself moves freely between names for the one Goddess. Her vanishing completes this manifestation; she appears again when needed, as she promises in 11.54–55.

4.40Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

इत्येतत्कथितं भूप सम्भूता सा यथा पुरा।
देवीदेवशरीरेभ्यो जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी ॥ ४० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

इति | एतत् | कथितम् | भूप | सम्भूता | सा | यथा | पुरादेवी | देवशरीरेभ्यः | जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी

devīdevaśarīrebhyo jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī ||

devī | devaśarīrebhyaḥ | jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • इत्येतत्कथितं ityetatkathitaṃ → इति + एतत् + कथितम्
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ); consonant unchanged · इको यणचि (6.1.77); खरि च (8.4.55)
    i before e becomes y; the final t of एतत् stays unvoiced before k; the m of कथितम् becomes anusvāra before bh (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • देवशरीरेभ्यो जगत्त्रय devaśarīrebhyo jagattraya → देवशरीरेभ्यः + जगत्त्रय…
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced j becomes -o. देवी and देवशरीरेभ्यः are printed together, but they are two words: देवी needs no sandhi before d.
  • हितैषिणी hitaiṣiṇī → हित + एषिणी
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भूप bhūpa — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: भू + प · Vigraha: भुवं पाति इति bhuvaṃ pāti iti
    Literally “protector of the earth”; in the verse: king
  • देवशरीरेभ्यः devaśarīrebhyaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: देव + शरीर · Vigraha: देवानां शरीराणि, तेभ्यः devānāṃ śarīrāṇi, tebhyaḥ
    Literally “from the bodies of the gods”; in the verse: from the splendour that came out of the gods’ bodies (2.9–13)
  • जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī — upapada tatpuruṣa (with the affix ṇini), containing ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas
    Members: जगत्त्रय + हित + एषिणी · Vigraha: जगतां त्रयं जगत्त्रयम्; जगत्त्रयस्य हितम् जगत्त्रयहितम्; जगत्त्रयहितम् एषितुं शीलम् अस्याः सा jagatāṃ trayaṃ jagattrayam; jagattrayasya hitam jagattrayahitam; jagattrayahitam eṣituṃ śīlam asyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose nature is to seek the good of the triad of worlds”; in the verse: who wishes well to all three worlds

C. Morphology

  • कथितम् kathitam — Passive participle of the curādi root √कथ्: the stem’s -ay- is dropped and iṭ inserted, कथित. With इति एतत् it makes a closing formula: ‘thus this has been told’.
  • सम्भूता sambhūtā — Past participle of an intransitive verb, so active in meaning: ‘(she) arose’, not ‘was produced’.
  • जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī — The last member -एषिन् is √इष् ‘to seek, wish’ + ṇini (a habitual agent: ‘one who regularly seeks’), feminine in -ī: हितैषिणी, ‘well-wisher’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) भूप, जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी सा देवी पुरा देवशरीरेभ्यः यथा सम्भूता, (तत्) एतत् इति कथितम्।

(he) bhūpa, jagattrayahitaiṣiṇī sā devī purā devaśarīrebhyaḥ yathā sambhūtā, (tat) etat iti kathitam |

A main clause (इति एतत् कथितम्) followed by an indirect question of manner (यथा … सम्भूता) that says what ‘this’ was.

6Translation

LiteralThus this has been told, O king: how she, the Devī, wishing the welfare of the three worlds, formerly arose from the bodies of the gods.

ReadableSo, O king, I have told you how long ago the Goddess, who seeks the good of the three worlds, came forth from the bodies of the gods.

The impersonal passive ‘this has been told’ is rendered ‘I have told you’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas sums up the Madhyama Carita (chapters 2–4) by its first event: the Goddess arose from the gathered splendour of the gods’ bodies (2.9–13) to destroy Mahiṣa. The epithet जगत्त्रयहितैषिणी names her purpose.

Traditional interpretationIn the first carita the Goddess appears from Viṣṇu’s body as Yoganidrā; in the second, from the bodies of all the gods; in the third (announced in the next verse), from Gaurī’s body. The tradition reads these three emergences as one Goddess taking form in different ways.

4.41Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पुनश्च गौरीदेहात्सा समुद्भूता यथाभवत्।
वधाय दुष्टदैत्यानां तथा शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः ॥ ४१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

पुनः | च | गौरीदेहात् | सा | समुद्भूता | यथा | अभवत्वधाय | दुष्टदैत्यानाम् | तथा | शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः

3IAST

As recitedpunaśca gaurīdehātsā samudbhūtā yathābhavat |
vadhāya duṣṭadaityānāṃ tathā śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

Separatedpunaḥ | ca | gaurīdehāt | sā | samudbhūtā | yathā | abhavat
vadhāya | duṣṭadaityānām | tathā | śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
गौरीदेहात्gaurīdehātfrom the body of Gaurī (Pārvatī)m. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); source of समुद्भूता
साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject
समुद्भूताsamudbhūtāhaving arisenpast participle (kta) of सम् + उत् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), active in sense, f. nom. sg.

शुम्भनिशुम्भयोःśumbhaniśumbhayoḥof Śumbha and Niśumbham. gen. du.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on वधाय

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पुनश्च punaśca → पुनः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and the s becomes ś next to c.
  • गौरीदेहात्सा gaurīdehātsā → गौरीदेहात् + सा
    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)
    Final t stays unvoiced before the unvoiced s; the words are only written together.
  • यथाभवत् yathābhavat → यथा + अभवत्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • दुष्टदैत्यानां तथा duṣṭadaityānāṃ tathā → दुष्टदैत्यानाम् + तथा
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • गौरीदेहात् gaurīdehāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: गौरी + देह · Vigraha: गौर्याः देहः, तस्मात् gauryāḥ dehaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the body of Gaurī”; in the verse: from Pārvatī’s body, as told in 5.85–87
  • दुष्टदैत्यानाम् duṣṭadaityānām — karmadhāraya
    Members: दुष्ट + दैत्य · Vigraha: दुष्टाः च ते दैत्याः च, तेषाम् duṣṭāḥ ca te daityāḥ ca, teṣām
    Literally “of the wicked Daityas”; in the verse: the demon host of the third carita
  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: शुम्भ + निशुम्भ · Vigraha: शुम्भः च निशुम्भः च, तयोः śumbhaḥ ca niśumbhaḥ ca, tayoḥ
    Literally “of Śumbha and Niśumbha”; in the verse: the two asura brothers whose defeat is the subject of chapters 5–10

C. Morphology

  • अभवत् abhavat — Imperfect of √भू with augment a- and guṇa (भव-): ‘came to be’. With the participle समुद्भूता: ‘how, having arisen, she came to be’.
  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ — A dvandva of two names takes the dual: genitive dual in -योः.
  • समुद्भूता samudbhūtā — Two prefixes, सम् + उत्, on √भू: ‘to arise fully, spring up’; the past participle of this intransitive verb is active in sense.

D. Anvaya — prose order

पुनः च सा गौरीदेहात् समुद्भूता दुष्टदैत्यानां तथा शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः वधाय यथा अभवत्, (4.42 …)

punaḥ ca sā gaurīdehāt samudbhūtā duṣṭadaityānāṃ tathā śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ vadhāya yathā abhavat, (4.42 …)

A second indirect question of manner (यथा … अभवत्), continued in 4.42 and taken up there by तत्.

6Translation

LiteralAnd again how she, having arisen from Gaurī’s body, came to be, for the slaying of the wicked Daityas and likewise of Śumbha and Niśumbha,

Readableand how once more she arose, this time from the body of Gaurī, to slay the wicked Daityas, and Śumbha and Niśumbha with them,

‘This time’ makes explicit the contrast with 4.40 that पुनः implies; the sentence continues in 4.42.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas announces the third story, the Uttama Carita (chapters 5–13): the Goddess will arise again, from the body of Gaurī, to destroy Śumbha, Niśumbha and their Daityas. In chapter 5 Ambikā comes forth from Pārvatī’s bodily sheath and is called Kauśikī (5.85–87).

Traditional interpretationThe liturgical tradition of the Gita Press pāṭha assigns the Uttama Carita to Mahāsarasvatī, as it assigns the first to Mahākālī and the second to Mahālakṣmī.

4.42Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

रक्षणाय च लोकानां देवानामुपकारिणी।
तच्छृणुष्व मयाऽऽख्यातं यथावत्कथयामि ते ॥ ४२ ॥ ह्रीं ॐ

2Padaccheda — the words separated

रक्षणाय | च | लोकानाम् | देवानाम् | उपकारिणीतत् | शृणुष्व | मया | आख्यातम् | यथावत् | कथयामि | ते

3IAST

As recitedrakṣaṇāya ca lokānāṃ devānāmupakāriṇī |
tacchṛṇuṣva mayā''khyātaṃ yathāvatkathayāmi te ||

Separatedrakṣaṇāya | ca | lokānām | devānām | upakāriṇī
tat | śṛṇuṣva | mayā | ākhyātam | yathāvat | kathayāmi | te

रक्षणायrakṣaṇāyafor the protectionn. dat. sg. of रक्षण (√रक्ष् + lyuṭ); dative of purpose, parallel to वधाय in 4.41

देवानाम्devānāmof the godsm. gen. pl. of देव; depends on उपकारिणी
उपकारिणीupakāriṇīhelper, benefactressf. nom. sg. of उपकारिन् (उप + √कृ + ṇini, feminine in -ī); qualifies सा (4.41)

शृणुष्वśṛṇuṣvahear√श्रु (svādi, 5P ‘to hear’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada ending — an epic (ārṣa) form for classical शृणु
